reset `RESET` p.332-333 (locked) | `CSB` p.249 | `STB?` p.358 (barrier only) | `ERRSTR?` p.264 | `ERR?` p.262 | `AUXERR?` p.241 | `ID?` p.276 | `REV?` p.334 | `TEMP?` p.370 | `LINE?` p.285 |
`CALNUM?` p.246 | `OPT?` p.314 | `TERM?` p.371 | `INBUF?` p.277 | `FUNC` p.271-275 | `RANGE` p.324-327 | `ARANGE` p.240 | `NPLC` p.301-303 | `APER` p.239 | `NDIG` p.300 | `AZERO` p.243 |
`FIXEDZ` p.267 | `LFREQ` p.284 | `DELAY` p.253 | `SETACV` p.342 | `ACBAND` p.236 | `LEVEL` p.281 | `SLOPE` p.343 | `TRIG` p.374 | `TARM` p.366 | `NRDGS` p.304 | `END` p.261 |
`OFORMAT` p.308 | `QFORMAT` p.322 | `MATH` p.287 | `SMATH` p.344 | `RMATH` p.334 | `ACAL` p.234-235 (locked).  Not implemented on purpose: `CAL`, `SCAL`, `SECURE`, `CALSTR` (calibration writes
are out of scope), `PRESET`, `TEST`, `ADDRESS`, `LOCK`, `DISP`, `SRQ`/`RQS`/`EMASK` (needs a serial poll the transport does not have), reading memory, `SINT`/`DINT` formats (need `ISCALE?`).

Behaviour:
- **Reset: `RESET`, not `PRESET`** (p.332-333).  RESET is the power-on state without cycling power: END OFF, OFORMAT ASCII, QFORMAT NORM, NPLC 10, NDIG 7, TRIG AUTO, TARM AUTO, AZERO ON, DCV autorange,
  INBUF OFF, math off, error / auxiliary error / status registers cleared.  `PRESET NORM` (p.319) is similar but also sets NPLC 1, NDIG 6 and TRIG SYN, which the driver sets itself (confirmed) in `configure()`.
  The guide says to send the GPIB device clear before RESET from remote (p.333): `reset_to_defaults()` does (`t.clear()`), then RESET, CSB, an `STB?` barrier, drains `ERRSTR?`, sets END ALWAYS (read back) and
  checks OFORMAT ASCII / numeric queries / INBUF OFF / AZERO ON by read-only queries.  Do not send RESET during an ACAL.
- **`ID?` answers the text `Keysight 3458A`** (p.228, 276); the brief said HP3458A.  Older firmware may answer `HP3458A`: the identity check matches on `3458A` (both pass; a 34410A or anything else is refused).
- Reading transfer: TRIG SYN makes the controller's read request the trigger (p.374-375), so `read()` takes a fresh reading each time; TRIG SGL (driver mode: meter in HOLD, `TRIG SGL` before each read) is the alternative;
  TRIG AUTO / HOLD / EXT are refused by `read()` (AUTO may hand over a stale reading).  `TRIG SGL` cannot be read back (it reverts to HOLD, p.374), so it is the action `trig.sgl`, not a `trig.set` value.
- Termination: the transport gets read termination LF at connect (module helper `set_read_termination`, which uses the transport's own method or the pyvisa resource; `drivers/transport.py` is NOT edited because 40 procedure sidecars list it); binary readings are read by byte count (helper `read_raw`, same approach) so EOI is not needed;
  END ALWAYS is still set after the reset.  Query replies are always ASCII (p.309).
- Formats: ASCII 15 bytes (+CR LF), SREAL IEEE-754 32 bit, DREAL IEEE-754 64 bit, most significant byte first (p.153-154, the guide's own SREAL conversion program is a unit test), overload +-1E+38 (p.309; SREAL rounds slightly
  below, so the driver treats |x| >= 9.9E+37 as overload and raises `OverloadError`).
- Timeouts: `reading_timeout()` = max(30 s, 5 s + 2 x integration time at 50 Hz worst case, doubled with autozero on the DC functions, +15 s for AC) and is applied by `configure()`; `acal_timeout()` = 1.25 x the guide's
  duration + 60 s.  ACAL runs with the bus held (INBUF OFF, p.277): the one write blocks until it ends, so `acal()` raises the transport timeout for it, restores it, never retries the write, checks `INBUF?` first
  and refuses without `inputs_disconnected=True` (p.235: disconnect every input, 2 hours warm-up, then settle 15 / 30 min).  **ACAL ALL takes 16 minutes (p.235, p.77); the brief's 11 minutes is ACAL OHMS.**
  (The p.278 INBUF example comment says ">11 MINUTES": the guide contradicts itself, the driver uses the table on p.235.)
- Writes are set, read back, compared and never retried (`set_confirmed`); only read-only queries retry; the error queue (`ERRSTR?`, 0,"NO ERROR" when empty) is drained after `configure()`, `null()` and `acal()`;
  the ACAL security code is a secret placeholder (never logged, masked in the golden fixture); GTL goes out at `local()` / `cleanup()`.
- `state_summary()` (read-only) returns the settings a procedure depends on; it never sends `ERRSTR?`, `ERR?` or `STB?`.  A `start_state` / `start_state_checks` hook was looked for in `origin/dsb-runtime-rules` and does not exist
  yet, so nothing is wired to it; `state_summary()` is the place to hang it later.
- Null / offset: `null()` sets MATH NULL, takes a reading that loads the OFFSET register (the input must be in the state to be nulled), optionally writes `SMATH OFFSET,x` (read back with `RMATH OFFSET`); `null_off()`.

UNVERIFIED (not printed in the guide, or only assumed; confirm on the real meter):
1. The exact reply layout of numeric queries (`NPLC?`, `FUNC?`, `TEMP?` ...): the guide gives the numeric equivalents but not the number format; the parser accepts any number format.  `FUNC?` / `RANGE?` may report the nominal range
   or the full scale: the driver accepts either (within 0.1 %).
2. Whether `FUNC <f>,<max_input>` turns autorange off: the guide implies it (p.272); the driver reads `ARANGE?` back and refuses if it is on.
3. The exponent form `1E-06` (the guide's examples print `1E-3`, `120E-6`): zero-padded exponents are assumed accepted; a rejection shows up as a read-back mismatch or an `ERRSTR?` entry.
4. `ERRSTR?` numbering in the simulator (100 + the error bit) and the exact message texts; the driver only uses the leading number (0 = no error).
5. The heuristics for reading and ACAL timeouts (the guide gives integration times and ACAL durations, not total reading times).
6. `STB?` as a sequencing barrier after RESET (INBUF OFF holds the bus until the command is done, p.277); `STB?` never shows the ready bit (p.358).
7. `TERM?` numeric reply (1 FRONT, 2 REAR), `INBUF?` 0 / 1, `MATH?` two numbers (NULL = 9), the `OPT?` reply: from the numeric-equivalent tables, never seen on a meter.
8. The `RMATH OFFSET` register holds the first reading after MATH NULL (p.287, example p.290); the driver assumes the reading it triggers is that one.
9. GTL / REN behaviour of the 3458A after `go_local()`, the GPIB address (factory 22, p.238; set from the front panel), SDC not altering settings (p.333 uses it before RESET only).
10. Transfers: one `read_raw` of 4 / 8 bytes per reading with NRDGS 1; multi-reading bursts (NRDGS n) are not used.

Manual misprints met while reading the range tables from the page images (p.273-274): the OHM / OHMF row ">12 to 120" shows a full scale of "120 k-ohm" (it is 120 ohm); the ACI / ACDCI row "0 to .120E-6 -> 100 uA"
should read 120E-6 (the next row starts at 120E-6).  The driver uses the consistent values.  Both are in the report for Mike; nothing printed was silently changed in any limit table (no limits are in this driver).

DCI, for the later procedure that characterises the DC current of calibrators (100 uA to 1 A):
- DCI ranges 100 nA, 1 uA, 10 uA, 100 uA, 1 mA, 10 mA, 100 mA, 1 A; `max_input` up to 1.2 selects the 1 A range whose full scale is **1.05 A** (p.273): a calibrator point above 1.05 A reads as overload, and **points above 1 A cannot be
  measured directly** (the current terminals, `I`, are rated +-1.5 A peak, non-destructive < 1.25 A RMS, p.80; protected by a 1 A 250 V NTD (non-time-delay) fuse, NEVER slow-blow, p.40, 87, 101).  The 5522A / 5560A 2 A to 20 A points need a shunt and
  a DCV reading, which is a different procedure design.
- A procedure needs: the Terminals switch (`terminals()` reports FRONT / REAR; it cannot be set remotely) and the hook-up prompt for the I terminals (diagram from Mike); `reset_to_defaults()` once, then `configure('DCI', range, nplc=N, trigger='SYN')`
  per point (fixed range, never autorange); a zero (open input) `null()` before the points if the lab's method needs it, with the calibrator in standby; settle time after each output change (set by the procedure, not the driver);
  `read_n()` for averaging; `check_errors()`; calibrator back to standby after each point; ACAL only as a separate, prompted step (inputs disconnected, 2 h warm-up, 15 / 30 min settle) and `temperature()` to decide when it is advisable
  (+-1 C since the last autocal, p.234); GPIB address 22 unless the lab changed it; no CAL writes.
- The simulator measures an applied source (`dt.apply('DCI', 1e-3)`) or the bench calibrator; its gain (+2 ppm) and offsets are simulation choices, not facts about a 3458A.

Tests (per file; a single `discover` hangs on test_170_points): `test_driver_3458a`, `test_golden_commands`, `test_reset_startup`, `test_driver_commands_base`, `test_driver_commands_dmm`, `test_cmdreg`.
Golden transcripts: only `tests/golden/agilent_3458a.json` is new and `tests/golden/fstring_lint_baseline.json` gained the `agilent_3458a` block (all zeros); every other fixture is byte-identical.

## Testing
Python with tkinter, `pyvisa`, `pyserial`, `reportlab`. Run any launcher with `--demo` for no hardware.
Real-window tests (need a display; `xvfb-run -a python -m unittest tests.test_asleft tests.test_specmanager`).
Simulated bench: `drivers/simulated.py` (`make_dacq_bench`, `make_dmm_bench`, `make_bench`).

## Procedure modifications in the engines (procmods_engine.py)
Every procedure (truevolt, dacq, fgen, handheld, 34401A, 8846A, MDO4000C, WaveSurfer) mixes in `procmods_engine.ProcModsMixin`. At the start of a Run
(`pm_begin_run`) it loads the stored overrides for the model / instrument ID, patches the point list in place (skip, text, per-point settle, and - only
from valid, enabled, HMAC-verified `limit` rows - error / low / high), and binds the command overrides to the drivers (`use_commands`). Tunables are read
with `self.pm_param(name, default)`: with nothing stored it returns `default` itself, so unmodified runs are byte-identical (the golden transcripts prove
it). What is tunable is listed in `procmods_engine.TUNABLES` (name, default, bounds); a default declared there must equal what the engine passes (a test
checks it). Ranges are not tunable (they are part of a point's identity and of the manufacturer's table). Engines that judge `low <= x <= high` while the
point also carries `error` (8846A) are registered with shape='band': create overrides against `pm_limit_points()`. The report reads
`procmods_engine.applied_modifications(run)` / `applied_info(run)`; the checkpoint carries `overrides` (snapshot) only when something is modified.
New procedure rule: wire the mixin (pm_init / pm_begin_run / pm_attach / pm_checkpoint_fields / skip filter) and read every settle time through pm_param.

## Standards specification manager and uncertainty budget
- `specs/build_standard_spec.py <SpecTrack pdf>` reads a manufacturer accuracy sheet into `specs/standards/<maker>_<model>_standard.json` (with a `problems` list
  from `specmanager.validate_data`).  `specmanager.seed()` loads those files into **lab_registry.sqlite3** (tables standards, standard_assets, spec_rows,
  spec_changes); calibration_procedures.sqlite3 is untouched.  All values are SI base units; scaling is display-only.
- `uncertainty_budget.py`: u(standard) = published accuracy / divisor (2.0 when the sheet gives no confidence level or says 95 % (laboratory convention), 2.576 for 99 %, editable per standard; the stored levels are fixed by the 'Confidence level rule' section below),
  u(resolution) = calibrator last digit / sqrt(12), EMU = k * RSS, k = 2.  Reported = larger of budget and A2LA scope CMC, rounded UP (never below either).
  `audit(reported, budget)` returns OK / UNDER CMC / UNDER BUDGET / NOT IN SCOPE.
- `report_pdf.point_uncertainty(p, standard=None)` uses the budget when the calibrator model is passed (handheld_dmm passes 5522A / 5560A from its calibrator
  choice; truevolt_verification, Procedures/Agilent_34401A.py and Procedures/Fluke_8846A_Verification.py pass 5560A; dacq_verification passes the selected
  calibrator).  Every multimeter procedure now uses the budget.  The MDO4000C, FGEN and WaveSurfer (oscilloscope / generator) engines use it for the points that have a matching sheet (branch `uncertainty-engines`, section 'Oscilloscope and generator engines on the uncertainty budget'); the rest of their points, and the TDS2000C, are still CMC-only.
- Temperature (thermocouple simulation) uncertainty and TAR / TUR, all handheld multimeters (110-series 116, 87V, 287/289, 170-series 179; the Fluke 77 and Klein MM325 have no temperature point):
  the handheld engine sources Type K from the 5522A / 5560A (`TC_TYPE K` + `OUT <t>CEL`, through the 80AK adapter) and the report row now gets an Uncertainty and TAR / TUR from the
  calibrator's published 1-year Type K SIMULATION specification in degC (`specs/standards/fluke_5522a_standard.json` / `fluke_5560a_standard.json`, kind TEMP, direction output, loaded
  into lab_registry.sqlite3 as for every other kind; no new data file).  `specmanager.StandardSpec.lookup_temperature(celsius, tc='K')` is the lookup (signed closed bands, degC rows only, larger
  limit on a border, never extrapolates; the generic `lookup()` works on magnitudes and would mix in the degF rows, so TEMP does not use it).  `uncertainty_budget.build` routes kind TEMP to it:
  u = spec / divisor (5522A 99 % -> 2.576 since 2026-10-22, was 2; 5560A 95 % column -> 2) (+ resolution / sqrt(12) when known), EMU = 2 * RSS, rounded UP to 2 sig. figs.  Printed like '0.17 degC' (`uncertainty.format_value`, never SI-prefixed).  TAR = (high - low) / 2 / spec in degC;
  TUR = the same tolerance / the printed uncertainty.  Outside the calibrator's range (-200 to 1372 degC), with no calibrator chosen, or for a degF point: Uncertainty / TAR / TUR print N/A.
  `Temperature, open input` (functional check, no source) stays N/A.  `handheld_dmm.App.has_uncertainty` decides which points carry an uncertainty; `temperature_note()` adds the basis to the stored footer note.
  Manual values (5522A Operators Manual R0, Ch. 1 'Temperature Calibration (Thermocouple)', printed p. 1-17 = PDF page 37, read from the page image twice), Type K, degC, 90 day / 1 year:
  -200..-100 0.25 / 0.33, -100..-25 0.14 / 0.18, -25..120 0.12 / 0.16, 120..1000 0.19 / 0.26, 1000..1372 0.30 / 0.40; resolution 0.01 degC (note 2) is used for the 5522A (`specmanager.TC_RESOLUTION`; a value edited in
  Standards Specs wins); note 3: 'does not include thermocouple error'.  5560A (checked 2026-10-22 against the 5560A Product Specifications Rev. 8, 6/26, 'TC Specifications', Type K rows on PDF p. 17 = printed p. 16, table header and resolution on printed p. 15, rendered page image):
  1 year, 95 % confidence column, degC: -200..-100 0.22, -100..-25 0.10, -25..120 0.09, 120..1000 0.16, 1000..1372 0.27 = the stored sheet values, unchanged (they were the 95 % / 1 y column; the 5560A sheet is stored at
  95 %, divisor 2, for every function, so TC uses the same column; the 99 % / 1 y column prints 0.28 / 0.13 / 0.11 / 0.21 / 0.35, 99 % / 90 d 0.22 / 0.10 / 0.09 / 0.17 / 0.28).  The 5522A values above are the manual's single
  published column, which the 5522A manual states is at 99 %: stored with no level (divisor 2) until 2026-10-22, now 99 % / divisor 2.576 (see 'Confidence level rule'; 5522A Type K budgets printed 0.17 / 0.27 / 0.19 / 0.41 degC at 0-100 / 400 / -40 / 1000 degC, now 0.13 / 0.21 / 0.14 / 0.32).  Resolution 0.01 degC is printed (p. 15)
  and is now in `specmanager.TC_RESOLUTION['5560A']`, so the 5560A budget includes it as the 5522A does: reported 0.091 (0 / 100 degC, was 0.090), 0.17 (400, was 0.16), 0.11 (-40, was 0.10), 0.28 (1000, was 0.27), 0.23 (-200, was 0.22).
  A2LA: the shipped scope file (a2la_1888_03_cmc.json, Section V pp. 8-32 and XII) has no temperature entry, so NO CMC applies and none is invented: the budget alone is reported.  If the lab's scope lists
  a temperature CMC, add an entry {basis 'generate', kind 'TEMP', bands [{lo, hi (signed degC), rel, abs}]} to that file: `Scope.dmm('TEMP', t)` then floors the reported value as for every other kind (tests cover it).
  Spec condition: the manual's table states none for the reference junction ('tcal +/- 5 degC', 'source/measure', excludes thermocouple error).  The procedure never sends `TC_REF`, so the calibrator is at its reset
  default INT (internal isothermal block, p. 6-35; p. 4-37: use INT for alloy-wire thermocouples, EXT at 0 degC gives the best accuracy); no TC offset is used (TC_OFFSET is for measure mode only).  The printed figures are applied as published.
  `tests/test_beta_temp_uncertainty.py` (hand-computed 5522A / 5560A values, out-of-range N/A, PDF rows, demo marking); `tests/test_tartur_wiring_dmm.py::Exports::test_off_identical_to_baseline` now allows the 287 Temperature rows to differ
  from the baseline (Uncertainty only), like the 87V rows before; every non-temperature row is byte-identical.  `tests/dmm_export_harness.py` knows 'Fluke 116' / 'Fluke 179' (not in ENGINES) and a `cal_mode` argument.
- "Standards Specs" tab: divisor, assets, calibrator resolution per range (logged in spec_changes), sheet issues, "Check an EMU".
- Tests: `python -m unittest tests.test_specmanager`.

## Datasheet Builder core (stage A, branch dsb-core; no UI yet)
- `datasheet_builder.py` (model, exact-Decimal specification expressions, test point calculator, validation, versioned storage), `datasheet_builder_specs.py`
  (datasheet -> runnable `manual-reading` spec with the additive `dsb` extension), `datasheet_builder_match.py` (which imported standard sources / measures each
  point, TAR / TUR, driver capability table). API: module docstrings; extension schema: `specs/README.txt`.
- User datasheets live in the app data folder (`user_specs/`, versions kept, soft delete) plus two NEW tables in `lab_registry.sqlite3`
  (`user_datasheets`, `user_datasheet_versions`); `calibration_procedures.sqlite3` is untouched. Limits are exact (nominal -/+ tolerance), like `build_mm325.py`.
- `procspec.validate_manual_reading(spec)` checks handheld `manual-reading` specs (the shipped ones pass); `procspec.load` now accepts them.
- Not verified: every automation status marked 'unverified' in `datasheet_builder_match.DRIVER_TABLE` (capacitance / thermocouple outputs, 5522A scope option,
  5790B, 53131A/53132A); the example accuracy numbers in tests/dsb_fixtures.py are illustrative, not any manufacturer's.
- Tests: `tests/test_datasheet_builder*.py`.

## Datasheet Builder runtime (stage C, branch dsb-run)
- A saved user-built datasheet is listed by the Manager as its own row (`dsb_manager.user_rows()`, iid `ds:<uid>`, tag `user-built`; nothing is written to
  calibration_procedures.sqlite3) and launched through `Procedures/User_Datasheet.py --spec <file>` -> `dsb_runtime.main()`.
- `dsb_runtime.DsbApp` extends the handheld engine (handheld_dmm.App) with the spec injected, so reset-before-tests, standby after each point, function
  prompts, direct HV, explicit 0 Hz DC, reading padding, TAR/TUR, procmods, checkpoint/resume, As Found / As Left and the PDF are the engine's own.
  `handheld_dmm.py` got additive hooks only (build_points, reading_labels, checkpoint_problem, report_title/header/details, dsb_out/dsb_desc/dsb_device).
- `DsbIO` opens the standards: 5560A / 5522A (sources, scope option for scope points), 5790B and 53131A/53132A (measure generator outputs).
  Per point `route_for` picks the assigned standard when connected and able (driver_table / automation_status), else the engine's calibrator, else by hand.
  Writes are the drivers' set / read back / compare, never retried; outputs go to standby after each point.
- datasheet_builder.save() writes the runnable spec without a matcher plan, so `with_plan()` runs the matcher at load time (read only).
- Tests: tests/test_dsb_runtime.py (units), tests/test_dsb_runtime_bench.py (simulated benches; tkinter stubbed, harness in tests/dsb_run_harness.py).

## Regenerate a report PDF from Asset History (branch reprint-pdf)
- `report_snapshot.py` (UI-free): `report_pdf.write_calibration_pdf` (the one function every engine's PDF goes through) calls `report_snapshot.record_issued` after it writes a REAL
  (non-demo) report that has an Asset History event; it stores header, title, details (technician, conditions, standards with cal info, TAR / TUR choice), the rows exactly as printed,
  footer note, modifications list, printed issue date and version stamps as canonical JSON with a SHA-256, in the NEW table `report_snapshots` of lab_registry.sqlite3 (key = `runs.id`,
  the history event; `ON DELETE CASCADE`). Engines pass the stamps with `snapshot_context=report_pdf.context_for(self)`. Nothing raises on a missing / damaged database; the PDF is unchanged.
- Rows are stored as the display strings that were printed (report_pdf.cert_row already scaled them); no SI numbers exist at that boundary, so none are stored.
- Asset History tab (`labregistry_ui.HistoryPanel`): button, right-click menu and double-click choice on a report row call `report_snapshot.regenerate`: verifies the hash (a mismatch is
  refused, never replaced by a rebuild), asks where to save (default `asset-MM-DD-YYYY.pdf` with the ORIGINAL issue date), asks before replacing any existing file, writes through
  `write_calibration_pdf(issued_at=..., reprint_note=..., record_history=False)`: original Report date kept, one footer line added, no new history event.
- Events from before this feature (no snapshot) are rebuilt from the history rows only if those are complete (point count and fail count agree, every point has As Found and limits); the
  PDF is then marked REBUILT on every page and leaves As Left, uncertainty, TAR/TUR and modifications blank (history does not store them). Otherwise a plain message says what is missing.
- Not stored anywhere today (so not in any snapshot): procedure modifications are not printed by any engine (`modifications=` is never passed) - their summary is kept in the stamps only.
- Tests: `tests/test_reprint_*.py` (named to sort after test_beta because they install tests/_tk_stub.py); `tests/reprint_harness.py` runs the engines' real export code on the simulated benches.


## Live procedure editor (branch live-editor)
"Edit point" (formerly "Edit procedure"; see the next section) in every running procedure window opens the per-point window; the whole-procedure procmods editor (ModifyWindow) is no longer reachable from a running procedure (the point window's "All procedure changes..." button was removed; the module is kept). Logic is in
procmods_live.py (LiveState, diff, events, LiveBridge) and the pm_* live methods of procmods_engine.ProcModsMixin; tests: tests/test_beta_live_editor*.py.
- Edits are saved at once but applied only at the next point boundary (`pm_step`, or `pm_live_boundary` for WaveSurfer). The point in flight is never changed. Outputs go to standby first (a failed standby stops the run and leaves the edits saved); the instrument reset is not re-run.
- Every edit is an event in the run's audit trail (`live_edits` in the checkpoint; who/when/old/new/before_point) and a normal override, or run-only ("this run only"). Developer-mode gate, locked/risk commands, verified flags and HMAC limit rules are unchanged. Live editing is blocked while a pinned progress file or a factory run is active.
- Limits: allowed with the existing rules (devmode, reason, HMAC). The tolerance of a point that already has a reading changes only together with a redo of that point.
- Redo uses the engine's own queue (no automatic write retries). WaveSurfer has no skip and no redo. Demo edits are marked DEMO and stay run-only.
- Covered: truevolt, dacq, fgen, handheld family (incl. DsbApp by inheritance), 34401A, 8846A verification, MDO4000C, WaveSurfer. Not wired: TDS2000C (no procmods layer), Fluke_8846A_Adjustment.
- Unverified: the Tk windows (never viewed); live command edits (no engine supplies a test_command); the simulator accepts command shape only.
- 5522A OUT? reply for a square wave (observed on the bench): `2.000000E+00,VPP,0E+00,0,6.000E+01` for 2 Vpp at 60 Hz = amplitude, unit VPP, 0, 0, frequency. The square-wave read-back requires unit VPP.
## Datasheet Builder grid editor (branch dsb-grid-ui)
- The editor window is now one spreadsheet-like grid (`datasheet_builder_ui.GridEditorWindow`, drawn on a Canvas by `SheetCanvas`) instead of the seven-step wizard: columns STEP NUM, FUNCTION TESTED,
  NOMINAL VALUE, AS FOUND, AS LEFT, Out of Tol, CALIBRATION TOLERANCE (now LOW LIMIT / HIGH LIMIT, see 'Datasheet Builder: Low / High limits'); an Edit Cell bar above it; the spec boxes (Select Spec Types, entry fields, Calc / Recalc, Test Point Definitions) below; a tool column at
  the right (symbols, Ins. Header, Warnings, row remark, Run this row, Lock Status). As Found / As Left stay blank: the printed sheet is a template, never a record (the preview says so).
- All grid logic is in `datasheet_builder_ui_model.GridModel` (a subclass of EditorModel; tkinter-free): rows, selection, keys, cell edits, the boxes -> tolerance (Decimal expression model), multiple test points,
  undo (the EditorModel snapshot gate), print view / PDF. `GridGeometry` holds the pixel maths. The older detail pages (Ranges, Specification table, Sections, Details, Standards and automation) open from More.
- Core change, additive only: `Point.spec` (optional list-of-one `SpecEntry` that governs that one point; written to JSON only when set). `Function.governing(point)` picks it, `limit_for` / `tol_text` / `export_spec`
  use it, and the function-level 'no_spec' error is raised only for points that have neither their own spec nor a function table entry. Old files (no `spec`) load and export unchanged; the schema id stays 'dsb-1'.
- Rows are created in an auto-made range (the next decade up from the nominal); Meas Res / Auto Res / Display Decs write `Range.resolution`.
- Tests: tests/test_dsb_grid_model.py (no tkinter), tests/test_dsb_ui_widgets.py (tests/_tk_stub.py; the stub now has a real `TclError`). The window has never been viewed on a real display.

## Verified on hardware, and the lock (branch `verified-lock`)
- `verified.py` (Tk-free): `Registry` over a NEW append-only table `proc_verify_events` (verify / unlock / relock / seed) in lab_registry.sqlite3; no existing table is touched.
  A procedure's key is its launcher file name (`Fluke_117_Verification.py`) or `ds:<uid>` for a user-built datasheet. State is worked out from the events (who / when / why).
- Seed: `verified.SEED` (code, shipped with every copy) = TDS2000C / TDS1000C-EDU (`Tektronix_TDS2000C_Verification.py`), WaveSurfer WS4104HD (`WaveSurfer_WS4104HD.py`), Fluke 117
  (`Fluke_117_Verification.py`). Written into the real registry the first time it is used; a registry given an explicit path (tests) is never seeded; an unreadable registry keeps the seeded ones locked.
  To seed another one, add it to `SEED` (or use the button in the Procedures list).
- Locked means refused where the edit is saved: `procmods.Store._insert` / `set_enabled` (devmode command / setting / point / text / tolerance edits, import), `procmods_ui_model.ProcModsModel.read_only_reason`
  (the editor before a run and the in-run editor, run scope included), `ModsState.live_blocked_reason`, `datasheet_builder.save` (existing uid) and `.delete`. The in-run 'Edit procedure' control is disabled with the reason
  (`procmods_ui.LiveHook`, green chip via `ui_shell.Shell.set_verified`). Running is unaffected; overrides saved before the lock stay and still apply. Locks are by model for library procedures (the saved changes are kept per
  model) and by key for user-built ones.
- Fingerprint (the files the procedure's own sidecar declares: launcher + engine + driver files, the spec / limit JSON, the saved overrides for the model; line endings and JSON layout ignored; see 'Per-procedure fingerprints' below) is stored at verification time.
  If a LOCKED procedure no longer matches it, it is auto-unlocked: one `unlock` event, who `system`, reason `files changed since verification: <files>` (`Registry.sync`, run by the list refresh and by `lock_reason`, so an
  editor never refuses a changed procedure and the event is written once). Status kind `changed` = 'Changed since verification - needs hardware re-verification' (warn, not green); it stays until 'Mark verified on hardware'
  is pressed again. 'Unlock for editing' (typed reason) remains for an unchanged procedure. User-built procedures are synced by the Procedures list (their spec path is known there).
  When you add a procedure to `Procedures/`, add its `Procedures/<id>.procedure.json` (its `fingerprint` block replaces the old `verified.FILES` table; `tests/test_verified_model.py` and `tests/test_procscan.py` fail otherwise). The suite never blocks an update.
- Not in the PDF reports. Tests: `tests/test_verified_model.py`, `test_verified_editors.py`, `test_verified_ui.py` (the windows have never been viewed).

## Per-point editor (branch point-editor)
Right-click a test point in any engine's point list -> Edit opens a small window for THAT point (procmods_point_ui.PointWindow; logic in tkinter-free
procmods_point_model.PointEditModel; tests: tests/test_point_editor_*.py). The toolbar button is now "Edit point" and opens the same window for the selected row;
the point window has no button for the old whole-procedure editor any more (removed on request; the module stays). Layout: grid, bounded command table (5 rows), scrolling details area, footer always visible, default 980x960 (clamped to the screen), minimum 720x560, resizable; viewed on real Tk 9 under Xvfb in light and dark. The menu item is disabled, with the reason,
on a verified/locked procedure and while that point is being measured. Storage, scopes, audit trail, next-point-boundary rule and redo are the existing
procmods / procmods_live layer; nothing in calibration_procedures.sqlite3 changes.
- Commands panel: the commands the engine will send for this point, in send order, with this point's values filled in. Derived by a DRY RUN of the engine's own
  point routine (`pm_plan_point`, procmods_plan.py) on throw-away drivers over simulator transports with a private clone of the override state; waits are skipped
  (thread-aware `dry_clock`); the live app, io, override state and clock are untouched. So the list cannot drift from the engine (tests compare it with the
  golden transcripts). Per row: registry key, rendered text, read-back query, risk class, verified/UNVERIFIED, changed-from-default marker, Restore default.
- Editable: NORMAL-risk commands. A command is edited as its TEMPLATE (cmdreg.validate requires the placeholders to be kept); locked/output rows are read-only with
  the reason. Stored as kind 'command', target `Driver:key@<pointkey>` (this point only; wins over a driver-wide override). Extra lines before/after the point:
  target `Driver:+before1@pk` / `+after2@pk`, at most `procmods_pointdef.MAX_EXTRA`; a write needs a read-back query and the expected reply; sent once, read back,
  compared, never retried; a mismatch stops the point (outputs to standby). Extra lines are logged `[EXTRA ... UNVERIFIED]`. Everything typed is UNVERIFIED.
- Developer mode (existing gate, devmode.py unchanged: passphrase, PBKDF2, 15-minute idle auto-lock, lock-out; UI `procmods_ui.shared_lock` / `UnlockDialog`): the
  window's toggle only asks that gate. While locked, the limit/nominal/unit/description fields are read-only and the saved scopes (this asset / this model)
  are unavailable. **A RUN-ONLY command edit (typed command, extra line, or removing one made this run) needs NO passphrase (Mike, 'no' to 'should typed
  commands need the passphrase even for a run-only edit'):** `PointEditModel` sets `ProcModsModel.run_commands_ungated`; `ProcModsModel.run_only_commands()` is true only
  when scope is 'run' and every staged item is a kind 'command' (a reset only of an entry in `ctx.run_entries`); `_write_set` then calls
  `procmods.make_run_entry(..., dev_gate=False)`, which `build_entry` honours ONLY for scope 'run' + kind 'command' (limit/param/text/point and every
  stored scope still raise without a session). Run entries are in memory (`ctx.run_entries`), so `procmods.Store` is never touched and its `_need_dev` gate is
  unchanged (tests/test_point_editor_store.py). Blocks that apply whatever the passphrase: verified/locked procedure, point in progress, locked/output-risk
  commands, extra-line rules (write needs read-back + expected reply, sent once, never retried, UNVERIFIED), reason, audit event (who/when/old/new/reason),
  DEMO marking. A mixed change (field edit + command) or a scope other than run still needs developer mode; if the idle auto-lock fires after a saved scope
  was chosen the scope falls back to 'run' and Apply for field edits is blocked. The older all-procedure window (ModifyWindow) is still fully gated. Run-only
  entries live until the instrument ID changes (`pm_state.load(asset=...)`) or the window closes, as before; they never reach the store or another window. When on: tolerance (low/high or +/- error), unit, nominal, description. Stored inside the HMAC-protected 'limit' value (`nominal`, `new_unit`,
  `description`, `fields`). Decimal parsing, display-only SI scaling, low<=nominal<=high unless one-sided, valid units, typed reason, acknowledgement that
  pass/fail changes. A tolerance change on a point with a reading forces a redo; unit/description alone do not.
- Scopes: this run only (default; the only one in demo, marked DEMO) / this asset / this model. Audit event carries who, when, old, new, reason, point id,
  effective-from (`[point X]`, redo list in `live_edits`).
- Engine support: truevolt, dacq, a34401, 8846A verification, fgen, MDO4000C, handheld family: commands + fields. Handheld points with no calibrator output or
  a Manual calibrator: fields only (no commands, a note says why). 8846A AC / capacitance / frequency points: partial commands plus the refusal the procedure
  raises. fgen FLAT / TIMEBASE and MDO: the plan depends on the chosen method / group (MDO shows the scope default-setup block as for the first point of a group).
  DsbApp: source points only. WaveSurfer: no per-point commands (its measurements are driven from the section code, not a point routine): tolerance only.
  Not wired: TDS2000C, Fluke_8846A_Adjustment.
- "Test command": no engine supplies one, so the window says "not tested".
- Unverified: the Tk windows (never viewed, only constructed under tests/_tk_stub.py); every typed command; the dry-run plan is made on the simulator and does not
  predict real replies; fgen / MDO nominal semantics.
- FIXED (branch engine-err-fix): the `work()` worker threads used `except Exception as exc: ... root.after(0, lambda: self._error(exc))`; `exc` is deleted when the
  except block ends, so the lambda raised NameError and a failed point (including a read-back mismatch of an extra line) never reached `_error`. Same bug in
  truevolt, dacq, fgen, Agilent_34401A, Fluke_8846A_Verification. Fix: `err = exc` inside the block and `lambda err=err: self._error(err)`. Nothing else changed
  (the standby-on-failure in the first four is as it was; fgen never had one). Guard: `tests/test_beta_except_closure.py` (AST scan of every repo .py except
  tests/ and .claude/: no lambda / nested def inside an `except ... as NAME` may read NAME) and `tests/test_beta_except_closure_engines.py` (failed measure
  reaches `_error` with its message, busy cleared, on the simulated benches). Not covered by the behavioural test: mdo, tds2000c, handheld, WaveSurfer (the scan
  found no occurrence there).



## Virtual bench (demo mode)
`--demo` = a real run with VIRTUAL instruments (`virtualbench.py`, Tk-free). `--fast-demo` = the old quick demo (no windows, no commands; Manager
checkbox "Fast demo (no windows)"). Apps use `VirtualMixin`: `demo` (any demo), `fast_demo`, `virtual`. An object with only `demo` counts as fast demo.
- Commands go through the REAL driver code over `drivers/simulated*.py` (`calibrator_transport`, `dmm_pair`, `dacq_pair`, fgen/TDS/MDO benches).
  Shared modules test `fast_demo` (not `demo`) for reset (`reset_startup.reset_then`) and standby (`procmods_engine.pm_standby/commit_live`).
- Operator actions that cannot be commands are done on the simulator after the prompt (`operator_sets_up`: Front/Rear, connect a standard;
  `fgen_operator_sets_up`; `mdo_operator_confirmed`). Simulator additions are opt-in (`SimBench.freq_aware`, `external_input`) so golden transcripts hold.
- Handheld typed readings: reading window + marked "Fill with a simulated reading (DEMO)" button (deterministic, in tolerance; "Include some failures"
  toggle in the drawer, seeded by point number). The operator may type anything. Instrument-read procedures read the simulator.
- Waits are scaled by `virtualbench.WAIT_SCALE`; driver-internal sleeps are not. Dialogs are never skipped.
- Evidence: title suffix [DEMO], banner, chip "Demo - virtual instruments", `synthetic=True` on every record, PDF stamped DEMO on every page,
  `CommandLog.virtual` => never appended to ~/.calibration_suite/command_log.txt, no Asset History / registry / standards usage, no settings saved.
- Not switched: WaveSurfer (`--demo` loads canned data; ~4000-line engine, SimScope covers only a few VBS commands), Fluke_8846A_Adjustment (demo
  issues no CAL commands on purpose - safety), IR reading of 287/289 (typed/Fill instead), 5790B / counter standards of user datasheets (done by hand).
- Editing engine/driver files changes their `verified.py` fingerprints: Fluke 117, TDS2000C and WaveSurfer show "Changed since verification" until re-marked.
- Tests: `tests/test_beta_virtual_bench*.py`, harness `tests/virtual_harness.py`.
## Looking at the windows on a real Tk (Linux sandbox)
- `uv python install 3.13` (python-build-standalone from GitHub; includes tkinter) + `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1600x1000x24" <that python> script.py`; screenshots with
  `PIL.ImageGrab.grab(bbox=..., xdisplay=os.environ['DISPLAY'])`. pip/PyPI is not reachable there: borrow Pillow/reportlab by putting the system `dist-packages` of the
  SAME minor Python on PYTHONPATH. `tests/real_tk_checks.py` + `tests/test_beta_real_tk_windows.py` drive the point window, Edit point, Mark verified / Unlock, the Datasheet
  Builder grid and Asset History Regenerate PDF on a real Tk (skipped without tkinter / a display). The older stub-based tests are designed for NO tkinter: do not run the
  whole suite under a real Tk.
- Point window layout (procmods_point_ui.py): grid; command table 5 rows then scrolls; details (selected command, add a line, developer fields) scroll; footer always visible.

## Standards Specs tab, Option A (branch `specs-tab-a`; presentation only; viewed on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb in light and dark at 1260x800 and 1100x700)
- `specmanager_ui.py` (Tk) + `specmanager_view.py` (display model, no Tk, no database; `tests/test_specmanager_view.py`). Layout: standards rail (one card per standard: model, maker, description, a confidence / divisor chip
  that is blue when the sheet states a level or the divisor was set and grey 'k = 2 assumed' when not, the range count, a sheet-issues pill), header card of the selected standard (Confidence, Divisor + Set divisor link, Period,
  Ranges; Assets chips + Set assets; Approved; clickable Sheet issues pill; Export JSON and Check an EMU), then a card with function-type chips (counts, one row, the rest under 'More'), the text filter, the table grouped by
  function (collapsible bands, banded rows) and a footer with the count and the 'double-click a row to set its calibrator resolution' hint. Import spec JSON sits at the foot of the rail.
- Display only. A specification is formatted from the PRINTED text with decimal arithmetic (no float): `+/-(800 ppm I.V. + 0.006 mV)` shows as `±(800 ppm + 6.00 µV)`; a term is never rounded (3 significant digits at least, more when the
  printed value needs them); any line that is not the usual shape (dB, 'x N', notes) is shown as printed with the signs replaced. The printed text is the tooltip of the cell. Spans / frequencies / resolutions are SI-scaled
  (3 significant digits). Stored rows, `DEFAULT_VARIANT` (excludes auxiliary and 7 day variants in a plain lookup; the table still lists every row), limits and the uncertainty / TAR / TUR numbers are untouched.
- The period columns follow the sheet: the standard's default period first (unless the sheet hardly prints it), then the most printed ones; up to three, two when the table is narrow; the Period block names what is shown and what else is on the sheet
  (8563E: 2 yr / typical, 8902A: spec / supplemental, 5790B: 1 yr / 2 yr / 90 day / 24 hr).
- Widths: rail 300 px at 1180 and wider, 248 px below, 224 px below ~1000, and below ~940 the rail is replaced by a selector above the header card. The window itself has a minimum of ~1040 px, so the selector only shows if the panel is hosted narrower.
- Found while doing this: the old table's Sheet issues column and the Sheet issues window read `export_data()['problems']`, a key `export_data` never had, so they always said 0 / 'None found.' although the import stores the issues in
  `standards.problems_json` (5520A 12, 5522A 12 of them 2 errors, 5790B 19, 8563E 31, 8902A 27, 96270A 12, 5560A 0). `specmanager.problems_of(standard_id)` (new, read only) reads them; the rail, the pill and the window use it. No schema change.
- Real-Tk checks: `tests/real_tk_checks.py` `checks_standards_specs` / `checks_standards_specs_small` (set divisor, set assets, issues window, Check an EMU, export / import, type and text filters, resolution edit, band toggling, empty states, 1100 px, collapsed rail).
- Not verified: Windows fonts and DPI (the Xvfb Tk here has only bitmap fonts, so the screenshots are wider than Segoe UI would be), the macOS look; the band chevrons are drawn images, the chip / card shapes are canvas drawings.

## Handheld dialogs, layout C (branch `function-window-c`; presentation only; viewed on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb in light and dark)
- "Change the function" window: built in `ui_theme.confirm_steps(..., layout='function_change', card=...)` (`_confirm_function_change`), called ONLY by `handheld_dmm.App._prompt_function`
  (the other engines keep the numbered-list layout; the shared `confirm_steps` still receives the same title / heading / steps / facts, so tests, harnesses and golden transcripts see the
  same dialog as before; the harnesses' `_confirm` take `**_layout`). `handheld_dmm.function_change_card(p, total, standby_text, standby_known=False)` builds the content (pure, tested in
  `tests/test_function_card.py`): a rounded point strip ("14 of 40" in the accent colour, bold test text, muted "Meter: V~  range 6.000 V") and a three-step stepper (standby / turn the rotary
  switch to the short name, with the bracketed text as its muted line / press Done when the meter shows it). Switch text splits at the double space before "(" (110 / 170 / 77 / 87V / MM325);
  287 / 289 and plain names ("Temperature", "AUTO-V LoZ") are used whole. Fixed width, grid, wraps long text, Enter = Done, Esc / close = Cancel.
- Step 1 is a green tick ONLY when `standby_known=True`. The engine sends STBY before the dialog with `standby_quiet()` (errors swallowed, no read-back), so it passes False and step 1 is a
  plain numbered step with the original sentence ("The calibrator output is OFF (standby) while you turn the rotary switch." / the manual-calibrator "Keep the calibrator in STANDBY ...").
- Step 3's muted line says "The program then goes on with this point." (the mockup's "The next window asks for the hook-up" is not true: the hook-up prompt comes BEFORE the function prompt).
- Reading window (`handheld_dmm.ask_reading`, signature unchanged): header line + test text, then rounded cards "METER" (switch / range / the point's note or a datasheet's device text) and
  the source card ("CALIBRATOR OUTPUT": output text + note), the red time-limit line, and an accent-outlined reading card (numeric: "WHAT DOES THE METER SHOW?", entry + unit, annunciator box
  when asked for, error line, ACCEPT IF, Limit, demo fill button + DEMO note; yes / no: question + Yes / No) and Stop run / Skip point / Record (Enter). Marker circles: the meter card is
  ticked (the operator just confirmed the set-up); the source card is ticked only when the note says the program has the output ON (`OUTPUT_ON_NOTE`), otherwise an empty circle. The mockup's
  "Connect" card is NOT built (the reading window has never shown the hook-up and the data is not passed to it).
- Helpers in ui_theme: `rounded_card`, `status_circle` (drawn tick, no font glyph needed), borders drawn as two filled shapes (a 1 px smoothed outline renders broken on this Tk).
- Real-Tk checks: `tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_handheld_dialogs` (nothing clipped, Enter / Esc / Skip / Stop).

## Procedure registration and packages (branch `procedure-packages`; checked on the simulated bench / throw-away trees and on a real Tk under Xvfb only)
- Registration: the Manager no longer lists procedures by hand. Each procedure is `Procedures/<id>.py` + `Procedures/<id>.procedure.json` (schema 1; fields in `procscan.py`'s docstring: id, name,
  manufacturer, model, category (= instrument class), version, order, launcher, engine, drivers, specs, `fingerprint {code, spec}`, `hookup {meter, diagram_prefixes}`, `seed_verified` / `seed_order`, `own_files`, `tests`).
  `procscan.scan()` reads them (cached on the sidecars' mtimes; a bad sidecar is skipped and listed in `ScanResult.problems`, it never stops the others). `Calibration_Procedure_Manager.init_db()` registers
  `[p.registration for p in procscan.procs(BASE)]` exactly as the old hand list did (same names, order, categories, launch behaviour; same `INSERT` / rename / de-duplication logic; the procedures table
  schema is untouched); `STABLE` and `VERSIONS` are filled from the scan. A launcher with no sidecar (`User_Datasheet.py`) is not a registered procedure.
  The sidecar is a separate file so editing metadata never changes a launcher (a launcher edit changes a verified procedure's fingerprint).
  New procedure = launcher + sidecar (+ spec / driver / diagrams / tests). Nothing to edit in the Manager, `hookups.py` or `verified.py` any more. Exception: a NEW handheld meter family still needs its
  `hookups.py` tables (not done from metadata; `hookups.py` and the engines were deliberately not touched).
  `tests/test_procscan.py` compares the scan with `tests/fixtures/legacy_registry.json`, the frozen hand-written lists of main 59cfe1c (records, STABLE, VERSIONS, verified.FILES, verified.SEED).
  Older tests that grepped the Manager source for the hand list use `tests/_registry_text.manager_text()` (the same strings rebuilt from the scan).
- Per-procedure fingerprints (`verified.py`): `FILES` is now a read-only view of the sidecars' `fingerprint` lists (paths only). A label `specs/x.json#117` = only the part of a shared multi-model spec that
  model 117 uses (its `models` entry and the steps that list it; everything common stays); `hookups.py#fluke110` = hookups.py with the other meters' entries of METERS / AVAILABLE / EXTRA left out
  (code and shared tables still count). So editing 113's steps or the 87V jack names no longer unlocks 117; a step shared by two models, the common part of the spec, `handheld_dmm.py`, `drivers/fluke_5560a.py`,
  `drivers/base.py`, `drivers/transport.py` and the hookups code DO still unlock everything that runs on them (they change what those procedures do). `hookups.py` was dropped from 287 / 289 (their spec has no `hookup_meter`).
  Records taken before this (whole files stored) are still compared: only the files declared TODAY are looked at, a sliced label is compared on the whole file when the record has none, a declared file the record
  lacks counts as changed (`Registry.changes_since`). Auto-unlock, the lock rules, `proc_verify_events` and the seed are as before. The three seeded procedures (TDS2000C, WaveSurfer, Fluke 117) are NOT re-marked.
  `verified.SEED` is derived from `seed_verified` in the sidecars; a package can neither add nor remove that flag (refused on import; an existing flag is kept when a package replaces its sidecar; `make_package` strips it).
- Packages (`packages.py`): zip with `manifest.json` at the root (package_format 1, procedure id / name / version, min_suite_version, author, created, requires, `files` [{path, sha256, size}], optional `verified` {date, who, fingerprint}).
  `packages.inspect()` checks everything and writes nothing; `packages.install()` repeats the check, refuses if the zip changed since the preview, backs up every replaced file to `Backups/packages/<folder>/<name>_backup_vNN<ext>`,
  writes atomically one by one, rolls back on failure, and records the import in the NEW append-only table `package_imports` of lab_registry.sqlite3 (procedure, version, zip sha256, origin, who, when, files, backups, unlocked, mark).
  `calibration_procedures.sqlite3` is never opened by the importer (the Manager registers a new procedure the usual way when the list refreshes). Allowed: Procedures/ specs/ drivers/ diagrams/ tests/ only; extensions .py .json .png .jpg .txt .md .csv;
  never executable / database names; `.py` is parsed with `ast`, never run. Zip-slip, absolute / drive / backslash / `..` / hidden / device-name paths, symlinks, unlisted or missing files, bad or missing checksums, a sidecar that disagrees,
  a duplicate display name, the seeded flag, unmet requirements (a fingerprint file that is neither packaged nor installed), newer format / suite, size limits are all refused. Engines at the suite root cannot be packaged (outside the allowed folders):
  they are `requires` (the target must have them); a NEW procedure's engine has to live in `Procedures/`.
  Verified: replacing files of a verified-and-locked procedure unlocks it (`unlock`, who `package import`); a package mark counts only if `file_fingerprint` (declared files with the package's files in place, no overrides) equals the mark and the model has
  no saved procedure changes; then a `verify` event (mark's date / name) follows. The mark is the exporting lab's claim: it proves identical files, not a hardware run. Other locked procedures hit by a replaced shared file are listed in the preview (`Preview.affected`) and show 'changed'.
  `tools/make_package.py <id> [--out DIR] [--include PATH] [--verified-mark] [--author NAME]` (`--list`). Zips are reproducible.
- UI: Procedures tab button 'Import procedure package...' (`Manager.import_package`: picker -> `packages_ui.ask_install` preview -> install -> refresh); the library folder setting is the 'Library folder...' button next to it on the Procedures bar (the Procedure Development tab was removed, see 'Procedures tab: Run at the top')
  (`~/.calibration_suite/packages.json`, key `library_folder`, default empty; the picker opens there; the preview says whether the file is inside it). The preview carries a plain warning that a package is program code, shows the origin
  (full path + sha256), and keeps Install disabled until a confirmation box is ticked. No hosting, no database of packages. Tests: `test_procscan`, `test_packages`, `test_fingerprint_isolation`, `test_packages_ui`, plus `checks_import_package` in
  `tests/real_tk_checks.py` (run by `test_beta_real_tk_windows`; screenshots when PKG_SHOTS is set).
- UNVERIFIED / limits: nothing here sends an instrument command. The package windows were viewed on a real Tk under Xvfb only (Linux), never on Windows. Mark validity cannot prove a hardware run. Packaging of engines at the root and of a new `hookups.py` meter family is not supported.

## Run queue for the handheld multimeters (branch `run-queue`; checked on the simulated bench and on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only, never run on hardware)
Several meters of one model in one session. `handheld_dmm.App` only (every handheld procedure: 110 series, 77 IV, 87V, 170 series, 287 / 289, MM325). One meter = today's run, byte for byte.
- Files: `run_queue.py` (Tk-free model: `RunQueue` / `QueueMeter`, `share_reason`, `plan`, `summary`, `progress`), `run_queue_engine.py` (`QueueMixin`, mixed into `handheld_dmm.App`), `run_queue_ui.py` (set-up window `ask_queue_setup`,
  one-window reading entry `ask_readings`, non-modal `QueueProgress`), `tests/test_beta_run_queue.py`, `tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_run_queue` / `checks_run_queue_app`. Harness: `tests/dsb_run_harness.py` / `virtual_harness.py` patch
  `handheld_dmm.ask_readings` and the progress window (`queue_readings`, `queue_default`, `queue_no_reading`, `queue_action`, `progress_log`).
- The point loop is NOT copied. `App._next` runs over each UNIT of `run_queue.plan(...)`: a SHARED unit (all meters wired together: the hook-up / function / meter-setup / HV prompts appear once and name every meter, the calibrator is set once per
  point, `_ask` -> `_ask_shared` shows ONE reading window and stores one record per meter) or a SINGLE unit (one meter, the unchanged single-meter flow with that meter's context). `handheld_dmm.py` got small hooks only: the mixin, `execute` ->
  `queue_execute` when the queue has two or more meters, `_next` (unit end, progress tick, fast-demo record), `_ask` (shared dispatch), `_qfacts` / `_qsteps` / `_qnote` / `_q_hv_text` wrapped around the existing prompt texts (identity when no
  shared unit is running), `busy` setter (an aborted queue keeps every meter's progress), `run_all` / `rerun` / `export_pdf` / `load` / `_checkpoint_path(asset=None)`, the drawer button and the chip. No instrument command, limit, judging code
  or prompt text of a single-meter run changed; `tests/test_beta_run_queue.py::SingleMeterUnchanged` runs a one-meter queue and the old run and compares the wire transcript, every window and the results.
- Per-meter state: `QueueMeter` holds asset, serial, technician, status, its two result dicts (As Found / As Left) and its rest timer. `_queue_activate(m)` swaps them in (asset / serial variables, `_found` / `_left`), so `_store`, the checkpoint file
  (`<brand><model>_<asset>_checkpoint.json`, as before), `certificate_rows_and_points`, the report header and Asset History / report snapshot (through `write_calibration_pdf`) are that meter's own. Nothing is merged between assets;
  an asset can be listed once. After a run the window shows meter 1; the progress window's Show buttons switch (`queue_show`).
- Modes: POINT BY POINT (default) and ONE METER AT A TIME. One at a time = the whole existing flow per meter in queue order, the calibrator reset before each meter (as before every run), a 'Next meter' prompt between meters.
  Over IR (287 / 289 on the IR cable) the queue is one meter at a time only (one cable).
- HOW SHARING IS DECIDED (`run_queue.share_reason(p, spec options)`; None = shareable): shared by default: DCV, ACV (incl. frequency, the high-voltage points), OHM (2-wire, beeper / continuity), CAP, TEMP, and the no-calibrator yes / no checks
  (display test ...). NOT shared: current (kinds DCI / ACI, groups AMP10 / UAMP: meters in series), 4-wire ohms (group OHM4), VoltAlert (group VOLTALERT), scope outputs (groups SCOPE_*), the 87V duty-cycle / square-wave special sources (kinds DUTY / SQUARE),
  numeric points with no calibrator output (set by hand). Explicit flags win and are additive (no shipped spec carries one, so no spec file or its fingerprint changed): point field `queue_share: true | false`; spec `options.queue_share: false`
  (whole family one-meter-only), `options.queue_single_kinds`, `options.queue_single_groups`. A not-shareable point (and its neighbours that are not shareable either) becomes a one-meter-at-a-time unit: a 'One meter at a time' prompt names the reason and
  the meter, then that meter runs the single-meter flow for those points, then the next meter. Only meters that still need a point are asked for it (resume / rerun).
- HV: the HIGH VOLTAGE question (threshold per spec `hv_threshold_v`, unchanged) is asked once per high-voltage point for the whole queue and names every meter on the leads (not once per meter, not once per group: the existing per-point rule is kept).
- Reading window (`run_queue_ui.ask_readings`): one row per meter (asset on the row, own box, live PASS / FAIL via `judge_numeric` / `pad_reading` with the point's own limits, padding note, deviation from nominal), Enter = next box, Enter in the last box
  or 'Record all (Enter)' stores, 'No reading' on a row (that meter gets no record for the point), Skip point / Stop run apply to the whole queue (nothing is stored for the point on Skip; Stop ends the queue, progress saved). Yes / no points (display test, beeper,
  diode checks) use the existing yes / no window once per meter (naming the asset) and store only when every meter answered. The AC annunciator question is asked per meter (names the asset). Timed points (`max_on_s`) keep their limit.
  Demo: 'Fill with a simulated reading (DEMO)' fills every box (deterministic by point number and row; 'include some failures' applies).
- Resume: each meter's checkpoint is its own file. Starting a queue whose meters have saved progress (same model, same demo flag, datasheet check) asks ONCE: Yes = continue (recorded points are not measured again), No = start those meters over, Cancel = nothing runs.
  `pm_on_checkpoint` is applied for the first resumed file only. The list of meters and the mode are saved in `<brand><model>_queue.json` and offered (never activated) when the window is opened with an empty queue. 'Load progress' keeps the queue meter in step.
- Procedure modifications (stored per asset) must be identical for every meter of a queue (normally none); otherwise the queue refuses to start and says which asset differs (a shared point list can follow one set only). Live edits made during the run apply to
  the shared points from then on, for all meters. The in-run editor's 'measured' view follows the meter shown.
- Reports: `queue_export_pdfs()` writes one 'Calibration Data' PDF per meter (asset#-date, never overwrites an existing file: `-2`), the queue technician replaces the technician of the details dialog, TAR / TUR as in the single report, Asset History + report snapshot
  per meter. 'Export Report (PDF)' with a queue asks 'all meters?'. Demo reports stay marked DEMO, nothing reaches Asset History.
- Not supported: user-built datasheets (`dsb_runtime.DsbApp`: `queue_supported()` is False, no queue button, runs as before).
- Remote-meter engines (34401A, 8846A, Keithley 2000, 34410A, 8808A, Truevolt) are NOT touched. What a queue would need there: a second instrument connection per meter (each meter has its own GPIB / USB / RS-232 address and driver instance,
  so the meters are read by the program, not typed); per-meter `IO` / driver sessions with reset-to-defaults for each; a per-meter results store like `QueueMeter`; an automatic multi-meter reading (all meters triggered, read back, compared with set / read back /
  compare, no write retried); a decision on how meters share the calibrator leads and the front / rear terminal choice; a per-meter command log; the same per-meter report / Asset History / checkpoint rules; and a plan like `run_queue.plan`.
- UNVERIFIED: nothing was run on real hardware or Windows. Parallel connection of several meters on one calibrator output for resistance, capacitance and thermocouple simulation is the lab's call (voltmeter loading, ohmmeter test currents interacting, a single TC
  connector): the defaults follow the project brief, `queue_share: false` / `options.queue_single_kinds` switch any of it off per point or family. The handheld_dmm.py edit changes the fingerprint of every handheld procedure (Fluke 117 shows 'Changed since
  verification' until re-marked); the three new modules are not in any sidecar's fingerprint list (`tests/fixtures/legacy_registry.json` is frozen).

## 5560A SCOPE output impedance (fixed)
`OUT_IMP` takes `Z50` or `Z1M` (5560A Remote Programmer's Manual, OUT_IMP page, supplied by Mike; same form as the 5522A manual p. 10-31). The shared driver used to send `OUT_IMP 1M` / `OUT_IMP 50`, which the 5560A rejects (-117). `outimp.set` is now `OUT_IMP Z{source_impedance}` and `select_scope_dcv` compares an `OUT_IMP?` reply of `Z50`/`Z1M` with what was written. Golden transcripts changed only on those lines (WaveSurfer DCV, MDO4000C, fluke_5560a). Shared driver: every procedure on it shows CHANGED since verification until re-marked.

## Fluke 5520A standard sheet (specs/standards/fluke_5520a_standard.json)
Source: 5520A Service Manual (5520A_Manual.pdf), Chapter 1 specifications, read from rendered page images. Page numbers are PRINTED pages (PDF page = printed + 16 in the 1-x chapter). Builder: `specs/build_5520a_standard.py` (table driven, writes the JSON through the same `specmanager` parsers as the 5522A sheet). Test: `tests/test_standard_5520a.py` (hand-worked values plus `uncertainty_budget.build` / `tartur.compute`).
- Stored, 90 day and 1 year columns, tcal +/-5 C: general, confidence, conditions p. 1-7; DC voltage 1-8 (plus AUXILIARY output, variant `auxiliary`); DC current 1-9; resistance 1-11 (12 h zero floor in "Resistance", 7 day floor in "Resistance [7 Day Zero Cal Floor]", variant `7 day`); AC voltage sine 1-12 and auxiliary 1-13; AC current sine, LCOMP off 1-14 and on 1-15; capacitance 1-16; thermocouple simulate and measure (B C E J K L N R S T U), resolution 0.01 C, p. 1-17; RTD, resolution 0.003 C, p. 1-18; frequency 1-23 (2.5 ppm + 5 uHz, 1 year only, jitter 100 ns). The sheet's `limits` key holds the output limits (AC volts above 330 V only to 10 kHz, AC current frequency bands, max 500 kHz, capacitance range, power/dual limits p. 1-20, extended bandwidth note p. 1-25).
- Not stored: stability, noise, burden, compliance adders, power, phase, harmonics, non-sine and extended AC, TC linear mode, SC300/SC600.
- `specmanager.DEFAULT_VARIANT` now excludes `auxiliary` (DCV, ACV) and `7 day` (OHM) from plain lookups. No existing sheet uses those words, so existing lookups are unchanged.
- Confidence: the 5520A manual prints 99 % (p. 1-7); the sheet is stored at 99 % (divisor 2.576). WAS inconsistent across standards (5522A: no level, divisor 2, although its manual says 99 %); harmonised 2026-10-22, see 'Confidence level rule': 5522A and 5520A both 99 % / 2.576, 5560A its 95 % column / 2. TAR is unaffected (no divisor). Their 10 V DC budgets are now equal as stored (tested).
- 5522A sheet vs 5520A manual (possibly a genuine model difference; NOT changed, to be checked against the 5522A manual): LCOMP on, 32.999 mA, 10-100 Hz, 1 yr: 0.8 % (5522A sheet) vs 0.08 % (5520A manual); LCOMP on, 329.99 uA, 100 Hz-1 kHz, 1 yr: 0.5 % vs 0.6 %; capacitance floors differ in several ranges. All AC volts and LCOMP-off AC current figures agree; DC and resistance agree except where the 5522A sheet's note 8 overrides.
- Manual misprints (stored as interpreted, flagged in the sheet's problems): DC current first row "0 to 329.999 mA" (stored uA) and its resolution unit; LCOMP-off 3-10.9999 A band "100 kHz to 1 kHz" (stored 100 Hz to 1 kHz); LCOMP-on footnote [3] looks transposed; capacitance "0.33 uF to 1.09999mF" (stored 0.33 mF). Pt 385 100 ohm RTD is absent from the 5520A table.
- UNVERIFIED: DC volts printed "30 V to 329.9999 V" and "100 V to 1000.000 V" overlap lower ranges, stored as non-overlapping bands (33-329.9999, 330-1000 V) with the printed text kept; no DC row from 1000 to 1020 V; the 12 h resistance floor is used by default and the 2-wire +5 uV/A floor term and low-current floor scaling are not applied.
- Registration (generic only): `sm.seed()` loads the sheet, so it is selectable in Standards Specs and is a driver-less candidate in the Datasheet Builder ("cannot automate"); `uncertainty_budget` and `tartur` work with `standard='5520A'`. Tests listing the registered standards now include 5520A. 34410A suitability (`specs/analyze_34410a_suitability.py`, regenerated .md) uses the real 5520A sheet instead of the 5522A proxy: identical to the 5522A except DC current 100 mA and 3 A (5522A sheet deliberately conservative).
- Calibrator pickers: DONE on branch `picker-5520a`, see 'Fluke 5520A calibrator pickers' below. Still NOT done: fixed-5560A procedures (Truevolt / 34401A / 8846A), `LAB_DEFAULT` entry. Defaults unchanged.
- Remote commands (5520A vs the Fluke5522A driver): this manual covers only CAL_* commands (printed pp. 3-28 to 3-33); normal commands are in the Operators Manual Ch. 5-6 (not supplied). Confirmed on the 5520A (sample program pp. 3-18 to 3-20): `*CLS`, `*RST`, `*OPC?`, `ERR?`, `OUT <v>, <f>` (explicit 0HZ for DC), `OUT?` with frequency in field 5, `OPER`, `CAL_SW?`; STBY (error 532, p. 4-10). Error list pp. 4-8 to 4-11 implies DUTY (510, 538), WAVE (536), compensation (539, 548), TC_REF (541), OUT_IMP (547), SCOPE option (551/552), and 1331 "OPER not allowed while error pending". NOT in this manual, so no comparison possible: ZCOMP, EXTSENSE, TC_TYPE, RTD_TYPE, OUT_IMP / WAVE / DUTY syntax. SCOPE is an option on the 5520A. Needs the Operators Manual or Programmers Guide (PN 688744).
- Serial: two rear ports, SERIAL 1 FROM HOST and SERIAL 2 TO UUT (pp. 1-4, 1-5, Fig. 1-2); host cable Fluke PM8914 (p. 3-28); loopback test pins 2, 3, 5 on a straight-through cable (p. 4-7). Baud, parity and terminator are NOT stated; the repo SERIAL_DEFAULT (9600 8N1, write CR, read CRLF) is unverified for the 5520A. IEEE-488.1/488.2 (p. 1-4).

## Fluke 5520A calibrator pickers (branch `picker-5520a`, merged with main 294185c)
Rule: the 5520A is a picker choice only where its published sheet (`specs/standards/fluke_5520a_standard.json`) has a row for every point the procedure sources. A missing row means the 5520A does not publish that output. Decided by `calibrator_fit.py` (`check` / `assess` / `make_offer`), which calls `StandardSpec.lookup` and, for the reason text, `datasheet_builder_match.explain`; TAR from `tartur.compute`. Nothing is hand-listed per procedure. Test: `tests/test_calibrator_fit.py`.
- Offered (all points inside the sheet): Fluke 110, 113, 114, 115, 116, 117; 175 / 177 / 179; 287 / 289; 77IV; Klein MM325; Keithley 2000; Keysight 34970A / 34972A; user datasheets in the Datasheet Builder (`dsb_runtime`, `DRIVER_TABLE['5520A']`, per-point manual fallback). Handheld choices: 'Fluke 5520A  (automatic output)' and 'Manual  (5520A front panel)'.
- Offered with per-point manual routing: Fluke 8808A. The 5520A cannot source 5 points (750 V AC at 20 / 50 / 100 kHz, 1 MHz, and 10 A AC at 40 Hz); those use the engine's existing manual path (technician connects another standard, meter still read automatically). The reason comes from `calibrator_fit`, not the spec's `cal_limits`.
- NOT offered, with the reason in the note under the picker and its tooltip: Fluke 87V (3 square-wave points 1-16, 1-48, 1-49: the sheet holds no non-sine rows and WAVE / DUTY / DC_OFFSET are unverified); Keysight 34410A (3 AC volts points at 300 kHz). A saved 5520A choice on these falls back to the default. Unchanged by design: the 34410A picker still offers the 5522A although it cannot source those 3 points either (not touched: existing behaviour).
- Procedures with a fixed calibrator (Truevolt, 34401A, 8846A, scopes, generators, TDS2000C) were not touched.
- LCOMP rule: AC current lookups use variant 'lcomp off' because the driver never switches LCOMP (otherwise 10 A at 40 Hz would pass on LCOMP-on rows).
- TAR below 1:1 is a warning in the note, not an exclusion (same as the 5522A).
- Driver: `drivers.Fluke5520A(Fluke5522A)` (MODEL 'Fluke 5520A', IDN_MATCH '5520'). Registry: only 6 of 41 commands are confirmed by the supplied Service Manual (`ERR?`, `*OPC?`, `OPER`, `OUT` with explicit frequency, `OUT?`, `*CLS`); everything else is verified=False: STBY, OPER?, what `*RST` leaves, volts / ohms / capacitance / temperature OUT forms, WAVE / DUTY / DC_OFFSET, SCOPE option, ZCOMP (never sent: the operator sets 4-wire by hand), baud / parity / terminators (the repo default is used). The note tooltip lists this (`Fluke5520A.unverified_summary()`). Hook-up pictures use the 5522A ones (same NORMAL / AUX terminals; UNVERIFIED). HV and ramp logic unchanged.
- Simulation: `drivers/simulated_5520a.py` (`Sim5520ALimitsTransport` refuses any OUT the sheet does not publish; a run of an offered procedure must see zero refusals). Demo mode unchanged: reports stay marked as demo.
- Golden transcripts: only new entries (`Fluke5520A.open`, `Fluke5520A.reset_to_defaults` in tests/golden/fluke_5560a.json); no 5522A / 5560A line changed.
- Effect: editing handheld_dmm.py, the 8808A / k2000 / dacq / 34410A engines, dsb_runtime.py and drivers/fluke_5560a.py changes their verified.py fingerprints: those procedures show 'Changed since verification' until re-marked.

## Confidence level rule (lab reports at 95 %, k = 2) - branch `confidence-95`, NOT merged: Mike must approve the changed uncertainties
Mike (A2LA): "We operate on a 95 spec": the expanded uncertainty printed on a report is k = 2.
- Rule: u(standard) = published accuracy / divisor, where the divisor belongs to the confidence level the sheet's NUMBERS are printed at (`specmanager.StandardSpec.divisor`): 99 % -> 2.576 (NOT 2), 95 % -> 2.0.
  A manufacturer that prints a 95 % column next to the 99 % one (5560A): store that column, divisor 2. A manufacturer that prints only one level: store that level. A sheet that states no level keeps divisor 2 (laboratory
  convention, flagged: the sheet issue 'Confidence level is not specified' stays). Resolution is /sqrt(12), EMU = 2 * RSS, reported = larger of EMU and the A2LA CMC rounded UP: all unchanged. TAR (accuracy ratio) uses no divisor: unchanged.
  `tests/test_standard_confidence.py` fails if a sheet's stored level / divisor disagrees with this table, or if a new sheet is added without a decision.
- Survey (printed level; stored `confidence_pct`; divisor; consumers). Budget = `uncertainty_budget.build` (reports' Uncertainty column and TUR through `report_pdf.point_uncertainty` / `tartur.compute`; Datasheet Builder `datasheet_builder_match`); TAR = `tartur.standard_accuracy` (no divisor).
  | sheet | printed by the manufacturer | stored (was -> now) | divisor (was -> now) | who consumes it |
  | 5522A (specs/standards/fluke_5522a_standard.json) | 99 %, one column (Operators Manual, General Specifications, printed p. 1-8 = PDF p. 28, read from the rendered page image 2026-10-22); the lab's SpecTrack sheet itself prints [Not Specified] | none -> 99 | 2 -> 2.576 | budget + TUR for every procedure that picks the 5522A (list below); TAR |
  | 5520A (fluke_5520a_standard.json) | 99 %, one column (Service Manual printed p. 1-7) | 99 (unchanged) | 2.576 (unchanged) | budget + TUR when selected (34410A picker; budget when a procedure passes '5520A'); TAR |
  | 5560A (fluke_5560a_standard.json) | 99 % (k = 2.58) AND a 95 % (k = 2) column; "Fluke Calibration guarantees product performance to the 99 % level" (Product Specifications p. 1, rendered image) | 95 (unchanged: the stored numbers are the 95 % column) | 2 (unchanged) | Truevolt 34460A-70A, 34401A, 8846A (fixed 5560A), picker elsewhere; TAR |
  | 5790B (fluke_5790b_standard.json) | 99 %, k = 2.58 (Operators Manual p. 1-9); the 5790B/AF 50 MHz line is 95 %, k = 2 (p. 1-22, sheet note 20) | 99 (unchanged) | 2.576 (unchanged) | TAR only in the generator procedures (`tartur.standard_accuracy('5790B', ...)`); its budget is not used on a report. Per-standard divisor: the one 50 MHz /AF row (95 %) would be divided by 2.576 if it were ever budgeted: FLAG |
  | 8563E, 96270A, 8902A | no level printed | none | 2 (assumption) | nothing on a report yet (no procedure uses them as a budgeted standard) |
  | 53131A / 53132A | no accuracy table in the Programming Guide: no sheet exists (`specs/standards/agilent_53131a_53132a_NOTE.txt`) | - | - | nothing |
  | 3458A (keysight_3458a_standard.json) | no level and no coverage factor printed (data sheet 5965-4971E, pp. 8-35; accuracy 'relative to the calibration standards', p. 8) | none | 2 (assumption, owner accepted for now) | TAR / budget when asked with direction 'input'; Datasheet Builder matcher (driver-less, ranked after any driver-backed candidate) |
- Change (metadata only, no spec number touched; `functions` of the 5522A JSON are identical to origin/main): `specs/standards/fluke_5522a_standard.json`: `standard.confidence_pct` null -> 99.0, `confidence_text` '[Not Specified]' -> '99 % (5522A Operators Manual ... p. 1-8 ...)', new note 9 with the source, and the sheet issue 'Confidence level is not specified' removed.
  `specs/build_standard_spec.py` has `MANUAL_CONFIDENCE` so a rebuilt 5522A sheet keeps the level. The 5520A / 5560A / 5790B / other sheets are not changed (5520A note 2 and `build_5520a_standard.py` only lose the 'not harmonised' wording).
- Existing databases: `specmanager.seed()` skips a model already in lab_registry.sqlite3, so a lab database made before this change would have kept no level. New `specmanager.sync_confidence()` (run by `seed()` and once per process from `standard()`) fills `confidence_pct` / `confidence_text`
  from the shipped sheet ONLY where the database has none, logs it in spec_changes (who 'sync'), never overwrites a level or an explicit divisor somebody set in Standards Specs, and touches no spec row. The divisor column of lab_registry.sqlite3 is not written.
  calibration_procedures.sqlite3 is not touched.
- Impact (old = divisor 2.0 forced, new = sheet 99 % / 2.576; `python tools/confidence_impact.py [--all]` re-runs it: real `tartur.compute`, no instruments). u(5522A) shrinks by exactly 2/2.576 = 0.7764 (-22.4 %); the EMU by about -22 % (the resolution term does not shrink),
  and the PRINTED uncertainty (rounded UP to 2 significant figures) by -20 % to -28 % at most points, less where the scope CMC or the resolution term dominates (some points do not move). TUR = tolerance / printed uncertainty grows by +25 % to +40 %. TAR does not change.
  Counts (points that print an uncertainty with the 5522A as calibrator / of those, how many change): Fluke 117 (110 series) 31 / 27, Fluke 87V 40 / 36, Keysight 34410A 65 / 54, Fluke 8808A 84 / 59; the rest sit on the CMC floor or are capacitance / diode-type points.
  Examples, printed uncertainty old -> new (TUR old -> new): 117 DC 5 V (3-19) 81 uV -> 63 uV (-22.2 %) (333:1 -> 429:1); 117 AC 5 V 1 kHz (3-12) 1.4 mV -> 1.1 mV (-21.4 %) (73.6 -> 93.6); 87V DC 3.3 V (1-17) 60 uV -> 47 uV (-21.7 %) (33.3 -> 42.6);
  87V AC 3.3 V 60 Hz (1-3) 1.1 mV -> 0.86 mV (-21.8 %); 34410A DC 10 V 150 uV -> 110 uV (-26.7 %) (2.33 -> 3.18); 34410A AC 1 V 1 kHz 220 uV -> 170 uV (-22.7 %) (4.09 -> 5.29); 8808A AC 1 V 50 Hz 220 uV -> 170 uV (-22.7 %) (54.5 -> 70.6);
  8808A DC 19 V 250 uV -> 200 uV (-20.0 %). Type K simulation (handheld Temperature rows, 5522A): 0.17 -> 0.13 degC (0 / 100), 0.27 -> 0.21 (400), 0.19 -> 0.14 (-40), 0.41 -> 0.32 (1000); TUR e.g. 5.8:1 -> 7.6:1.
  Fluke 8846A: NO change (it is fixed to the 5560A, as are the Truevolt 34460A-70A and the 34401A; the 5560A sheet is untouched).
- Affected: every report whose Uncertainty / TUR comes from the 5522A: the handheld engine (110-series, 77 IV, 170-series, 87V, 287/289, MM325; default calibrator 5560A; affected when the technician picks the 5522A), `dacq_verification` (34970A / 34972A),
  `agilent34410a_verification`, `keithley2000_verification` and `fluke8808a_verification` (default calibrator 5522A) when the 5522A is selected, and the Datasheet Builder match (`datasheet_builder_match`: its TUR / uncertainty for a 5522A assignment; the automatic choice by TUR can move toward the 5522A where the two were close: spot checks 5522A new vs 5560A printed uncertainty: DC 5 V 63 uV vs 44 uV, AC 1 V 1 kHz 170 uV vs 130 uV, DC 100 mA 32 uV vs 9.8 uV (5560A still ahead), 1 kohm 0.026 vs 0.026 (now a tie: the lab default order, 5560A first, decides)).
  The scope / generator engines (TDS2000C, MDO4000C, WaveSurfer, 33210A-33250A) use TAR or their own CMC-only budgets: unchanged by that branch (the later branch `uncertainty-engines` moved the MDO4000C, WaveSurfer and 33210A-33250A points that have a sheet onto the budget; the 5790B sheet's 99 % divisor 2.576 is part of it). A report is never below the A2LA CMC either way.
- Reports already issued are NOT changed: `report_snapshots` stores the printed rows (strings, display-scaled, with Uncertainty / TAR / TUR) and `report_snapshot.regenerate` prints them again from the stored record without recomputing (tested: `tests/test_standard_confidence.py`
  `ReprintKeepsIssuedUncertainty`, a reprint after the change is page-identical to the original). An event without a snapshot is rebuilt from history rows with Uncertainty / TAR / TUR BLANK (never recomputed). Only newly generated reports carry the new numbers (a run exported again from a procedure window is a new report).
- Tests: existing tests that embed 5522A uncertainties were updated and reviewed one by one: `test_specmanager` (divisor, wizard example 1.5 uV / 3.0 uV -> 1.1646 uV / 2.33 uV, audit), `test_standard_5520a` (same-divisor test: the 5520A and 5522A budgets are now equal as stored),
  `test_beta_temp_uncertainty` (5522A Type K budgets, TAR / TUR, PDF rows). Golden transcripts did not change. New `tests/test_standard_confidence.py`.
- UNVERIFIED / to confirm: (a) the 5522A level was read from the rendered page image of printed p. 1-8 (single "99 %" line); no k factor is printed, 2.576 is the normal-distribution value for 99 % (the 5560A / 5790B print k = 2.58, the table uses 2.576 as the code always did);
  (b) the lab's own 5522A SpecTrack sheet says [Not Specified]: its numbers are the 5522A manual's (four 1-year rows deliberately more conservative, sheet note 8), so they are 99 % numbers; (c) 8563E / 96270A / 8902A keep divisor 2 for want of a printed level; (d) the 5790B /AF 50 MHz row (95 %) shares the 5790B's 2.576.
## Oscilloscope and generator engines on the uncertainty budget (branch `uncertainty-engines`, part A; simulated bench, stub tests and a real Tk under Xvfb only; never run on hardware)
Owner ('yes please' to the offer): move the engines that printed the A2LA scope CMC ALONE (MDO4000C, 33210A / 33220A / 33250A, WaveSurfer WS4104HD) onto the budget the multimeter engines use: u(standard) = published accuracy / divisor (+ resolution / sqrt 12 when the sheet has one), EMU = 2 * RSS, reported = `ceil_sig(max(budget, CMC))`. Only points with a matching standards sheet and a well defined budget; the rest stay CMC-only and are listed. Reports already issued are not changed (snapshots). Part B (frequency steps of the handhelds) is a separate commit, see 'Handheld frequency steps print no uncertainty' below.

SURVEY (done before any edit; `report_pdf.scope()` = `specs/a2la_1888_03_cmc.json`; sheets = `specs/standards/*.json`; `tartur.NATURAL_UNITS` guard = a tolerance in one unit is never divided by an uncertainty in another):
| engine | points that carried an uncertainty (CMC-only) | standard that actually applies the stimulus / measures | sheet in specs/standards? | NATURAL_UNITS guard | decision |
| MDO4000C `OFFSET` (64 listed), `DVMDC` (36) | scope 'oscilloscope DC' line, absolute V | 5560A SCOPE DCV (the 5560A sources DC) | 5560A 'Voltage, DC' (95 %, divisor 2) | not used by this engine: `tartur.from_values` compares the unit strings (V / V) | CONVERT |
| MDO4000C `GAIN` (108) | the same line at both levels, quadrature, % of the expected difference | 5560A SCOPE DCV at vneg and vpos | the same DC rows | % tolerance vs % uncertainty; TAR blank (spec in V) | CONVERT (each level, then RSS as before) |
| MDO4000C `IMP` (25) | 'Resistance - Measure' CMC | the 5560A MEASURES the input resistance | no (the 5560A sheet has no resistance INPUT row) | ohm / ohm | CMC-only |
