| MDO4000C `BW` (224) | scope level-sine flatness CMC (dB -> ratio) | 5560A leveled sine head | no flatness / leveled-sine rows on the 5560A sheet (only the 5522A sheet has them) | ratio | CMC-only |
| MDO4000C `DELTA` 464, `DVMAC` 20, `DVMFREQ` 24, `ABSAMP`, `PHASE`, `XTALK`, `IMD`, `TPAFUNC`, `REFFREQ`, `AFG*` | N/A today (timing, jitter, RF level / phase, AFG: no CMC, `mdo4000c_reference_cmc.json` all null) | 5560A time mark / 96270A / 33220A / reference meters | no sheet for a time mark; the 96270A sheet is RF level, not these quantities | - | stay N/A (never invented) |
| MDO4000C `NOISE`, `BAL`, `DANL`, `SPUR`, `TRIGOUT`, `DIGITAL`, `SELFTEST`, `SPC` | N/A (no stimulus, or pass / fail) | - | - | - | stay N/A |
| WaveSurfer `dcv` (80), `offset` (16), `trigger` (24) | scope 'oscilloscope DC' line, 1 MOhm, absolute V (trigger: the larger of the two bracket steps) | 5560A SCOPE DCV | 5560A 'Voltage, DC' | V / V (TAR only at the sourced level) | CONVERT |
| WaveSurfer `impedance` (28), `bandwidth` (24) | 'Resistance - Measure' CMC; level-sine flatness CMC (dB) | 5560A measures / leveled sine | no (as for the MDO) | ohm; dB | CMC-only |
| WaveSurfer `timebase` (1), `noise` | blank | - | - | - | stay blank |
| 33210A / 33220A / 33250A `ACAMP` sine (6 each), `LFFLAT` sine (3 each) | scope 'AC Voltage - Measure' CMC at the nominal and frequency | Fluke 5790B MEASURES the generator (3458A in the manual; this program uses the 5790B) | 5790B 'Voltage, AC [Sinewave, Absolute]', direction INPUT (99 %, divisor 2.576) | V / V | CONVERT (needs `direction='input'`, `basis='measure'`) |
| 33210A / 33220A / 33250A `ACAMP` square (1 each) | the same Measure CMC | 5790B | the 5790B sinewave rows do not describe a square wave (the existing TAR rule, same reason) | V / V | CMC-only |
| 33210A / 33220A / 33250A `TIMEBASE` (1 each) | 'Frequency - Measure' CMC | 53131A / 53132A counter (or typed) | NO sheet (`agilent_53131a_53132a_NOTE.txt`: the Programming Guide has no accuracy table) | Hz / Hz | CMC-only |
| 33210A / 33220A / 33250A `FLAT` (27 / 42 / 42), `SELFTEST` | blank (no CMC in dB) | power meter (default method) or the 5790B wideband input | the 5790B wideband flatness rows exist, but the default method is a power meter (no sheet) and there is no dB CMC | dB | stay blank |
Not in the request, untouched: the TDS2000C engine (`tds2000c_verification.py`, seeded verified) has the same CMC-only pattern for its 5560A DC gain points; it is a candidate for the same change on request.

CHANGE (A):
- `uncertainty_budget.py` (shared, in no procedure fingerprint): `build(..., direction='output', basis=None, cmc=None)` (defaults = the old call, so every multimeter engine is byte for byte as before): `direction='input'` looks the standard up as a MEASURING standard (the 5790B), `basis='measure'` checks it against the lab's Measure CMC, `cmc=` replaces the scope lookup (the oscilloscope DC lines are not in the DMM tables); `Budget.value` = the larger of EMU and CMC, unrounded; new `osc_dc(volts, impedance, standard, scope)` = the DC budget for a level a calibrator applies to a scope input (None where the lab's oscilloscope DC line has no entry: nothing is extrapolated; a missing sheet or a raising lookup falls back to the CMC alone).
- `mdo4000c_verification.py`: `uncertainty_parts` for OFFSET / DVMDC / GAIN go through `osc_dc` (`CAL_MODEL = '5560A'`); IMP, BW and the reference-file kinds are untouched; the report note names the budget.
- `fgen_verification.py`: `measure_budget(p)` (5790B, sine ACAMP / LFFLAT only; `STD_MODEL = '5790B'`) and `uncertainty_value` uses it; source text 'budget'; square wave, time base, FLAT as before; the editable `fgen_332xx_reference_cmc.json` value is still floored by the larger of the budget and the CMC.
- `Procedures/WaveSurfer_WS4104HD.py`: `_dc_budget_value` for dcv / offset / trigger; the PDF footer note passes '5560A' (the footer text is not drawn by the report layer today, `footer_note` is ignored there).
- No instrument command, limit, pass / fail rule, safety prompt or driver was touched. TAR / TUR code is unchanged (TUR follows the printed value, TAR is the unscaled specification).
- Tests: new `tests/test_scope_gen_budget.py` (hand-worked values: 5560A 1 V = 7.4 uV and 5 V = 41 uV, 5790B 20 mV at 1 kHz = 3.5 uV -> EMU 2.7174 uV, oscilloscope DC CMC 640 uV at 1 V and 3.04 mV at 5 V, GAIN quadrature 0.81239 % -> 0.82 %; the budget path is proved by making it dominate (divisor patched to 0.001); CMC-only / blank kinds unchanged; WaveSurfer rows, footer and demo flag through the real `write_certificate_pdf`; whole-export PDFs for all three engines through `tests/tartur_scopes_harness.py`).

RESULT, before / after (origin/main vs this branch, every point of every engine run through the same code, `uncertainty_text` / `point_uncertainty` / `scope_uncertainty` and the TAR / TUR strings): NOTHING CHANGED. 0 of 1307 MDO points, 0 of 39 / 54 / 54 generator points, 0 of 173 WaveSurfer points differ in the Uncertainty, TAR or TUR text. Reason: the reported value is the larger of budget and CMC, and the CMC is the larger everywhere today.
| engine | point | CMC (floor) | budget EMU | budget / CMC | printed before | printed after |
| MDO | OFFSET +0.9 V into 50 ohm | 2.4 mV | 6.8 uV | 0.3 % | 2.4 mV | 2.4 mV |
| MDO | OFFSET +5 V into 50 ohm | 14 mV | 41 uV | 0.3 % | 14 mV | 14 mV |
| MDO | OFFSET +99.5 V into 1 MOhm | 60 mV | 950 uV | 1.6 % | 60 mV | 60 mV |
| MDO | DVM DC -5 V | 3.1 mV | 41 uV | 1.3 % | 3.1 mV | 3.1 mV |
| MDO | DVM DC -1 V | 640 uV | 7.4 uV | 1.2 % | 640 uV | 640 uV |
| MDO | GAIN +/-4.5 mV into 50 ohm (id 124, % of 9 mV) | 0.81239 % (51.7 uV per level, quadrature) | 0.8 uV per level | 1.5 % | 0.82 % | 0.82 % |
| WaveSurfer | DC gain 6 mV | 44 uV | 860 nV | 2.0 % | 44 uV | 44 uV |
| WaveSurfer | DC gain 3 V | 1.9 mV | 29 uV | 1.6 % | 1.9 mV | 1.9 mV |
| WaveSurfer | Offset +10 V | 6.1 mV | 72 uV | 1.2 % | 6.1 mV | 6.1 mV |
| WaveSurfer | Trigger level 0 mV (steps +/-20 mV) | 52 uV | 990 nV | 1.9 % | 52 uV | 52 uV |
| 33220A | ACAMP sine 20 mV @ 1 kHz | 2.9 uV | 2.8 uV | 94 % | 2.9 uV | 2.9 uV |
| 33220A | ACAMP sine 670 mV @ 1 kHz | 20 uV | 19 uV | 92 % | 20 uV | 20 uV |
| 33220A | ACAMP sine 7 V @ 1 kHz | 170 uV | 150 uV | 87 % | 170 uV | 170 uV |
| 33220A | LFFLAT sine 670 mV @ 100 kHz | 120 uV | 97 uV | 83 % | 120 uV | 120 uV |
| 33220A | LFFLAT sine 2 V @ 100 kHz | 340 uV | 250 uV | 73 % | 340 uV | 340 uV |
| 33220A | LFFLAT sine 7 V @ 100 kHz | 1.2 mV | 1.1 mV | 87 % | 1.2 mV | 1.2 mV |
So the change is structural today: the DC budgets are 0.3 % to 2.7 % of the oscilloscope DC CMC and cannot matter, but the 5790B budgets are 73 % to 94 % of the AC Measure CMC and WILL decide the printed value as soon as a number moves: the 5790B sheet is stored at 99 % (divisor 2.576, 'Confidence level rule'); at divisor 2 the printed values would be 3.5 uV, 6.1 uV, 9.1 uV, 24 uV, 48 uV, 190 uV (ACAMP 20 mV ... 7 V) and 130 uV, 340 uV, 1.4 mV (LFFLAT): 7 of 9 rise. A resolution term is not in any of these budgets (neither the 5560A nor the 5790B sheet records a resolution; the budget notes say so) and would add to the 5790B figures (the Standards Specs tab can record one).

VERIFIED STATUS (fingerprints worked out with `verified.Registry.file_components` for every sidecar procedure, origin/main vs this branch, 40 procedures; user-built `ds:` procedures use `dsb_runtime.py`, untouched): 5 procedures change fingerprint and show 'Changed since verification' if they were verified:
- `WaveSurfer_WS4104HD.py` (SEEDED VERIFIED, so it flips): its launcher `Procedures/WaveSurfer_WS4104HD.py` (the whole engine is that file). The printed numbers did not change.
- `Tektronix_MDO4000C_Verification.py`: `mdo4000c_verification.py`.
- `Agilent_33220A_Verification.py`, `Agilent_33250A_Verification.py`, `Keysight_33210A_Verification.py`: `fgen_verification.py`.
- Not flipped: TDS2000C and Fluke 117 (the other two seeded), every multimeter procedure (`uncertainty_budget.py`, `report_pdf.py`, `tartur.py` are in no fingerprint).
- The owner's own registry is not visible here: MDO and the three generators flip only if they were marked verified there.

UNVERIFIED / to confirm: (a) that the 5560A normal-output DC voltage specification is the right budget for the 5560A SCOPE-mode DC output (the sheet has no scope-mode DC row; the existing TAR already uses the same row); it is far below the CMC either way; (b) the 5790B sheet divisor 2.576 (99 %) for a lab that reports at 95 %: see above, this alone decides 7 of 9 generator values; (c) no resolution term for the 5560A / 5790B (not on the sheets); (d) the oscilloscope DC CMC lines (0.06 % + 40 uV at 1 MOhm, 0.26 % + 40 uV at 50 ohm up to 6.6 V) are the existing scope file; above 6.6 V into 50 ohm the MDO prints N/A as before; (e) the budget does not include the unit under test (resolution, repeatability), as before.
Run `python -m unittest tests.test_scope_gen_budget`.

## Frequency points use the lab 'Frequency - Measure' CMC (branch `uncertainty-engines`, frequency work; replaces the first part-B approach, which blanked the cells)
Owner: "All frequency interfaces are tied to Frequency Measure and the budget was calculated using the error of the GPS. Everything that accepts an external reference is on it." So the A2LA line 'Frequency - Measure' (`specs/a2la_1888_03_cmc.json`, kind FREQ, 10 Hz to 26.5 GHz, 7.1e-12 of the frequency) is the uncertainty of every frequency / time base point. It is already the expanded figure: NO calibrator-sheet divisor, no resolution term, nothing combined with it; the printed value is `ceil_sig(7.1e-12 x f)` to 2 s.f. with its SI prefix (nHz, uHz ...).
- Handheld (Fluke 77 IV 2-17; 110-series 3-17 for 115 / 116 / 117; 87V 1-11 .. 1-14; 287 / 289 4-24): the steps source AC volts and read a frequency. New optional spec key `step.unc_kind: "FREQ"` (specs/README.txt). `handheld_dmm.py` (certificate_row, certificate_fields; the smallest edit, about 8 lines) then asks `report_pdf.point_uncertainty` for kind FREQ at the APPLIED FREQUENCY with no standard, i.e. the CMC alone, instead of the calibrator's voltage budget. The limits, commands, pass/fail and the source of the point are unchanged. Printed: 77 IV 2-17 and 110 3-17 (50 kHz) 360 nHz; 87V 1-11 / 1-13 / 1-14 (99.95 kHz) 710 nHz; 87V 1-12 (199.5 kHz) 1.5 uHz; 287 / 289 4-24 (45 Hz) 320 pHz (was '2.9 mHz' etc.: the voltage uncertainty with a Hz unit).
- TUR is computed against that uncertainty with Hz natural units (tolerance is in Hz, so the guard passes): >1000:1 at every one of these steps. TAR is BLANK: no calibrator frequency accuracy is used (the frequency CMC stands alone), so there is no accuracy to divide by. Flagged, not invented.
- Fluke 170 series (175 / 177 / 179; steps 2-16..2-19, 2-25, 2-26, 3-16..3-19, 3-25 in the spec; `build_170_spec.py` hzread rows): were `no_uncertainty` (blank); now `unc_kind: FREQ` like the others (owner decision): 45 Hz 320 pHz, 900 Hz 6.4 nHz, 9 kHz 64 nHz, 50 kHz 360 nHz; TUR >1000:1, TAR blank. Rows with no source (87V 1-15, 287 4-25) stay blank.
- Bench DMM engines with FREQ points (an AC volts output at the test frequency, read by the DMM counter): 34410A / 34411A (2), Truevolt 34460A / 34461A / 34465A / 34470A (2 each), 34970A / 34972A DACQ (2 each), 8808A (6). The Keithley 2000 spec has no FREQ point. They printed the calibrator's own frequency accuracy (a 5522A budget, e.g. 5.8 Hz at 50 kHz, TAR 559.9:1) combined with the CMC. Now the Frequency - Measure CMC ALONE (50 kHz: 360 nHz; 10 Hz: 71 pHz), whatever the calibrator; TUR against it, TAR blank. Done in two lines in `report_pdf.point_uncertainty` and `tartur._compute` (kind FREQ ignores `standard`), NOT in the engine files: no verification fingerprint moves and the stop-on-surprise hooks in truevolt / dacq / 34410A are not touched. Test: `tests/test_bench_freq_cmc.py`. Any future engine that builds a FREQ point through those two functions gets it.
- Scope / generator engines surveyed for frequency / time base points (what accepts the 10 MHz reference):
  - 33210A / 33220A / 33250A TIMEBASE: ALREADY the Frequency - Measure CMC (source 'scope'); unchanged, pinned by a test. (The counter reference is the 'External 10 MHz (GPS)' default; with 'Internal timebase' selected the lab GPS statement does not hold: UNVERIFIED, no code change.)
  - WaveSurfer WS4104HD Time base (5560A scope marker, error in Hz): was blank, now the CMC at the marker frequency (10 us -> 100 kHz -> 710 nHz) and a TUR of >1000:1 against +-0.350 Hz (`scope_uncertainty_value('timebase')`, `_TOL_KEYS`). Assumption: the 5560A marker is locked to the GPS 10 MHz in use. UNVERIFIED on the bench.
  - MDO4000C: DVM frequency (DVMFREQ, 24 points, 5560A frequency into the DVM counter) and AFG frequency (AFGFREQ, 4 points, AFG read on the lab counter; stim 'none' before, which blanked it) now print the CMC (10 Hz -> 71 pHz; 10 kHz -> 71 nHz); an editable reference-file value is still used when it is larger. Unchanged: REFFREQ and DELTA (time intervals in seconds, a different quantity: no time-interval CMC in the scope), AFGAMP / AFGOFF, RF points.
  - Stay CMC-only / blank (not frequency quantities): rise time, jitter, delay / skew, phase noise.
- Tests: `tests/test_freq_steps_cmc.py` (handheld), `tests/test_freq_cmc_scope_gen.py` (MDO4000C, WaveSurfer, generators); `tests/test_tartur_wiring_dmm.py` allows exactly the frequency-row difference on the 115 / 87V / 287 PDFs.

## Run queue sharing rules (Mike, 2026-10-22)
Resistance / continuity (kind OHM), capacitance (CAP) and thermocouple simulation (TEMP) are NOT shared: one meter at a time (reason: SOURCE_REASON). Points set by hand with no calibrator output (open input) ARE shareable. The high-voltage confirmation is asked once per hook-up group in a shared unit (still once per point outside a queue). Cap stays 8 meters; no queue in the Datasheet Builder.

## Datasheet Builder window, layout B (branch `dsb-layout-b`; viewed on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb at 1400x900 and 1100x700, light and dark; never seen on Windows)
Mike chose mockup B. The bottom entry strip and the right tool column are replaced by a right-hand INSPECTOR for the selected row (`datasheet_builder_ui.Inspector`, old name `SpecPanel`
kept as an alias; attribute `GridEditorWindow.panel_box`, same `.types` / `.v` / `.b` / `.form()` / `.load()` API). 336 px wide, 290 px when the window is under 1250 px, scrolls inside, hides to a
slim rail ('Hide >' / '<' / Ctrl+I). Presentation only: `GridModel`, tolerance maths, pass/fail, print/PDF output and saved files are unchanged (`calc_row`, `copy_tolerance`, `recalc_all`, `set_remark`, `set_run` are the same calls).
- Where every control went (old -> new):
  - Banner pills 'saved' / warnings -> same, in a one-line title strip; the warnings pill is clickable (opens the warnings list; tooltip = `problem_summary()`).
  - Make / Model / Title / Tolerances for -> sheet bar (labels above the boxes; 'Tolerances for' only when the sheet has more than one period, as before).
  - Lock Status (tool column) -> sheet bar. Undo / Redo -> sheet bar (small). Standards... / More -> sheet bar. Save (Ctrl+S) -> sheet bar, the one accent button. Print / Preview -> 'Preview / Print' (Ctrl+P), sheet bar.
  - Edit Cell bar -> spans the grid only; the symbol buttons (± ° µ ¹ ² ³ · Units, tool column before) sit at its right. They no longer take focus, so they insert into the box you are typing in (Edit Cell if none).
  - 'Calculate' (Edit Cell bar) -> removed (duplicate of Calc). 'Calc' -> Tolerance section, accent button; Enter in any inspector box = Calc. 'Recalc' -> 'Recalc all', under the grid. 'Copy tolerance to rows below' -> Tolerance section.
  - 'Warnings' text + 'Show list' (tool column) -> 'Show list' under the grid + the pill. The issue line and the message line under the grid -> ONE status line at the bottom (message of the last action, else the selected row's problem, else the row hint). `win.note` / `win.issue` StringVars still hold the texts.
  - 'This row tests' -> Test point section (first box). Nominal Value, Units of Measure ('Units') -> Test point. Frequency, Signal level, Beeper should be -> Test point, only on the rows that use them.
  - 'Select Spec Types' check boxes -> five chips (% of reading, % of range, Floor, ± dB, Calculator); only the boxes for ticked chips show. '% of I.V.' -> '% of reading', '% of F.S.' -> '% of range', 'Calculator: type the accuracy statement' -> 'Accuracy statement, as the manual prints it'. Technical names are in the tooltips.
  - Multiple Test Points + Lo to High / n % / n Units / n ppm + High + n -> 'Several rows at once' (closed; header shows ON when ticked; the spacing / High / n boxes show only when ticked).
  - Asymmetrical Tol + Operand -> 'Asymmetrical tolerance' (closed; 'Different tolerance above and below' + 'Side being typed'; opens by itself on a row that has one).
  - Display Decs. / Meas Res / Auto Res -> 'Resolution and decimals' ('Decimals shown' / 'Resolution' / 'Work out from the decimals'). Test Point Definitions -> 'Rules for this section' (a section heading row shows only this section).
  - Row level remark, Run this row -> 'Row options'. Ins. Header / Page Break / Delete row, the '...' unit picker, the Manager's Procedure changes button: still removed.
- On-screen grid shows only STEP NUM, FUNCTION TESTED, NOMINAL VALUE, CALIBRATION TOLERANCE (now LOW LIMIT and HIGH LIMIT, five columns: see 'Datasheet Builder: Low / High limits') (`VIEW_COLUMNS`, Mike: 'not needed in this view'). VIEW ONLY: `GRID_COLUMNS` (the printed template, 7 columns) is unchanged and `print_view()` / the PDF still print AS FOUND / AS LEFT / Out of Tol blank; row dicts still carry them; `COL_KEYS` / `EDIT_COLS` / navigation / `GridGeometry` use the four view columns. Row height 28 px, heading wraps instead of truncating ('STEP NUM').
- Decisions Mike did not answer (follow the mockup; reverse by editing `datasheet_builder_ui.py`): the duplicate Calculate button and the duplicate issue line were dropped; a single warnings pill; the function box ('This row tests') stays in Test point (the mockup left it out but a blank row needs it); the row's problem now shows in the status line; the section note 'Auto Res' is only in the tooltip; the inspector starts open and hides on request. (Superseded by the follow-ups below: it now collapses by itself under 1150 px.)
- Run / Try in demo (sheet bar): `GridEditorWindow.run_sheet(demo)` refuses a sheet with errors (opens the warnings list), asks to save when dirty / unsaved, then `dsb_manager.launch_process` (new helper; same command line as the Manager's `_launch`: `User_Datasheet.py --spec <spec> [--demo]`, cwd Procedures/). The runtime is untouched; demo = `--demo` only (the Manager's 'Fast demo' box is not offered). Tooltips and the status line give `GridModel.automation_line()` (matcher summary: runs automatically / unverified commands / set by hand / not covered).
- Handheld template: New... asks 'Start from a handheld template' (default off = blank, as before); More > 'Add handheld functions...' for an existing sheet. `GridModel.add_handheld_functions()` adds an empty section for DCV, ACV, DCI, ACI, OHM2, CONT, CAP, FREQ, TEMP, DIODE from the builder's own catalogue (`_new_section`); ranges come with the rows typed. NOT built: 'Copy from an existing handheld procedure': the library specs (`specs/fluke_*_pv.json`) carry no `dsb` datasheet, and `datasheet_from_spec` only reads user-built specs, so there is no safe existing path (follow-up).
- Tests: tests/test_dsb_grid_model.py (view columns, print still 7, template, automation), tests/test_dsb_ui_widgets.py (inspector, status line, Run / demo, template, More item; tests/_tk_stub.py now answers winfo_req* with numbers), tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_dsb_layout (1400x900 and 1100x700: nothing clipped, rows visible, Enter applies, chips, hide / show). Screenshots: scratchpad `dsb_b/shots/` (see the report).
- Unverified: Windows fonts / DPI (the Linux fallback font is wider than Segoe UI, so the sheet bar wraps to two lines sooner here than it should there); the tooltips on the Run buttons run the standards matcher when hovered.

## Datasheet Builder follow-ups (branch `dsb-followups`; Mike's three answers; presentation / validation only; viewed on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb at 1400x900 and 1000x700, light and dark; never seen on Windows)
- Empty sections are warnings. `datasheet_builder.validate`: a section with NO points now gives `no_ranges` and `no_spec` as 'warn' (they were errors; they stay errors when the section has points), next to the existing `no_points` warning. Every other error is unchanged and still blocks Save as runnable / Run / Try in demo.
  NEW blocking error `no_test_rows` ("The datasheet has no test rows yet"): a sheet whose sections are ALL empty (a fresh template) cannot be saved as runnable (it still saves as a draft), because a run of zero points is nothing. This is my addition, not Mike's wording: reverse by deleting the `no_test_rows` check in `validate` if an empty template should save as runnable.
  Runtime / export need no change (verified): `export_spec` only walks points, so an empty section writes no step; `dsb_runtime` and the report only ever see steps, so no empty section is run, printed or numbered (`tests/test_dsb_runtime_bench.py::EmptySectionRun`: simulated bench, PDF text has no empty-section name).
  The blank printout (Preview / Print, `print_view`, the PDF of the blank sheet) still prints each section heading, empty or not, so a template can be filled in by hand: old behaviour kept, report layout untouched.
- Warnings list (`GridModel.problems` / `problem_rows` / `problem_counts`): the redundant per-section `no_ranges` / `no_spec` warnings are dropped (one message per empty section: `no_points`, now worded 'no test rows yet (skipped when the sheet runs)'). With `EMPTY_GROUP_FROM` (2) or more empty sections the list shows ONE entry ('9 sections have no test rows yet and are skipped when the sheet runs: ...' - names when 4 or fewer, else the first three), 'Go to the row' goes to the first one, and the pill / summary count it as ONE warning. A single empty section is listed on its own. Each empty section's heading row still shows its own message in the status line.
- Run on real instruments asks first: `GridEditorWindow.confirm_real_run()` -> 'Run <title> on real instruments? The calibrator will be driven from this program.' Yes / No, default No (`default='no'`, warning icon). Order in `run_sheet(False)`: problems block first (no question), then this question, then the existing save-first question, then the launch. 'Try in demo' (`run_sheet(True)`) asks nothing about real instruments. Answering No changes nothing (no save, no launch).
- Inspector auto-collapse: `datasheet_builder_ui_model.INSPECTOR_COLLAPSE_BELOW = 1150` (96-dpi px, scaled by `ui.px`) and the pure `inspector_should_be_open(width, below, manual, is_open)`. `GridEditorWindow._window_size` (on <Configure>, so also the first time the window is shown) collapses the inspector to the slim rail under 1150 px and opens it again at 1150 or more, never moving focus. `toggle_inspector` ('Hide >', the rail '<' button, Ctrl+I) sets `_insp_manual`: from then on the width no longer opens or closes it for the rest of that window's session. 290 px / 336 px width choice (`NARROW_BELOW` 1250) is unchanged. Threshold from the layout study: at 1150 the grid is about 1150 - 290 - margins; below that it drops under ~8 rows / cramped columns.
- Not built (Mike said no): 'Copy from an existing handheld procedure'.
- Tests: tests/test_dsb_grid_model.py (EmptySectionTests, InspectorAutoTests), tests/test_dsb_ui_widgets.py (real-run question, auto-collapse and manual stick, template sheet saves and runs with warnings only), tests/test_dsb_runtime_bench.py (EmptySectionRun), tests/real_tk_checks.py (`checks_dsb_layout` now 1400x900 open, 1200x760 open narrow, 1000x700 collapsed; `checks_dsb_auto_collapse` resize 1400 -> 1000 -> 1400, Ctrl+I, hand choice sticks, template sheet warnings list; screenshots to `$DSB_SHOTS`).
- Unverified: the real-run Yes / No box is the native Tk message box (not screenshotted; the stub tests check the text, title, icon and default); Windows fonts / DPI; the 1150 threshold at other DPI scalings (it is multiplied by `ui.px`).

## Datasheet Builder workflow test (branch `dsb-workflow-test`; the technician workflow doc followed literally on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb; never seen on Windows)
- What was done: the Claude Doc 'Datasheet Creator: technician workflow (handheld multimeters)' was followed step by step in the REAL windows (Manager > Datasheet Builder > New... with the handheld template ticked, header, remove sections, Ranges, rows, Calc, Copy tolerance, Recalc all, Show list, Preview, Standards, Save, Lock, Try in demo started as its own process, then the same spec run in demo in-process with the real reading / function windows driven, Export Report). The sheet is a MOCK ('MOCK Acme Mock 8000', invented numbers, 14 points: DC V, AC V, DC mA, 2-wire ohms, simulated temperature); nothing comes from a manual. No real-instrument Run was used.
- Headless regression: `tests/test_dsb_mock_workflow.py` builds the same mock sheet through the model calls the window makes, saves it, runs it in demo on the simulated bench and exports the PDF (asset number-date name, DEMO marking, nothing recorded in Asset History; the same run without demo does record). It also pins what the workflow doc says: rows are sorted by range then frequency, the cursor jumps to the next section's empty row after a first row, Lock Status is not stored in the saved sheet, a forgotten AC frequency is a blocking error.
- One small fix found by it (presentation only): the Datasheet Builder detail windows (Ranges / Specification table / Standards / Preview) are made at least as tall as their content asks (`datasheet_builder_ui.grow_to_fit`; their button row was partly cut off under Xvfb's fixed font). Real-Tk check: `checks_dsb_dialogs_fit` in `tests/real_tk_checks.py`.
- FIXED (owner approved): the page-footer title of a DEMO report (`report_pdf` `_furniture`, left text 'Instrument ID: ... | <title>') printed over the centred red 'DEMO DATA - NOT CALIBRATION EVIDENCE' when long. In demo mode only, `report_pdf.demo_footer_left` now cuts the left text with '...' so it ends before the red line; non-demo PDFs are byte-identical. This changed the demo PDF bytes of the Fluke 115 / 87V / 287 / Klein MM325 library procedures (title cut, e.g. 'Fluke 115 Performance Ve...'); `tests/test_tartur_wiring_dmm.py::test_off_identical_to_baseline` now compares those four ignoring the footer-left text (body words/rows must still match the baseline). `tests/test_demo_footer.py` covers the geometry.
- Behaviour NOT changed, worth knowing: the Lock Status box is a per-window safeguard (a reopened sheet is in Edit Mode; the real lock is 'Mark verified on hardware...' in the Procedures tab); a demo run saves its progress under the same file name as a real run on that asset ID (use a throw-away ID in demo); the report heading is the datasheet's title, not 'Calibration Data'; typed readings are padded to the decimals of the LIMITS (not the range resolution); the list's Automation column says 'all N points run by the suite' even when some commands are unverified (the Standards page and the Run / Try in demo tooltips do say so) [CHANGED by 'Two-way datasheet rules', rule 2: the column now says how many points use unverified commands].
- Unverified: the temperature (thermocouple simulation) commands on the 5560A / 5522A; everything on Windows; the native Tk dialogs (message boxes, file dialogs) were answered by script / patch, not looked at.

## Two-way datasheet rules (Datasheet Builder)
Approved by Mike ('lets start with these') for building 2-way AUTOMATED datasheet sheets (a sheet whose points the suite sources and the technician reads, or the standards read). They sit on top of the project standing rules (Standing rules above: display-only SI scaling; the schema of `calibration_procedures.sqlite3` never changes, new data goes in `lab_registry.sqlite3`; instrument commands, limits, pass / fail and safety prompts are not changed unless asked; never write passwords to logs; writes are set / read back / compare and never retried; outputs go to standby / off after each point; flag anything unverified; no cover sheets, no standards-due tracking; reports are the PDF 'Calibration Data' named asset#-date; a demo never produces real-looking evidence). Rules 1-5 are BUILD-TIME (branch `dsb-rules-validation`, this section); rules 6-13 are RUN-TIME (branch `dsb-runtime-rules`); rule 14 is the Builder's limits display (branch `dsb-low-high-limits`). 'enforced by:' says where the code or the test is; 'guideline' = a working rule with no code check.
1. Calibrator coverage. Every sourced point must be inside the chosen calibrator's range and standards sheet; otherwise the Builder says so and offers manual entry for that point (extends 'Check which standards can do this').
   enforced by: `datasheet_builder_rules.coverage_findings` (the standards matcher, `datasheet_builder_match` / `calibrator_fit` rules; a point NO standard covers = warning with 'Apply the fix' = `manual_entry_for`; a point the author ASSIGNED to a standard that cannot make it = error) and `dry_findings` (a point that will not run on its assigned standard, `dsb_runtime.limit_problem`); tests `Rule1Coverage`.
2. Unverified commands block the real run. A point may use only commands with a manual page reference and the verified flag; anything else appears in a pre-run list and the operator must accept it before real instruments run. The Manager list's Automation column no longer says 'all N points run by the suite' when some commands are unverified.
   enforced by: `datasheet_builder_rules.unverified_uses` (every command the dry run of rule 4 recorded whose `cmdreg.Cmd` is not `verified` or has no `ref`, or is not in the registry), `dsb_prerun.accepted` (called by `dsb_runtime.main` before anything is opened, REAL runs only; declining opens nothing; the Builder's 'Run on real instruments' question lists the same commands and passes `--accept-unverified <fingerprint>` so the list is not asked twice) and `DatasheetList.automation_text`; tests `Rule2Unverified`, `PreRunAcceptance`, `ListColumn`. Build time the findings are warnings.
3. Units and kinds are checked. An unknown unit, or a unit that does not fit the function, is never converted silently; it becomes a flagged pass / fail point (as the Mudcats import does).
   enforced by: `datasheet_builder_rules.unit_findings` (range labels, point labels, generator amplitude unit, printed accuracy terms: error = the sheet is saved as a draft) and `make_check_row` (the fix: moves the row to a flagged pass / fail section, original text kept); tests `Rule3Units` (incl. the Mudcats import).
4. Dry run on the simulated bench. 'Try in demo' must run every point against the simulated instruments; a sheet cannot be marked 'ready' if a command is not supported by the simulated driver.
   enforced by: `datasheet_builder_rules.dry_run` (every enabled point through the real `dsb_runtime.DsbIO` + drivers on `drivers/simulated*.py`, headless; a command the simulated driver does not know, or a -1xx command error, is an error and `datasheet_builder.save` writes a draft; any other stop is a warning); the Sheet check window and `GridEditorWindow.run_sheet` (errors stop Run and Try in demo); tests `Rule4DryRun`. NOT enforced: that the demo PROCESS itself reaches the 5790B / counters (see Virtual bench: those are done by hand in demo); that is a run-time change.
5. Uncertainty source is shown per point, and no reading that should have an uncertainty is published without one. Each point shows which standards sheet its uncertainty comes from; a point with no source stays blank and is called out on the sheet, never shown as zero. Owner rule: 'If a reading is supposed to have an uncertainty and it isn't shown on the datasheet, it needs to be addressed before the datasheet publishes.' Publishing = `datasheet_builder.save` making the sheet 'ready' (runnable). 'Should have' = every kind the lab CMC table `specs/a2la_1888_03_cmc.json` covers (DCV, DCI, OHM, CAP, ACI, ACV, FREQ and the oscilloscope dc / flatness / amplitude sections), mapped to Builder kinds by `datasheet_builder_rules.CMC_KIND_MAP` (DCV, DCI, OHM2, OHM4, CAP, ACV, ACI, FREQ, PERIOD, GEN_FREQ, SCOPE_TIMEBASE, SCOPE_GAIN, SCOPE_OFFSET, GEN_OFFSET, SCOPE_BW, GEN_FLAT, GEN_AMPL) plus TEMP in degC (the handheld procedures print one); the set is read from the file (`should_have_kinds`) and a test fails if the table has a kind the map does not know. CAN BE RESOLVED TODAY (a standards sheet gives a value; `RESOLVABLE_KINDS`): DCV, ACV, DCI, ACI, OHM2, OHM4, CAP, TEMP. ONLY BY WAIVER (the suite has no uncertainty calculation in the Builder / DSB report for them yet): FREQ, PERIOD, GEN_FREQ, SCOPE_TIMEBASE, SCOPE_GAIN, SCOPE_OFFSET, SCOPE_BW, GEN_AMPL, GEN_FLAT, GEN_OFFSET; the waiver list offers 'Uncertainty calculation for this kind is not implemented in the suite yet'. Kinds not in the table (diode, continuity, conductance, percent, trigger, functional checks, degF) carry none by design and need no waiver. No report number of an existing procedure is changed. A should-have point with no resolvable uncertainty (no standard selected, no standards sheet covers it or no row covers its range / frequency, the sheet gives no usable value, set by hand) is an ERROR that blocks publishing until the author picks a standard, fixes the point or WAIVES it. A waiver is an explicit recorded reason (chosen: own standard, zero / offset check, no source exists, scope of accreditation does not cover it, functional check, manual calibration; or typed), stored in the sheet (`options.uncertainty_waivers`: reason, who, when), listed in the Sheet check as a note, named in the version's history note ('uncertainty waivers: N') and counted in the spec's `dsb.engine.check`. A .MDD (manual calibration) import records a whole-sheet waiver 'manual_calibration' (`mdd_convert.register`, by 'import (user)'). Existing published procedures are not changed: they are evaluated when next saved; the Manager list's Automation column shows 'missing uncertainties: N' for them.
   enforced by: `datasheet_builder_rules.uncertainty_source` / `source_findings` / `set_waiver` / `clear_waiver` (error `no_uncertainty`, note `waived`; `datasheet_builder.save` turns the error into a draft and writes the waiver note), the Sheet check window ('Waive...' and 'Apply the fix' = remove waiver, `GridModel.waive_finding`, one undo step), the point inspector section 'Uncertainty source', the Manager list (`DatasheetList.automation_text`); for the report, the existing `dsb_runtime.certificate_row` / `plan_values` (blank unless the assigned standard did the point). Tests `Rule5Sources`, `Rule5Blocking` (blocked publish, waiver path, fixed path, Mudcats import, handheld template, undo, list text). NOT modelled: the scope-of-accreditation table of `report_pdf.point_uncertainty` (the Builder has the standards sheets, not the scope); the author states 'scope does not cover it' as a waiver reason.
6. Writes are set, read back, compared, never retried; only read-only queries retry; the error queue is checked after writes. run-time (branch dsb-runtime-rules); the drivers already do set / read back / compare (standing rule).
7. Start-state check before the first point: read-only checks of the instrument settings that broke the 8808A (echo, print mode, remote) with plain operator instructions if wrong; where an instrument cannot answer *IDN? the driver declares it and the check is skipped. run-time (branch dsb-runtime-rules).
8. Settle by stable reading, not only by time: N readings agree within a stated limit; the fixed time is the minimum, a timeout is the cap; tunable, flagged unverified. run-time (branch dsb-runtime-rules).
9. Safe outputs: calibrator to standby after each point and on abort / error / window close; the HV confirmation once per hook-up group; cable prompts only when the hook-up changes; the dial prompt on every function change. run-time (branch dsb-runtime-rules).
10. Reset once per session with an operator notice that the meter under test was reset. run-time (branch dsb-runtime-rules); see Reset to default settings and the 8808A section.
11. Progress saved after every point with a datasheet-match check on resume; the command log saved before any error dialog. run-time (branch dsb-runtime-rules).
12. Per-section toggles: any section can be switched between automated, typed by hand and not tested; recorded in the report and the saved progress; a skipped section never shows as failed (pattern: the Fluke 376 iFlex toggle). run-time (branch dsb-runtime-rules).
13. Per-point override: a technician can type a reading for any automated point, with no note on the report (the 8808A manual-override mixin). run-time (branch dsb-runtime-rules).
14. Limits display: the Builder shows low and high limits. branch dsb-low-high-limits.
- How the build-time rules are wired (additive; the instrument commands, limits, pass / fail logic and safety prompts of every existing procedure are untouched): `datasheet_builder_rules.py` (Tk-free except the dry run, which imports `dsb_runtime` lazily and is skipped, with a note, where tkinter is missing) -> `datasheet_builder.save()` (error findings keep a sheet a DRAFT; the summary is stored in the runnable spec as `dsb.engine.check`) -> `datasheet_builder_check_model.CheckMixin` (mixed into `GridModel`: `sheet_check`, `check_rows`, `apply_check_fix`, `uncertainty_line`, `pre_run_list`) -> `datasheet_builder_check_ui.py` (Sheet check button under the sheet, pill in the title strip, `SheetCheckWindow`, `CommandsWindow`, the real-run question). `calibration_procedures.sqlite3` is not touched; nothing new is stored beyond the extra `check` key in the runnable spec.
- Rule 2 is applied from the registry flags (verified + manual page reference), not the driver table's coarse 'ok / unverified', EXCEPT routine housekeeping: `datasheet_builder_rules.HOUSEKEEPING` / `is_housekeeping` (one place, commented) exempts read-only status / identity queries (`*IDN?`, `*ESR?`, `*STB?`, `*OPC?`, `ID?`, `ERR?`, `SYST:ERR?` and long forms) and `*CLS` from the pre-run list. They are still sent; they are only not asked about. Writes that configure or source (`*RST`, `*OPC`, `OUT`, `STBY`, `OPER` ...) and `OUT?` / `SCOPE?` are NOT exempt. A sheet whose only unverified commands are the exempt ones asks nothing on a real run (e.g. plain DC volts on the 5560A); a capacitance sheet still lists the 5560A capacitance commands. Marking a command verified is a hardware claim only Mike can make.
- The sheet check FAILS CLOSED: if it crashes when a sheet is saved (`datasheet_builder_rules.blocking_problems`, or the rules module cannot be loaded in `datasheet_builder.save`), the sheet is saved as a DRAFT with the error 'The sheet check could not run: <error>' and the traceback goes to the log; it is never published unchecked. Tests `FailClosed`.
- Rule 3 accepts some unit spellings the program does not write itself (Ohm / Ohms, a capital K, a trailing rms / pp / dc / ac, the .MDD import's own spellings). Every accepted spelling is listed in one note ('unit_variants') in the Sheet check. The .MDD import's whole-sheet uncertainty waiver reads 'manual calibration: imported from .MDD, no automation', attributed 'import (user)', in the check and the version history. Rule 5 'missing uncertainty' and its waivers are shown in the Builder only, not on the printout. An uncovered point (rule 1) stays a warning; rule 5 blocks it unless it is fixed or waived.
- Severity (decision, reversible in `datasheet_builder_rules.py`): rule 1 not-covered and rule 2 are WARNINGS; rule 5 'no uncertainty' (a should-have reading with neither a source nor a waiver) is an ERROR (draft; Run and Try in demo refuse); a warning leaves the sheet 'ready'; rule 1 with an author-chosen calibrator that cannot make the point, rule 3 and rule 4 'unsupported command' are ERRORS (draft, Run and Try in demo refuse).
- Unverified here: the Sheet check window, the inspector section and the real-run question were looked at on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only (never on Windows); the dry run uses the simulated drivers, which model command SHAPE, not the instruments.
- Tests: `tests/test_datasheet_builder_rules.py` (each rule pass / fail, the Mudcats import, the handheld template, an out-of-range point, an unverified command, a unit mismatch, the pre-run acceptance, the list column, the window under the Tk stub); real-Tk views: `checks_dsb_sheet_check` in `tests/real_tk_checks.py`.

## Procedures tab: Performance Verification / Adjustment views (branch `manager-tabs`; the tab 'Performance Verification' is now 'Automated Calibrations' and a 'Manual Calibrations' tab was added, see the Manual Calibrations section below; viewed on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb, light and dark; presentation only, never seen on Windows)
- The Procedures tab has two views, a second tab strip (`Performance Verification`, `Adjustment`). One shared table and detail card: search, category filter (the list of categories belongs to the view), Run / demo / Mark verified / Unlock and the Verified column work as before on both. The Adjustment view shows a note (adjustment changes the instrument's calibration; adjustment procedures are separate from verification). A search with no hit says how many procedures match in the other tab. Status line counts are per view. Default window height 740 -> 800 (clamped to the screen) to pay for the extra strip.
- Classification: `procscan.classify(name, category, kind)`: an explicit sidecar field `kind` (`"verification"` | `"adjustment"`, optional) wins; otherwise 'adjust' in the category or the display name = adjustment; everything else, user-built datasheets and unknown scripts = verification. `Proc.kind` is the raw field, `Proc.effective_kind` the result. Today only `Fluke_8846A_Adjustment` (category 'Digital Multimeter Adjustment') is an adjustment. A new adjustment procedure (Keithley 2000 ...) needs only its sidecar (category or name containing 'Adjustment', or `"kind": "adjustment"`); no Manager change. A bad `kind` value is a bad sidecar (skipped and listed in `ScanResult.problems`, like any other bad field). `registration`, STABLE, VERSIONS, the fingerprints and the legacy fixture are unchanged; packages carry the field through build / inspect / install unchanged (the sidecar is copied as it is). Nothing in any procedure (commands, limits, pass/fail, safety prompts) was touched.
- Tests: `tests/test_manager_tabs.py` (classification, sidecar field, package round trip, bad value), `checks_manager_tabs` in `tests/real_tk_checks.py` (run by `tests/test_beta_real_tk_windows.py`). Screenshots (not in the repo): `manager_{verification,adjustment}_{light,dark}.png`.

## Keithley 2000 guided ADJUSTMENT (branch `keithley-2000-adjustment`; simulated bench and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only, never run on a real 2000 or a calibrator)
- What: `Procedures/Keithley_2000_Adjustment.py` (window) + `keithley2000_adjustment.py` (logic, no tkinter: `Session`, `AdjustIO`) + `adjustment_audit.py` (audit record) + `drivers/keithley_2000_cal.py` (`Keithley2000Cal`, the :CAL:PROT commands) + `drivers/simulated_k2000_cal.py` (simulated 2000 with temporary and saved constants, accepts only its own SIM_CODE). Spec `specs/keithley_2000_adjustment.json` (builder `specs/build_k2000_adjustment_spec.py`, read from the page images of 2000-905-01 Rev. F). Sidecar: category 'Digital Multimeter Adjustment', `kind: adjustment` (Adjustment tab), order 32.
- Method: the manual's remote SCPI command calibration (pp. 2-14 to 2-19), not the front-panel method (the manual offers both; the remote one has the commands, so the program drives it). 26 steps in the manual's order: D01-D13 (DC: front short, front open, +10 V, -10 V, +100 V, 4-wire 1 kohm to 10 Mohm, 10 mA, 100 mA, 1 A) then A01-A13 (AC: 10 mV to 700 V at 1 kHz and 50 kHz, 100 mA, 1 A, 2 A at 1 kHz). Scope: complete, DC only or AC only (the manual allows each, p. 2-14 note). Firmware numbering: A20 and later (13 DC steps) or A19 and earlier (no 10 Mohm step, steps 10-12 are the currents; Table 3-1 note, p. 3-4); read from `*IDN?` and asked of the operator when it cannot be read.
- Order of a run (the window): connect (INPUTS switch read) -> Start record (notice; both instruments reset, 2000 UNIT UNDER TEST notice first; DC volts / SLOW 10 PLC set and read back) -> Unlock (operator confirms warm-up and conditions, the As Found verification, authority, the 'changes the calibration' notice; then types the code; `:CAL:PROT:CODE` once, `:LOCK?` must read 1, `:INIT` once) -> per step: hook-up dialog (picture if installed) -> thermal wait (3 min, short and +10 V, p. 2-15) -> calibrator STBY, programmed, read back (`OUT?`), error queue, OPER confirmed, settle -> parameter dialog (DC steps 3-13: the calibrator's own read-back, editable only to a certificate value, checked against the manual's +/-10 % range in Decimal) -> final 'send' confirmation -> the command ONCE, `*OPC?`, error queue, calibrator to STANDBY -> after the last step: dates (`:DATE`, `:NDUE`, each read back) -> typed `SAVE 2000` -> `:SAVE` -> `:LOCK` (read back 0). High-voltage confirmation at 100 V and above.
- The code: asked at run time with a masked entry, passed to `Keithley2000Cal.unlock` and dropped. It is a secret placeholder in `CAL_COMMANDS` (`********` in the command log, `<code masked>` in the golden record), the transport error that carries the command is scrubbed and raised outside the except block (no chained exception holds it), `adjustment_audit.clean()` masks any `:CODE` text again, the settings file never gets it, the simulator logs it masked. Only SIM_CODE exists in the repository. Tested: command log, database bytes, audit events, exception text and chain, window labels.
- Writes: set, read back where a query exists (function, PLC, dates, lock state, calibrator OUT / OPER / ZCOMP), compare, never retried. Read-only queries retry (`query_retry`). A step whose error queue is not empty is `failed` and is never repeated by the program: the operator must confirm a repeat (`rerun_ack`). A step whose `*OPC?` gets no answer is `indeterminate`: not resent; the operator says from the front panel whether the 2000 is idle, then the error queue is read.
- Abort: calibrator to STANDBY, `:LOCK`, and an honest text. Before `:SAVE` the stored constants are unchanged (the manual: constants are 'only temporary unless :SAVE is sent', pp. 2-19 and 3-8). The manual does not say whether `:LOCK` drops the temporary constants at once, so the text tells the operator to switch the 2000 off and on. After `:SAVE` nothing can be undone (no restore command). If `:SAVE` was sent but not confirmed the record says UNKNOWN.
- Audit: new tables `adjustment_runs` / `adjustment_events` in `lab_registry.sqlite3` (`calibration_procedures.sqlite3` untouched). Statuses IN_PROGRESS, UNLOCKED, SAVE_SENT, SAVE_NOT_CONFIRMED, SAVED_NOT_VERIFIED, ABORTED. A demo run uses an in-memory database (every row demo = 1), writes no command log, no Asset History; nothing exports to CSV or PDF; no cover sheet. NOTE: `Fluke_8846A_Adjustment.py` still creates its own `adjustment_runs` tables inside `calibration_procedures.sqlite3` next to the launcher, which goes against the standing rule; not changed here (existing procedure), flagged for the owner.
- Calibrators: `Fluke 5560A`, `Fluke 5522A` and `Fluke 5520A`. The 5520A is in the picker because `calibrator_fit.assess('5520A', adj.requests())` finds a specification row for every one of the 24 sourced outputs (the rule of the other pickers; `Fluke5520A` driver, most of its commands UNVERIFIED, see its tooltip); like the 5560A it gets no `ZCOMP` and the hook-up dialog tells the operator to set its own 4-wire mode. SUITABILITY against the manual's own requirement: `specs/k2000_adjustment_standards.md` (written by `specs/analyze_k2000_adjustment_standards.py` from the standards sheets) compares each sourced step with the manual's Table 2-1 (Fluke 5700A 90-day ppm; 'alternate equipment ... at least as good', p. 2-6): the 5560A meets it at 4 of 24 steps, the 5522A and the 5520A at none (the 5522A / 5560A sheets hold the 1-year column, which is conservative). The window states this under the picker; the lab decides. The 5790B / 5700A are not driven.
- Hook-up: the pictures are the owner's (`hook_k2000_ADJ_<cal>_<GROUP>.png`, see `diagrams/README_k2000.txt`); the verification pictures are the fallback. Missing pictures do not stop a run.
- Tests: `tests/test_k2000_adjustment.py` (49: spec and A19 mapping, full runs GPIB / RS-232 / 5522A / 5560A / A19, command order and one-time commands, code secrecy, wrong code, every refusal, failure / hang / save / abort, standby, demo persistence, sidecar), `tests/golden/keithley_2000_cal.json` (new golden record, reviewed; the f-string lint baseline gains one `query_retry_f` for the registry key `f'{key}.query'` in `_set_date`, not a command text), `tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_k2000_adjustment` (run by `test_beta_real_tk_windows`; screenshots with PKG_SHOTS). Fixtures: `legacy_registry.json` extended, `test_reset_startup.py` (engine list, `Keithley2000Cal` among the manual-backed resets, reset-before-prepare), `test_beta_live_editor_engines.py` (NOT_WIRED), `test_manager_tabs.py` (two adjustment procedures).
- Manual misprints found (each in the spec `flags`, and the printed value is NOT silently used): Table 3-2 (p. 3-10) prints the 10 Mohm parameter limits as 9E-6 to 11E-6 (p. 3-14 and Table 2-9: 9E6 to 11E6, used); Table 3-3 (p. 3-16) and p. 3-17 label the 700 V AC step '100V AC at 1kHz' (Tables 3-1, 2-6, 2-11 and Program C-1: 700 V, used); p. 3-17 labels the 100 mA AC step '10mA AC' (Tables 3-1, 2-7, 2-12 and Program C-1: 100 mA, used).
- UNVERIFIED (not stated by the manual or not seen on a real 2000): what a wrong code does (the program stops after one code and says so); how long each step and `:SAVE` take (waits are 120 s and 60 s on `*OPC?`, this program's choice); whether `:LOCK` or EXIT discards temporary constants; whether `:COUNt?` increments on save (shown, not judged); the `:DATE?` / `:NDUE?` reply format (the driver reads three integers); the `*IDN?` firmware revision text (asks the operator when unreadable); the `OUT?` reply format on a 5560A (first number used) and `ZCOMP` on the 5522A at 1 Mohm and 10 Mohm 4-wire; the settle times (`SETTLE_S`: 8 s DC, 15 s AC and high ohms; the manual says only 'let the source settle'); the thermal wait is the manual's 3 min but not rechecked after a lead change; the hook-up wording and the calibrator terminal names; that the calibrator's external sense switches with ZCOMP on the 5522A; the `*RST` before calibration (the manual does not ask for it; the standing rule does).
- Not built: manufacturing calibration (`:DC:STEP0`, `:AC:STEP14` / `15`, the OPEN-key power-up, function generator and synthesizer; factory / repair only), the front-panel (keystroke) method, a PDF adjustment record, resuming a record after the window is closed (a restart starts a new record; nothing is stored until SAVE, so an interrupted run is simply repeated).
- 8808A DC current settle (owner, 2026-10-23: readings were taken too early on the 10 A and 190 uA DC points): `DCI_10A_SETTLE_S` = 8 s (10 A range) and `DCI_LOW_SETTLE_S` = 6 s (200 uA / 2000 uA ranges), from OPER to the first MEAS1?, tunable in procedure modifications (`f8808a.settle.dci_10a`, `f8808a.settle.dci_low`) or per point. BOTH ARE ENGINEERING ESTIMATES, NOT manual figures (UNVERIFIED): tune on the real meter. Other points keep their times. Test: `tests/test_8808a_dci_settle.py`.

## Fluke 8808A guided ADJUSTMENT (branches `8808a-adjustment`, `-2`, `-3`; simulated bench and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only, never run on a real 8808A or a calibrator)
- What: `Procedures/Fluke_8808A_Adjustment.py` (window) + `fluke8808a_adjustment.py` (logic, no tkinter: `Session`, `AdjustIO`) + `adjustment_audit.py` (the K2000's audit record, reused unchanged) + `drivers/fluke_8808a_cal.py` (`Fluke8808ACal(Fluke8808A)`, the calibration dialog, registry `CAL_COMMANDS`, all risk 'locked' so never overridable) + `drivers/simulated_8808a_cal.py` (simulated 8808A with the dialog; its reply formats are SIMULATION CHOICES). Spec `specs/fluke_8808a_adjustment.json` built by `specs/build_8808a_adjustment.py` from the rendered pages of the Calibration Manual (Oct 2008) Table 3-9 (pp. 3-12 to 3-15), the dialog (pp. 3-16 / 3-17) and the Calibration Points lists (pp. 3-17 / 3-18); read twice. Sidecar `Procedures/Fluke_8808A_Adjustment.procedure.json` (kind 'adjustment', Adjustment tab). Standards note: `specs/analyze_8808a_adjustment_standards.py` -> `specs/8808a_adjustment_standards.md`.
- Method (revision 2, owner policy): the CALIBRATION MODE IS ENTERED BY THE OPERATOR AT THE FRONT PANEL with the calibration button; the program sends NOTHING to enter it. The dialog after that is the manual's RS-232 one (pp. 3-16 / 3-17): for each function its name (VDC, VAC, ADC, AAC, OHMS, FREQ), the meter replies with the recommended value, optional `CALREF <value>` ('=>' accepted, '!>' not), Enter confirms the value, Enter again starts calibrating ('WAIT...'), the next recommended value, the banner 'Select a Function or input EXIT to QUIT!' at the end of a function; `EXIT` leaves. 124 steps in Table 3-9's order: VDC 21 (S001-S021), VAC 21, FREQ 12, OHMS 28 (12 four-wire, 16 two-wire), AAC 17, ADC 25. Entry check: the manual prints NO query that shows the calibration mode, so it cannot be verified read-only: the program reads whatever the meter has already sent (the banner may or may not arrive; the manual does not say), logs 'ENTER CAL / OPERATOR', and treats the mode as proved only when the first function name is answered with a recommended value ('ENTER CAL / VERIFIED'); if it is not, nothing was changed and the operator is told the meter is probably not in the calibration mode. `*TER` (the manual's RS-232 entry, p. 3-16) is kept as an OPTIONAL path, OFF by default (a checkbox in the window, `Session.enter_calibration(use_ter=True)`), and as the ONE operator-approved retry described below, clearly UNVERIFIED: it is the one RS-232 entry the manual prints, and the manual does not say whether it works without, or in addition to, the front-panel entry, so it is not removed.
- NO password, no SAVE, no lock and no calibration date exist in the manual. Nothing is stored 'at the end': every point changes the calibration when its second Enter is sent, and a function that is started must be completed (an incomplete function shows CAL ERROR, p. 3-16). So the safety is in the confirmations: warm-up / As Found / authority / notice / 'entered at the front panel' before the first function; a confirmation of every FUNCTION start (it says all N points must be done); a confirmation of EVERY POINT (a dialog that names the meter's recommended value, the calibrator output and any CALREF); the second Enter is sent once and never repeated.
- Order of a run: Connect -> Start record (reset both instruments once per connection, UUT notice first; a later record on the same connection runs `session_check`) -> Calibration mode entered (acks, then the operator step 'Enter calibration mode on the front panel using the calibration button, then confirm'; nothing is sent) -> Run next point (function start, value decision, hook-up, safety, source, point) ... -> Finish (EXIT; `*ESR?` once). The window cannot be closed in the calibration mode; Abort puts the calibrator in STANDBY, sends `EXIT` (the only exit the manual prints) and says what is not known; if the meter still shows the calibration mode the operator leaves it at the front panel as the lab procedure says (the manual's front-panel exit is not in the pages used; switching the meter off is UNVERIFIED) (the manual does not say whether the points done in an unfinished function are kept, so the text says to repeat the function and verify).
- Table 3-9 against the Calibration Points lists (read twice, they differ): AC volts first gain points (steps 23, 27, 31, 35, 39: table 10 mV / 0.1 / 1 / 10 / 37.5 V, list 0.05 / 0.5 / 5 / 50 / 100 V) and AC current (steps 92, 96: table 0.1 A / 0.5 A, list 0.01 A / 1.0 A). The METER's own recommended value is authoritative: each point is compared with the table and the list; a mismatch is shown, the operator follows the meter (default) or chooses the Table 3-9 value (then `CALREF` is sent and the calibrator is set to the table value; if the real meter answers '!>' nothing is sent again and the operator can follow the meter instead). A value that matches neither needs an explicit acknowledgement. The lists also carry a 0.0 first value per range where the table has one zero step per function (not a step).
- Characterization: the table says 'need characterize 5520A with 8508A' (this lab characterizes with a Keysight / Agilent 3458A: named in the value prompt, the hook-up text of those steps and the standards note; the suite has NO 3458A standards sheet or driver, so it is a text mention only and no specification is assumed) beside the 200 uA, 2000 uA, 200 mA and 2 A DC ranges (steps 101-108, 113-120). Those steps ask for the characterized value (at most 6 significant digits, within 1 % of the meter's value, this program's guard) and send `CALREF` with it; leaving it empty is an explicit, logged waiver (the calibrator's nominal is used). A CALREF with no '=>' / '!>' answer is not retried: the operator confirms the value on the meter display.
- Calibrators: `Fluke 5522A` (with `ZCOMP`: WIRE4 for the four-wire steps, WIRE2 below 110 kohm and NONE above for the two-wire steps, set and read back), `Fluke 5560A`, `Fluke 5520A` (no compensation command is sent; the hook-up text tells the operator to set the calibrator's own 4-wire / 2-wire mode). `calibrator_fit.assess`: each covers 118 of the 119 sourced outputs; S054 (1.9 V at 750 kHz, frequency step) is not in any sheet, so there the operator connects their own standard (a dialog; the calibrator stays in STANDBY; the meter is not read, the operator vouches). Table 3-1 names a 5700A + 5725A for volts and current, a Fluke 271 or equivalent (better than 0.0025 %) for frequency and a 5520A for resistance (better than 0.0045 %, 45 ppm); `specs/8808a_adjustment_standards.md` shows each calibrator's ppm and the TAR against the 8808A 1-year limit (several resistance steps exceed 45 ppm for all three). ZERO OHM (S055, owner policy): the low thermal 4-wire short on INPUT and SENSE (group ZERO_SHORT, shared with the DC volts zero), no calibrator output, calibrator in STANDBY; the meter shows 4WIRE. So 119 steps need a calibrator output (not 120).
- Terminals: the meter's lead messages ('Disconnect Test Leads!', 'Connect to mA socket!', 'Connect to 10A socket') are parsed; a message that does not fit the step's hook-up group is shown as a warning. Hook-up groups: ZERO_SHORT, VOLT, OHM4, OHM2, ZERO_OPEN, IMA, I2A, I10A. NO hook-up diagrams are used (owner decision): text wording only, no PNG lookup, no missing-diagram warning, sidecar `diagram_prefixes` empty, `diagrams/README_8808a.txt` says none are needed; do not add placeholders. High voltage (100 V and above) and high current (2 A and above) are confirmed once per hook-up group, not per point.
- Audit: the same `adjustment_runs` / `adjustment_events` tables as the 2000 in `lab_registry.sqlite3` (a demo run is in memory, every row demo = 1, no command log, nothing recorded). The audit table is the ONLY record: no PDF record (owner decision), nothing in the window or documents says one is coming; no cover sheet, no standards-due tracking. The manual-override mixin does not apply (no reading is taken).
- Writes: set, read back where something can be read (calibrator OUT? / OPER? / ZCOMP; the meter shows only prompts), compare, never retried; read-only queries retry. A point refused ('!>') is `failed` and only repeated on a fresh confirmation (`rerun_ack`); a point with no completion within the wait is `indeterminate`: the Enter is NOT resent, the operator can read what the meter has sent since (read-only) or abort.
- Verified procedures affected: none. The existing 8808A verification fingerprints cover `drivers/fluke_8808a.py` and not the new `drivers/fluke_8808a_cal.py`; `drivers/__init__.py` gained one export. New golden record `tests/golden/fluke_8808a_cal.json` (reviewed); the f-string lint baseline gains the `fluke_8808a_cal` entry (all zero).
- Tests: `tests/test_8808a_adjustment.py` (spec counts and the seven flagged conflicts, driver parse / registry, full runs on the 5522A / 5560A / 5520A with an own standard at S054, one `*TER`, one second Enter per point, standby after every point, every refusal, conflict / characterization / CALREF paths, failure / hang / abort / EXIT, reset once per connection, demo persistence, sidecar), `tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_8808a_adjustment` (the whole demo run in the real window; screenshots with PKG_SHOTS), `tests/test_manager_tabs.py`, `tests/test_reset_startup.py`, `tests/fixtures/legacy_registry.json`. Revision 2 adds: no TER by default, panel entry with / without banner, operator wrong about the entry, optional TER path, zero ohm = 4-wire short (119 sourced steps), 3458A named, no diagram code.
- UNVERIFIED (not printed in the manual, never seen on a real 8808A): whether the banner is sent after a front-panel entry, how the meter shows / leaves the calibration mode over RS-232 (no query is printed), whether the optional `*TER` works with or without the front-panel entry; the reply text and unit spelling of the recommended value (the parser reads '<value> <unit>, <hz> HZ [W4]' and treats an upper-case MV / MA as milli); the number format `CALREF` accepts (plain decimal, at most 6 digits) and whether it is accepted at a value other than the recommended one; the encoding of the Enter key (an empty line, LF); whether the prompts '=>' / '!>' / '?>' appear with Echo off; every timing (QUIET_S 1.5 s, POINT_TIMEOUT_S 180 s, the settle times, warm-up 30 min are engineering choices; the manual says only 'a stable input'); whether the constants are stored per point or per function; what EXIT does inside a function; `*ESR?` inside / after the dialog; 750 kHz own-standard point; the 5560A / 5520A command set (most commands UNVERIFIED, see their tooltips); the 5522A `ZCOMP` limit (110 kohm); the zero ohm point with the 4-wire short on INPUT and SENSE (table says '0 ohm, 4Wire'; owner policy).
- Not built: the front-panel keystroke method (only the entry is manual), the 8808A's own serial calibration of the factory (none in the manual), resuming an interrupted record, the As Found / As Left verification (the window says to run them).

- Revision 3 (owner answers): (1) ONE `*TER` retry. When the first function name is not answered with a recommended value (`Session.entry_failed`) the window opens a dialog ('Meter did not enter calibration mode - try *TER once? (UNVERIFIED)'); only its explicit Yes calls `Session.retry_entry_with_ter(confirmed=True)`, which sends `*TER` ONCE and reads the banner. `ter_attempted` is set before the write, so even a failing attempt is the one attempt; never more than one per session (also none if the optional entry `*TER` was used, none once the entry is verified, none after a point was sent); a failed or declined retry sets `must_stop` (the window aborts, nothing else is sent). The retry never re-sends the function name: that needs the operator's next, separately confirmed function start. A still-failing function name after the retry stops the run. Tests: `TerRetryTests`.
- (2) Leaving the calibration mode: abort and finish texts say that pressing the SHIFT key at the front panel exits it, marked OWNER-STATED (not from the manual pages read); the 'switching the meter off' suggestion is gone. `EXIT` over RS-232 is still sent (the manual's way).
- (3) Audit 'source': `adjustment_events` gained an additive column `source` (`ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` in `lab_registry.sqlite3` only; added when missing; `AuditStore.log(..., source='')`, `AuditStore.sources()`; the 2000's rows have ''). For the four characterized DC-current ranges (steps 101-108, 113-120; the 200 uA, 2000 uA, 200 mA and 2 A ranges) the CALREF, value, point-sent and point rows carry `3458A characterised` (`calibrator nominal (characterisation waived)` when the operator waives); text only, no 3458A driver or specification. NOTE: `adjustment_audit.py` is in the fingerprint of the 2000 adjustment too (additive, behaviour unchanged; that procedure is not marked verified). Tests: `AuditSourceTests`.

## Manual Calibrations: Mudcats .MDD import and typed readings (branch `mdd-manual-calibrations`; simulated bench and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only; the .MDD used is SYNTHETIC)
- What: the Procedures tab now has three views, `Automated Calibrations` (renamed from `Performance Verification`), `Manual Calibrations` (new) and `Adjustment`. A Cal Processor .MDD is imported from the Procedures tab (`Import Mudcats datasheet (.mdd)...`) or the Mudcats Datasheet Importer tab (`Import as manual calibration...`), previewed, then registered as a normal user datasheet whose kind is `manual`. The run window lets the technician type every reading into the test point grid.
- Stored kind: `'verification'` stays the stored value for automated calibrations (only the tab TEXT changed), `'manual'` is new (`procscan.KINDS`, `KIND_MANUAL`). Less risky than renaming: no sidecar, package, fingerprint, legacy registry fixture or `procscan.registration` changes, and an older program reading a newer sidecar just refuses `manual` like any bad kind. `classify()` NEVER returns `manual` from wording: only an explicit sidecar `kind` or a saved datasheet's kind does, so nothing moves tabs by itself.
- Kind of a user datasheet: new column `user_datasheets.kind` in `lab_registry.sqlite3` (added by `ALTER TABLE` when missing; `calibration_procedures.sqlite3` untouched). `datasheet_builder.classify_kind(ds)`: `manual` when `ds.options['manual_entry']` (the owner's flag, a checkbox on the Datasheet Builder header page; `EditorModel.set_manual_entry`) or when the standards matcher finds NO point a standard could automate; else `verification`. The kind follows the runnable version (a draft keeps the earlier one).
- Import (`mdd_convert.py`, no Tk): `parse_mdd` (unchanged: Cal Processor only, 30 MB limit, only the 'Data Sheet Header' and 'Test Points' tables are read; commands / scripts are never read or run) -> `convert()` -> `Datasheet` (sections per function text, consecutive points; a point = nominal in SI, frequency / input level from `@ ...` conditions, exact typed limits `Tol_Low`/`Tol_High` as printed in the tolerance unit, `settings['decimals']` = the most decimals printed, so a typed reading is padded by `handheld_dmm.pad_reading`; label = nominal text; note = MDD point / TP id / page / spec statement). Ranges are NOT in an MDD: one 'unspecified' range per section and printed unit prefix (never shown). New function kinds `TEMPF`, `COND`, `PCT`, `CHECK` (units degF, S, %, none; all hand-done, matcher status `manual`). `options.manual_entry` makes `no_frequency` / `no_amplitude` warnings and every point `MANUAL`. Period from `CalInterval` ('365 Days').
- Not converted (listed in the preview with the reason, never silently): unknown or mismatched units, limits that are not numbers or are inverted, empty points. Converted but marked CHECK: AC/DC guessed from the wording, a numeric range printed as the nominal (its middle is the nominal, the range is the limits), unknown nominal unit, missing frequency / level. As Found / As Left / Out of Tol text already in the MDD is NOT copied (a warning says how many points had some). Specification tables, page layout, header rows, commands, scripts: not read.
- Registration (`mdd_convert.register`): archive `<data>/mdd_archive/<sha256>.MDD` (`mdd_importer_v49.archive_mdd`, immutable, an existing archive is verified not rewritten) -> `datasheet_builder.save` (a NEW uid unless `replace_uid`: then a new VERSION, earlier versions kept) -> `mdd_imports` table in `lab_registry.sqlite3` (sha256, uid, version, archive, counts, who). `find_existing` reports the same file imported before or a procedure of that name; the preview asks (default: a separate new copy; or a new version). Nothing is overwritten silently.
- Run window (`dsb_runtime.DsbApp`, `manual_grid.py`): when `options.manual_entry` (also any DSB sheet saved with it): no connection / standards / calibrator, banner says Manual Calibration, a notice explains the keys. `GridEntry` puts an entry box over the As Found cell (As Left while collecting As Left): select a point + Enter / F2 / start typing (or click a selected point) -> type -> Enter or Tab saves and opens the next point, Up / Down / Shift+Tab move, Esc closes, an empty box only moves on, F9 fills a simulated reading in demo. Saving is `DsbApp.type_reading` = `handheld_dmm.pad_reading` + `judge_numeric` + `_store` (the same path as a reading typed in the reading window of Run All Pending, which still works): NaN / inf / letters / a comma / a unit / empty are refused with the reason and the box stays open; a functional check takes Y / N. No second engine; `handheld_dmm.py` is NOT edited (Fluke 117 fingerprint). The report is the usual 'Calibration Data' PDF (asset#-date name, demo marking and nothing real-looking in demo, no cover sheet, no standards-due), with the existing `[manual]` tag on each point.
- Tests: `tests/test_mdd_manual.py` (24: conversion counts / kinds / limits, uncertain and unconverted points, readings in the MDD not imported, embedded script never executed, size and table checks unchanged, archive immutable, registration, existing-procedure handling, new version, classification, manual run on the simulated bench with typed readings, padding, rejects, As Left, Run All Pending, PDF, demo), `tests/mdd_synth.py` (the synthetic .MDD), `tests/test_manager_tabs.py` (three views, `manual` kind, user-datasheet kind, registry migration), `checks_manager_tabs` / `checks_mdd_manual` in `tests/real_tk_checks.py` (three views, import preview, import, existing-file prompt, grid keys on a real Tk, As Left, PDF; light and dark).
- Unverified: the real Fluke 87V .MDD (not reachable: the owner's computer was not connected) - cell formats are inferred from `specs/build_87v_spec.py`, so the tolerance-unit rule, `~` condition lines, 'Pass/Fail' rows and unit spellings of the real file are untested; whether Cal Processor prints p-p / rms in units; the Windows look and fonts (the Xvfb Tk has bitmap fonts: Ω shows as a blank); the typed grid on Windows Tk focus rules.
## Light standard sheet: the lab's own A2LA CMC (specs/standards/a2la_light_standard.json; branch `light-standard`)
Owner request: 'create a standards spec for light using our CMC from the scope.' Model `A2LA-LIGHT`, description 'Light measurement (A2LA scope CMC, radiometer standards)'. Builder: `specs/build_light_standard.py` (reproducible; `tests/test_standard_light.py` rebuilds it and compares with the shipped JSON).
- Source: A2LA scope, cert. 1888.03 rev. 07/07/2026, printed p. 45 of 47, section X. Optical Quantities, 'Light - Measure' (column header CMC 2, 5, 12 (+/-)). VERIFIED against the RENDERED PAGE IMAGE of p. 45 (2026-10-23; the micro sign and the superscript 2 were read from the image): White (0 to 3000) FC, (0 to 35 000) lux, (0 to 100 000) La, 2.9 % each; Black @ 365 nm (0 to 3000) uW/cm2, 5.0 %; Comments 'Radiometer standards'. The Light row is not in `specs/a2la_1888_03_cmc.json` (that file holds Sections V and XII only) and was not added to it.
- Four functions / ranges, one row each, kinds `FC`, `LUX`, `LAMBERT`, `UVIRR` (new regexes in `specmanager.FUNCTION_KINDS`; no existing kind or lookup changes). Each CMC is stored as a percentage of reading: `[1 yr] Accuracy: +/-(2.9 % I.V.)`, no fixed term (footnote 5). The stored `rel` is built with Decimal (0.029 and 0.05 exactly the nearest float; `parse_spec` alone gives 0.028999999999999998), `specmanager.spec_value` then multiplies in float like every other sheet.
- Confidence: footnote 2 (expanded uncertainty, 'approximately the 95 % level, usually k = 2'); stored `confidence_pct` 95, divisor 2 (`tests/test_standard_confidence.py` has the decision). Budget at 10 000 lux: u(standard) = 145 lux, EMU = 290 lux = 2.9 %. A CMC has no 90 day / 1 yr columns: stored once under the period '1 yr' (default period). Direction 'output' (the light level the lab's standard establishes at the unit under test; the scope word is 'Measure'): needed for the default lookup; flagged in note 7 of the sheet.
- Units: exactly as the scope lists them, each its own unit, NOTHING converted: `FC`, `lux`, `La`, `uW/cm2` (stored ASCII; printed 'µW/cm²' in the sheet's `printed` text and in the Standards Specs view via `specmanager_view._SYMBOL`). 1 FC = 10.763910416709722 lux and 1 La = 10000/pi cd/m2 appear only in note 6 of the sheet (information; not used in any calculation). Display-only scaling: `specmanager_view.si_text` prefixes only V, A, F, Hz, W, H, S, s, ohm, so no 'kFC' or 'mµW' ever appears (tested). The Datasheet Builder / procedure-editor unit tables (`procmods_pointdef.UNIT_GROUPS`, `datasheet_builder` units) were NOT extended: no light procedure or datasheet exists yet.
- Registration (generic only): `sm.seed()` loads it, so it is in Standards Specs (viewed on real Tk 9 under Xvfb: rail card, header 95 % / divisor 2, four type chips Lux / FC / Lambert / UV 365 nm, the four rows) and is a driver-less candidate in the Datasheet Builder (`tests/test_datasheet_builder_match.py` lists it). No standard picker. `uncertainty_budget.build('LUX', ..., standard='A2LA-LIGHT')` and `tartur.standard_accuracy` work; `tartur.compute` now also accepts the four light kinds when a standard is given (same rule as TEMP: no separate scope entry exists, so no CMC floor; in the unit of the point only: a FC tolerance on a lux point is blank). Without a standard the ratios are blank as before.
- Not stored: stability / non-uniformity / spectral-mismatch terms of the radiometer, anything of the unit under test; the CMC excludes the unit under test, a certificate value may be larger. If the scope is revised, re-read p. 45 from a page image and set `VERIFIED_AGAINST_IMAGE` accordingly.
- Open question for the owner: is a light-meter procedure / datasheet template wanted? Nothing drives a light source or a reference radiometer today (no driver); any such procedure would be manual-reading with this sheet as the standard.

## 3458A standards accuracy sheet (specs/standards/keysight_3458a_standard.json; branch `std-3458a`; no driver here: another branch writes it)
Owner request: a standards accuracy table for the Keysight / Agilent 3458A. Model `3458A`, builder `specs/build_3458a_standard.py` (reproducible; `tests/test_standard_3458a.py` rebuilds it, compares it with the shipped JSON and holds a second hand-typed transcription of key cells). 13 functions, 228 rows, all direction 'input' (the 3458A measures): DC volts 15 (Option 002 high stability, Standard stability, 10 min transfer; 5 ranges each), resistance 18 (4-wire OHMF and 2-wire OHM, 9 ranges each), DC current 8, AC volts 154 (SETACV SYNC <= 2 MHz 42, SYNC > 2 MHz 21, ANA 52, RNDM <= 2 MHz 18, RNDM > 2 MHz 21), AC current 31, frequency 2.
- Source: `hp3458a_specs.pdf` (data sheet 5965-4971E), pp. 12, 13, 16, 20, 24, 27, 29, 32, 34 (intro p. 8). Every number read from the RENDERED PAGE IMAGES and read twice; `VERIFIED_AGAINST_IMAGE` is True.
- Periods (owner: the lab uses 1 year specifications): only '1 yr' (default) and '90 day' (the 5522A also stores 90 day and 1 yr) plus '10 min' for the DC transfer table. NO 24 hour and NO 2 year specification is stored anywhere (rows, notes, tests); the 2 yr 1000 V cell that was unreadable is therefore not an issue and has no flag. The AC and frequency tables print one '24-hour to 2 year' column and are stored under both periods. 2-wire ohms offsets stored: 0.15 ohm (90 day), 0.25 ohm (1 yr).
- OPTION 002 IS THE DEFAULT (owner: the lab's 3458A has Option 002 high stability). Only the DC volts table prints Option 002 figures (parentheses, p. 12 footnote 5); the resistance, DC current and AC tables have one set (re-checked on the page images). A plain DC volts lookup returns `Voltage, DC [Option 002 high stability]` (`specmanager.DEFAULT_VARIANT['DCV']` prefer 'option 002', one word); the standard-stability figures stay as `Voltage, DC [Standard stability]` and are selected with `variant='standard stability'`. Spot check, 10 V range, 1 yr: Option 002 4 ppm + 0.05 ppm of 10 V = +/-40.5 uV (plain lookup); standard stability 8 ppm + 0.05 ppm = +/-80.5 uV. The sheet note 7 says so.
- Terms: 'ppm of range' maps to the existing `fs_rel x fs_value`; `fs_value` is the range NAME (p. 8: 'range' is the range name, not full scale). New additive raw form `... of range{"Range" = 10 V}` in `specmanager.parse_spec` (other sheets unchanged) and the matching display branch in `specmanager_view._term` ('0.05 ppm of range (10 V)'). Stored ppm terms are Decimal-exact. DC current 100 nA to 10 uA rows are the printed 'typical' ones (flagged).
- `DEFAULT_VARIANT['ACV']` prefer is ('sinewave', 'sync, acband <= 2 mhz') so a plain ACV lookup picks the sine SYNC table instead of the largest limit across modes (one-tuple edit; the other sheets are unaffected, tested).
- Confidence: the data sheet prints no level and no factor: `confidence_pct` None, k = 2 assumed (repository rule; owner accepted for now), flagged in the sheet and in `tests/test_standard_confidence.py`.
- Direction stays 'input' (owner): `tartur.standard_accuracy(direction='input')` works; `uncertainty_budget.build` defaults to 'output' and so does not find a measuring sheet by default (same as the 5790B); not changed here.
- Datasheet Builder matcher: the 3458A is a driver-less standard. ONE additive change in `datasheet_builder_match._rank`: a covered candidate WITH a driver always ranks before one WITHOUT (new first sort key `0 if c.driver else 1`, before the TUR bucket). So the 3458A (and the other spec-only sheets: 8563E, 8902A, light, coils) can never take a point from a driver-backed instrument; it is offered as a manual candidate when nothing driver-backed covers the point. Every existing assignment test passes unchanged (generator frequency stays 53132A, DC offset stays 5790B); `tests/test_datasheet_builder_match.py::DriverlessLast` is new.
- OPEN ITEM (owner unsure): the factory traceability adders (the sheet is relative to the calibration standards, p. 8: DC volts 2 ppm, resistance 3 ppm, DC current 5 ppm, AC volts 2 ppm, AC current 5 ppm of reading) are NOT stored; flagged in the sheet. Also not stored: additional-error terms (footnote 6 above 100 V etc.), temperature coefficients, and the 1 Gohm with-ACAL tempco (unreadable). No row for DC current above 1.05 A: the 8808A 2 A range is outside the 3458A (owner: leave as noted); 200 uA falls on the 1 mA range, 2000 uA on the 10 mA range, 200 mA on the 1 A range. No procedure, picker or driver changed. Viewed on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb in light and dark.

## Manual Calibrations: the real Fluke 87 Series V .MDD (branch `mdd-real-file`; simulated bench and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only)
