- The real file (51 test points, Cal Processor 2.5.2.180, interval '365') was run through `parse_mdd` and `mdd_convert`: 51 of 51 converted, 0 not converted, 12 flagged CHECK. What it exposed, all fixed in `mdd_convert.py`: (1) the file as received holds U+FFFD where the micro / degree / plus-minus signs were (the bytes are gone, not a decoding problem): `repair_units` restores them ONLY in unit positions from the function text (`�A` = µA, `�F` = °F when the function is temperature else µF, `�V` / `�s` / `�S` / `�Hz` / `�Ohm` = µ) and flags the point CHECK; the spec statement's lost ± is shown as ±; (2) `Text_Function` is only filled on the first row of a group: '|' and bare range rows ('60V Range') continue the section above, 'AC Volts Accuracy: 600mV Range' is the section 'AC Volts Accuracy' (the range text goes to the point label), a truncated repeat ('AC Curren') joins its section; (3) AC / DC not named in a row's own text is taken from the section above and flagged `kind_inherited`; (4) a bare number interval ('365') means days (period '365d', '365 Days'); (5) `Pass/Fail` rows with a numeric range as the nominal (open capacitance, the two low-pass-filter rows, flagged: range middle used as nominal, a frequency in the function text is the point's frequency) work as before; (6) 'Text_AsLeft = Same' is Cal Processor's default and no longer counts as a reading (47 points DO carry an As Found text from an earlier run: warned, not imported); (7) a range of a base-unit section holding 1014 V was displayed in kV (3.3 V shown as 0.0033 kV): imported ranges now carry `settings['display_prefix']` (the printed prefix), honoured by `datasheet_builder_specs.display_prefix`.
- Owner decisions applied: points whose limit unit is not recognised are imported as pass / fail CHECK points (flagged in the preview, the printed limits kept in the point note); a unit that disagrees with the nominal's quantity is still not converted; the '[manual]' tag (row descriptions and the 'By hand' header line) is no longer printed for manual calibrations (`DsbApp.certificate_row` / `report_header` when `manual_entry`; the same code prints it for any other user datasheet whose points are done by hand, which is unchanged); existing-procedure default stays 'separate new copy', both options in the dialog.
- Compared with `specs/fluke_87v_pv.json` (limits, nominals, frequencies, kinds, per row): all rows equal except the ones the existing spec changes on purpose: rows 48 (Peak Min/Max MAX: spec uses the manual's 3.897..4.103 V / 4 V, the file prints 5.896..6.104 V / 6 V DC), 50 and 51 (temperature: spec uses degC limits -1..1 and 98..102; the file's degF 30.2..33.8 = -1..1 degC, but 210.2..213.8 degF = 99..101 degC), 46 and 47 (low-pass filter: the spec applies 400 V; the import uses the middle of the printed range as nominal and keeps the printed range as limits). The import never changes printed limits. Rows 30 / 31 are COND / DIODE here (the spec runs them as OHM / DCV with a source): by design, the import has no source.
- Test fixture: `tests/fixtures/fluke87v_points_excerpt.MDD` (28 of the 51 rows, made-up header, no author names, GUIDs, scripts or commands) + `tests/test_mdd_real_excerpt.py`. The full-file checks (convert, register, all 51 points typed on the simulated bench, PDF, preview screenshots) were a one-off script, not committed.

## Fluke 365 clamp meter: automated performance verification (branches `fluke-365-manual`, then `fluke-365-auto`; simulated bench and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only, never run on a real 365 or on hardware)
Owner request (Mike): first built as a manual calibration (PR #74), then re-classified: the 365 is an AUTOMATED handheld-style procedure (the suite drives the calibrator, the technician types the meter reading). Launcher `Procedures/Fluke_365_Verification.py` = `handheld_dmm.main('365', spec='fluke_365_pv.json')` (the 77 IV pattern; sidecar without `kind`, i.e. verification, so it lists under Automated Calibrations; category 'Clamp Meter', name 'Fluke 365 Clamp Meter Performance Verification'), NO 365-only engine (`fluke365_verification.py`, the typed grid, the 'Manual Calibration' title / banner and `QUEUE_SUPPORTED = False` are gone: the run queue is on like the other handhelds), spec `specs/fluke_365_pv.json` (written by `specs/build_fluke_365_spec.py`), diagram names `diagrams/README_fluke365.txt`, tests `tests/test_fluke_365_points.py`.
- Source: Fluke 365 Detachable Jaw True-rms Clamp Meter Calibration Manual (R0, 2010, 20 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page - 8). Table 2 'Performance Tests' printed pp. 7-8 (PDF 15-16), 32 points: AC volts 6 (30 / 300 / 570 V at 50 and 400 Hz), DC volts 7 (0, +-30, +-300, +-570 V), ohms 7 (0, 30, 300, 570, 900, 3000, 5700), AC amps 6 (0.5 / 5 / 9.5 A at 50 and 400 Hz), DC amps 6 (+-0.5, +-5, +-9.5 A). Specifications printed pp. 5-6, safety pp. 2-3, equipment Table 3 p. 8. Every number was read from the rendered page images (170 and 260 dpi) twice; `tests/test_fluke_365_points.py` holds the second transcription and compares all 32 rows.
- Engine: ONE additive edit to the shared `handheld_dmm.py`: the opt-in spec option `options.hv_once_per_group` (default off; `_hv_group_seen`, the key is reset when the hook-up group changes). It makes the HIGH VOLTAGE question once per hook-up group instead of once per high-voltage point. Only `fluke_365_pv.json` sets it; the 117 / 87V / 77 / 170 behave exactly as before (they ask per HV point in a single-meter run; once per group only in a shared run-queue unit), BUT `handheld_dmm.py` is fingerprinted by every handheld procedure, so they show 'Changed since verification' until re-verified (named here on purpose). Also the 365's own entries in the per-meter tables of `hookups.py` (METERS, AVAILABLE, EXTRA: `fluke365`; slice `hookups.py#fluke365`). The report is the usual PDF 'Calibration Data' named asset#-date; demo is marked on every page and synthetic everywhere; no cover sheet, no standards-due. The second round touched NO shared file: the iFlex option is the new root module `iflex_option.py` (a subclass; see below), so the handheld procedures verified before are not flagged by it (`handheld_dmm.py` and `hookups.py` stay as the first round left them).
- Calibrator per section: volts and ohms = the 5522A NORMAL / 5560A OUTPUT V/Ohm terminals on test leads in the meter VOhm and COM jacks; current = the calibrator's CURRENT output through a 20-turn wired coil (Table 3: 'wired coil, 20 turns'; the manual recommends a Fluke 55xxA with 4.5 digit resolution): 0.5 A on the low-current terminals (5522A AUX HI / AUX LO, 5560A VI AUX / OUTPUT LO; group COIL_AUX), 5 A and 9.5 A on the high-current ones (5522A 20 A / AUX LO, 5560A 30A / OUTPUT LO; group COIL_20A). `calibrator_fit` finds all 32 outputs inside the published 5522A, 5520A and 5560A sheets (LCOMP-off rows: the driver never switches LCOMP). 5520A is offered automatically by the existing picker rule.
- THE COIL: Table 2 lists the calibrator output (.5 / 5 / 9.5 A) and the METER limits (20 x that). Each current step has `applied.value` = calibrator current, `nominal` = what the meter shows (10 / 100 / 190 A), limits as printed, `dsb_desc` and `report_desc` ('200.0 A, 0.5 A x 20 turns'). UNCERTAINTY (owner-chosen convention, flagged): every current point carries the REGULAR calibrator current uncertainty (5522A / 5560A budget, `report_pdf.point_uncertainty` with the calibrator model) at the actual calibrator output current (point / 20: 0.5 / 5 / 9.5 A), AC at its frequency, DC at both polarities, AS IT IS: NOT multiplied by 20, and without the coil. TAR / TUR are computed in the suite's normal way (`tartur.row_fields`) from that unscaled uncertainty against the meter-reading tolerance printed in Table 2 (e.g. 9.6 .. 10.4 A), no x20 anywhere. So the ratio compares a coil-current uncertainty with a meter-reading tolerance: it is about 20 x more favourable than a like-for-like ratio (5522A, e.g. 5 A AC 50 Hz: 5.3 mA, TAR 260:1, TUR 245:1; 9.5 A DC: 4.1 mA, TAR 476:1). The point note states the convention (the 'NOT multiplied by 20' sentence) and `SOURCE_NOTE` / the report note repeat it. Volts and ohms points carry the usual uncertainty, TAR and TUR.
- Prompts (as the 117 / 87V / 77 / 170 runs, existing mechanisms): function prompt on every dial change (5 per run), cable-change prompt only when the hook-up group changes (9 per run: the leads once, then 8 coil moves between the low- and high-current terminals because the manual's order alternates 0.5 / 5 / 9.5 A), every coil prompt says REMOVE the test leads from the jacks (Safety Information p. 2), HIGH VOLTAGE confirmation ONCE per hook-up group (`hv_once_per_group`; all 12 points of 30 V and above are in the VOHM group, so 1 per run, before: 12, one per point; `hv_threshold_v` 30 = the manual's shock level), reading window per point (32). Total per full run: before 9 + 5 + 12 + 32 = 58, after 9 + 5 + 1 + 32 = 47. No per-point confirmations or notices. `,0Hz` on every DC output (tested), outputs to standby after each point, calibrator reset before the first point, set / read back / never retried, typed readings padded to the point resolution. Possible later: reorder the amps points by terminal to cut the 8 coil moves to 2 (manual order kept).
- LIMITS CROSS-CHECK, against the PRINTED accuracy of the 374 FC / 375 FC / 376 FC Users Manual (R3 ENG, 32 pages; General Specifications read from the RENDERED page images, twice, printed pp. 20-26): AC volts 1000 V, 0.1 V up to 600.0 V and 1 V up to 1000 V, 374 FC / 375 FC 1.5 % + 5 digits (20-500 Hz) but 376 FC 1 % + 5 digits (p. 24); DC volts same ranges 1 % + 5 digits (p. 24); mV dc (375 FC / 376 FC) 500.0 mV, 0.1 mV, 1 % + 5 (p. 24); resistance 1 % + 5, 0.1 ohm up to 600 ohm, 1 ohm up to 6000 ohm, 10 ohm up to 60 kohm (374 FC to 6000 ohm only) (p. 26); capacitance 1000 µF, 0.1 µF up to 100 µF, 1 µF up to 1000 µF, 1 % + 4 (p. 26); jaw AC amps 600.0 A (374 / 375) or 999.9 A (376), 0.1 A, 2 % + 5 (10-100 Hz) and 2.5 % + 5 (100-500 Hz) (p. 20); iFlex 2500 A, 0.1 A up to 999.9 A and 1 A up to 2500 A, 3 % + 5 (5-500 Hz) (p. 21); jaw DC amps 600.0 / 999.9 A, 0.1 A, 2 % + 5 (p. 23). The 376-II is not in that manual: it is checked with the 376 FC figures (an assumption, UNVERIFIED). The builder (`ACC` per model, `ACC_PAGE`) classifies every printed limit per model; the old fluke.com figures are gone (they had the AC volts accuracy of the 376 wrong: 1.5 % instead of 1 %). Result: every volts (DC), mV, ohms, capacitance and AC amps limit is the FULL printed accuracy to within half a display step; the iFlex simulation rows, DC amps 0.2 A and two iFlex-with-coil rows (0.2 A, 6 A) are HALF of it (2:1 margin). The printed limits are stored AS PRINTED, flagged in the point note, `step.check.models` and the builder output; the discrepancy list (rows flagged, with the Users Manual numbers): (a) 1-1, AC 10 V, 9.4 .. 10.7 V: 374 / 375 (1.5 %) +-0.65 V = 9.35 .. 10.65, the printed limits are those values rounded up (high 0.05 V looser, low 0.05 V tighter), 'rounded'; 376 / 376-II (1 %) +-0.6 V = 9.4 .. 10.6: the printed high limit is 0.1 V looser, 'looser'; (b) 1-2 and 1-4, AC 500 V at 50 and 500 Hz, 492.0 .. 508.0: 374 / 375 +-8.0 V is exactly the specification ('full'), 376 / 376-II +-5.5 V (1 %): the printed limit is 2.5 V looser on each side, 'looser'; (c) 1-3, AC 900 V (376 / 376-II only), 882 .. 919 V: 1 % + 5 digits at 1 V resolution = +-14 V (886 .. 914): the printed limits are 4 V and 5 V looser, 'looser' (the 1.5 % figure would be +-18.5 V = 881.5 .. 918.5, which the printed limits are the rounded values of: the table keeps the 374 / 375 numbers for the 376); (d) 1-28 and 1-29, DC amps 10 A and 18 A (500 / 900 A): printed +-5.0 / +-9.0 A, the accuracy (2 % + 5 digits, p. 23) is +-10.5 / +-18.5 A (half 5.25 / 9.25): tighter than either, all models; (e) 1-36 and 1-37, iFlex with coil 10 A and 18 A (500 / 900 A): printed +-7.0 / +-13.0 A, the accuracy (3 % + 5, p. 21) is +-15.5 / +-27.5 A, half of it +-7.75 / +-13.75 A: the printed +-7.0 / +-13.0 are tighter than half, all models. Nothing was corrected. Observations, not changed: (f) 1-34 is 750 mV at 500 Hz for 2500 A, the same reading as 75 mV at 50 Hz: consistent (the iFlex coil voltage is proportional to frequency, 30 µV per A at 50 Hz, 300 µV per A at 500 Hz), not a misprint; (g) the manual's '18 A @ 5 0Hz' is a typo for 50 Hz; (h) the DC-volts block holds -500 V first (the extracted text shows it in the AC block); (i) 6 A at 440 Hz is judged at the jaw's 100-500 Hz accuracy (2.5 %), the limits fit it.
- UNVERIFIED (listed in the final report). Settled by the Users Manual and the owner, no longer listed: the accuracy, ranges and resolutions (Users Manual pp. 20-26); how the yellow alternate functions are selected (the yellow button: mV with the dial on V⎓ / mV, capacitance with the dial on the Ω position and ONE press of the yellow button (owner-confirmed), Users Manual pp. 12 and 14; Hz is also the yellow button, not used here); ZERO for DC amps (yellow button, jaw clear of any conductor, then close the jaw on the coil, p. 11); the iFlex input (the far-left 2 mm jack: owner-confirmed; the iFlex probe plugs into the meter, p. 9); 6 A at 440 Hz and 18 A (the coil and the calibrator can do them: owner-confirmed; the run still stops if the calibrator refuses an output). STILL UNVERIFIED: the coil (50-turn 55XXA/COIL wiring, how the jaw and the iFlex loop sit on it, 5522A LCOMP, coil heating and duty cycle at 18 A: the coil data recorded in the coil sheet section below allows 11 A continuous and 20 A for 2 min); the order of the dial positions (read from the figure); whether ZERO has to be repeated before every DC amps point and whether it may be done with the coil in the jaw (the manual shows an empty jaw); the 376-II accuracy (assumed equal to the 376 FC: the Users Manual does not cover the 376-II); the ohms limits' treatment of test lead resistance; the 10 s capacitance and 5 s coil settle times; the 5560A current and capacitance commands (existing registry, `verified` false); the hook-up pictures (supplied by the owner, none exists yet; names in `diagrams/README_fluke376.txt`).
- Adjustment (Tables 3 and 4, printed pp. 8-10): the owner confirmed it is not needed; not built.
- Tests: `tests/test_fluke_365_points.py` (35: second transcription of Table 2, the four flagged rows, the builder's aborting rule, JSON equals builder output, coil factor 20, options, groups, hook-ups, sidecar = verification and no fork, wire commands on both calibrators, perfect meter passes all 32, prompt counts 9 cable / 5 function / 1 HV / 32 readings, uncertainty and TAR / TUR present for all 32 points and equal to the calibrator's at point / 20, other specs do not set `hv_once_per_group`, PDF 'Calibration Data' asset#-date, demo never real). `tests/test_manager_tabs.py` back to main; `tests/fixtures/legacy_registry.json` (name, FILES); `tests/real_tk_checks.py` `checks_fluke_365` (Automated view, ordinary handheld window, the three cable-change prompts), light and dark.
- Possible later: reorder the amps points by terminal (all 0.5 A points, then all 5 / 9.5 A) to cut the coil moves from 8 to 2 (the per-point editor can reorder; the manual's order is kept by default); AUTOMATING the adjustment is out of scope (front panel only: calibration button under the seal, Table 4 C000 .. C019 with the HOLD key, 4 s settle, no remote interface).

## Fluke 374 / 375 / 376 / 376-II clamp meters: automated performance verification (branches `fluke-376`, merged, and `fluke-376-models`; simulated bench and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only, never run on a real meter or on hardware)
Owner request (Mike): 'build a procedure like the handheld multimeters for the fluke 376 using this manual', after the 365; then (second round): register the 374, 375 and 376-II too, use the Users Manual for the accuracy and the front panel, add an iFlex on/off option. Launchers `Procedures/Fluke_374_Verification.py`, `Fluke_375_Verification.py`, `Fluke_376_Verification.py`, `Fluke_376II_Verification.py` = `iflex_option.main(MODEL, spec='fluke_376_pv.json')` (a subclass of `handheld_dmm.App`, see 'iFlex option' below); sidecars without `kind`, i.e. verification: Automated Calibrations; category 'Clamp Meter', names 'Fluke 374 / 375 / 376 / 376-II Clamp Meter Performance Verification' as the family title in the description and 'Fluke <model> Clamp Meter Performance Verification' as the row name (orders 36, 37, 35, 38), NO model-specific engine, spec `specs/fluke_376_pv.json` (written by `specs/build_fluke_376_spec.py`), diagram names `diagrams/README_fluke376.txt` (one set of seven pictures, prefix `hook_fluke376`, shared by the four), tests `tests/test_fluke_376_points.py`.
- Source of the points and limits: Fluke 374 / 375 / 376 / 376-II Clamp Meters Calibration Manual (December 2010, Rev. 2, 11/25, 14 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page - 4). Table 1 'Performance Tests' printed pp. 3-4 (PDF 7-8), 38 rows: AC volts 4, DC volts 8 (four of them millivolts), ohms 7, capacitance 3, AC amps (50-turn coil) 4, DC amps (50-turn coil) 3, iFlex simulated 5, iFlex with 50-turn coil 4. Equipment Table 2 p. 4, Figure 1 (meter) p. 2, the iFlex simulation paragraph p. 3. Adjustment (Table 3, pp. 5-8) is NOT built. Every number was read from rendered page images (130, 200 and 260 dpi) twice; the extracted text moves the -500 V row into the AC volts block and prints '18 A @ 5 0Hz'. `tests/test_fluke_376_points.py` holds the second transcription and compares all 38 rows and the X / - model columns.
- FAMILY SPEC (as the 110 and 170 series): every step lists its models from the X columns: 374 = 28 points (no mV, no 30K / 54K, no 900 V, no 18 A jaw rows), 375 = 34 (no 900 V, no 18 A jaw rows), 376 = 38, 376-II = 38 (Table 1 prints one column '376/376-II'). ALL FOUR are registered (one launcher + one sidecar + one `legacy_registry.json` fixture entry each, Manager rows under Automated Calibrations). All four models have all nine iFlex points (the X columns: the five iFlex simulated and the four iFlex-with-coil rows, including 18 A).
- Calibrator per section (the manual: 'Fluke 55XXA', 4.5 digit; Figure 3 shows a 5560A): the suite drives a Fluke 5522A or 5560A (5520A is offered by the existing picker rule), or you set it by hand. Volts, mV, ohms and capacitance: NORMAL HI / LO (5522A) or OUTPUT V/Ω HI / LO (5560A) on test leads in VΩ and COM (group VOHM, `hookups.py` 'fluke376'). AC / DC amps and iFlex with coil: the calibrator CURRENT output through the 50-turn coil (55XXA/COIL): 0.2 A on the low-current terminals (5522A AUX HI / AUX LO, 5560A VI AUX / OUTPUT LO; groups COIL_AUX / IFLEX_COIL_AUX), 10 / 18 / 6 A on the high-current ones (5522A 20 A / AUX LO, 5560A 30A / OUTPUT LO; COIL_20A / IFLEX_COIL_20A). iFlex simulated: the calibrator VOLTAGE output (3 / 30 / 60 / 75 mV at 50 Hz, 750 mV at 500 Hz) to the iFlex 2 mm jack (HI) and the black COM jack (LO), with the 2 mm to 4 mm Slim Reach probe (PN 6508922) and the test lead 6358 (group IFLEX_SIM). `calibrator_fit`: all 38 outputs are inside the published 5522A, 5520A and 5560A sheets (the coil, its compliance and the iFlex input are not part of that check).
- Engine: ONE additive edit to the shared `handheld_dmm.py` (2 lines in `certificate_row`): an opt-in step key `unc_unit` ('V' on the five iFlex simulation steps only) is the unit the Uncertainty is worded in; without it the unit is `base_unit`, so every other spec behaves exactly as before (a test lists that no other spec has the key). Needed because those steps are sourced as AC volts (3 mV) and read in amps: without it the calibrator's AC volts uncertainty would print in amps ('7.6 µA' for 3 mV). TAR / TUR for them stay blank by the existing guard (`tartur.NATURAL_UNITS`: a tolerance in amps is never divided by an uncertainty in volts). `handheld_dmm.py` is fingerprinted by every handheld procedure, so they show 'Changed since verification' until re-verified (named here on purpose). Also the 376's own entries in the per-meter tables of `hookups.py` (METERS, AVAILABLE, EXTRA: `fluke376`; slice `hookups.py#fluke376`); a procedure package cannot ship either root file, so a package of this procedure needs this build installed first (see Package below).
- Uncertainty (owner decisions, same as the 365): the jaw and iFlex-with-coil points carry the REGULAR calibrator current uncertainty at the actual calibrator output (0.2 / 10 / 18 / 6 A), AC at its frequency, DC as it is: NOT multiplied by 50, without the coil; TAR / TUR from that unscaled value against the meter-reading tolerance printed in Table 1 (about 50 x more favourable than a like-for-like ratio, e.g. 18 A AC 50 Hz: 950 mA, TUR 19.4:1). The lookups are at the calibrator current, 18 A at most, below the 20.5 A where the lab CMC table's coil lines (coil-standard-2) start, so those rows never apply here (tested). The iFlex simulated points carry the calibrator's regular AC VOLTS uncertainty at the output as printed (3 mV: 7.6 µV; 750 mV at 500 Hz: 140 µV on a 5522A), TAR / TUR blank. Volts, mV, ohms and capacitance carry the usual uncertainty, TAR and TUR (capacitance 500 / 900 µF: TUR about 1.6 to 1.8:1 on a 5522A, the calibrator's 5.3 / 7.1 µF against tolerances of 9 / 13 µF).
- Prompts (existing mechanisms): function prompt on every dial change (the iFlex simulation and iFlex-on-coil points share the iFlex dial text, so one prompt), cable-change prompt only when the hook-up group changes, HIGH VOLTAGE confirmation ONCE per hook-up group (`hv_once_per_group`; the 6 points of 30 V and above, 500 V and 900 V, are all in VOHM; `hv_threshold_v` 30), reading window per point. Prompt counts per full run (cable + function + high voltage + reading; iFlex option ON / OFF): 374 = 8 + 7 + 1 + 28 = 44 / 5 + 6 + 1 + 19 = 31; 375 = 8 + 8 + 1 + 34 = 51 / 5 + 7 + 1 + 25 = 38; 376 and 376-II = 8 + 8 + 1 + 38 = 55 / 5 + 7 + 1 + 29 = 42 (with the per-point high-voltage rule of the other handhelds: 376 60 / 47). OFF removes the three iFlex cable changes (the iFlex leads, the iFlex loop on the coil at 0.2 A and at 10 / 18 / 6 A), the iFlex dial prompt and the nine readings. `,0Hz` on every DC output (tested for both calibrators), outputs to standby after each point, calibrator reset before the first point, set / read back / never retried, typed readings padded to the printed resolution (the 900 V rows are stored and shown as whole volts, 882 .. 919 and 886 .. 914, `printed_text` keeps the printed 882.0 .. 919.0 and 886.0 .. 914.0), settle 5 s on coil points, 10 s on capacitance (engineering choices).
- LIMITS CROSS-CHECK. The Calibration Manual prints no specifications ('Complete specifications are in the printed Users Manual'), so the accuracy used (`ACC` / the resolutions in the builder) is from the fluke.com product page of the 376, read 2026-10-23 through a text-summarising fetch (NOT the Users Manual, NOT a page image): AC V 1.5 % + 5 digits (20-500 Hz), DC V and mV 1 % + 5, ohms 1 % + 5, capacitance 1 % + 4, jaw AC A 2 % + 5 (10-100 Hz) and 2.5 % + 5 (100-500 Hz), jaw DC A 2 % + 5, iFlex 3 % + 5 (5-500 Hz); resolutions 0.1 V up to 600.0 V and 1 V up to 1000 V, 0.1 mV, 0.1 / 1 / 10 ohm, 0.1 / 1 µF, 0.1 A (999.9 A), iFlex 0.1 A up to 999.9 A and 1 A to 2500 A. UNVERIFIED: confirm in the 374 / 375 / 376 Users Manual. Result: every volts, mV, ohms, capacitance and AC amps limit is the FULL published accuracy to within half a display step; the iFlex simulation rows, DC amps 0.2 A and two iFlex-with-coil rows (0.2 A, 6 A) are HALF of it (2:1 margin); none is looser than the accuracy by more than half a step. Six rows differ and are stored AS PRINTED, flagged in the point note, `step.check` and the builder output: (a) 1-1, AC 10 V, 9.4 .. 10.7 V: the accuracy is +-0.65 V (9.35 .. 10.65), the printed limits are the two values rounded up (high 0.05 V looser, low 0.05 V tighter, asymmetric); (b) 1-3, AC 900 V, 882.0 .. 919.0 V: accuracy +-18.5 V at 1 V resolution (881.5 .. 918.5), both limits rounded up (high 0.5 V looser); the same limits are printed with one decimal although the 1000 V range shows whole volts; (c) 1-28 and 1-29, DC amps 10 A and 18 A (500 / 900 A): printed +-5.0 / +-9.0 A, the accuracy is +-10.5 / +-18.5 A (half 5.25 / 9.25): tighter than either; (d) 1-36 and 1-37, iFlex with coil 10 A and 18 A (500 / 900 A): printed +-7.0 / +-13.0 A, half of the accuracy is +-7.75 / +-13.75 A: tighter. Nothing was corrected. Observations, not changed: (e) 1-34 is 750 mV at 500 Hz for 2500 A, the same reading as 75 mV at 50 Hz: consistent (the iFlex coil voltage is proportional to frequency, 30 µV per A at 50 Hz, 300 µV per A at 500 Hz), not a misprint; (f) the manual's '18 A @ 5 0Hz' is a typo for 50 Hz; (g) the DC-volts block holds -500 V first (the extracted text shows it in the AC block); (h) 6 A at 440 Hz is judged at the jaw's 100-500 Hz accuracy (2.5 %), the limits fit it.
- UNVERIFIED (listed in the final report): the coil (50-turn 55XXA/COIL wiring, jaw placement, calibrator compliance at 18 A, 5522A LCOMP, coil heating; the coil data recorded in the coil sheet section below allows 11 A continuous and 20 A for 2 min and limits 65-440 Hz coil drive to 2.2 A (5500A) / 3 A (5520A), while Table 1 asks 18 A and 6 A at 440 Hz: the calibrator refuses an output it cannot make and the run stops); the iFlex hook-ups (which jack is the '2 mm jack on the far left', the loop placement on the coil); the dial: Figure 1 shows OFF, V~, V⎓ (mV in yellow), Ω with the continuity symbol (capacitance symbol in yellow), A~, A⎓, iFlex A~ (Hz in yellow on three positions), the yellow button that selects the alternate functions mV / capacitance is not named in the manual (the only yellow button drawn is ZERO) and the order of the positions is read from the figure; whether DC amps need ZERO (note on the first DC amps point); the ranges named in the points (derived from the published resolutions, Table 1 names none); the ohms limits' treatment of test lead resistance; the 10 s capacitance and 5 s coil settle times; the 5560A current and capacitance commands (existing registry, `verified` false); the hook-up pictures (supplied by the owner, none exists yet; names in `diagrams/README_fluke376.txt`); everything in the accuracy table above.
- Adjustment (Table 3, printed pp. 5-8, not built): the meter is opened (battery door, calibration sticker removed, 3.0 V supply on the battery terminals, jumper across two pads for 1 s), then per function the rotary switch, the calibrator output of Table 3 and the HOLD key per step C-00 .. C-38 (AC volts C-00 .. C-06 600 V and 300 V at 50 to 500 Hz, DC volts, ohms and capacitance C-07 .. C-22, AC amps C-23 .. C-29 with the coil, DC amps C-30 / C-31, iFlex simulation C-32 .. C-38), each block ended with 'Save' in standby. Front panel only: no remote interface.
- iFlex option (owner request: not every meter is received with its iFlex). `iflex_option.py` = `class App(handheld_dmm.App)` + `main()`; NO shared file is edited (the engine's own hooks are used: `build_points`, `execute`, `save_fields`, `load_results`, `report_header`, `source_note`, and the procedure-modification list is re-registered on every change). The spec declares it: `options.include_toggles = [{id 'iflex', label 'iFlex probe supplied: run the iFlex tests', default true, off_text 'not tested (no iFlex supplied)'}]` and tags the nine iFlex steps `include: 'iflex'` (1-30 .. 1-38, all four models). A spec without the option runs exactly as the engine. Where: a check box with a one-line status above the point table, before Run (real Tk screenshots below). Default ON (a meter with an iFlex gets every test; it is never skipped silently): turning it OFF asks first ('9 iFlex point(s) will not be run or asked for, and the report will state "not tested (no iFlex supplied)"'), then the nine points leave the point list: not run, not asked for, not counted (point numbers, progress and section list follow the shorter list), not shown as pending or failed; the prompts and counts are those under Prompts. It cannot be changed while a run is active (the box snaps back, the status line says why). Changing it NEVER discards a reading: an iFlex point that already has a recorded reading (As Found or As Left) stays in the list and in the report while the option is OFF (the status says '1 recorded earlier kept'); turning it back ON restores the missing points in manual order. Audit record: the progress (checkpoint) file stores `include` ({'iflex': false}) and `include_log` (every change: time, setting, readings recorded at that moment); a file saved before the option existed loads as ON; the change is saved at once when progress exists (never an empty progress file over an earlier one). The report header carries the line 'iFlex tests: Tested (iFlex supplied)' / 'Not tested (no iFlex supplied)' (with '; n reading(s) recorded before it was turned off are included' when that applies) and the report note repeats it; the report rows are the recorded points as for every handheld report, so the points that were not run have no row (the existing convention for unrecorded points). No cover sheet; demo marking unchanged (a demo run with the option OFF is still marked DEMO). RUN QUEUE (branch `fluke-376-queue`, owner-approved): the four procedures offer the run queue again, with a PER-METER iFlex setting; see "Run queue with a per-meter option" below.
- Run queue with a per-meter option (`fluke-376-queue`; simulated bench and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only, never run on hardware). Owner: each queue meter (asset) carries its own 'iFlex supplied' value, default ON.
  - Where it lives: `meter.extra['include'] = {'iflex': bool}` on the `QueueMeter` (`iflex_option.App._ensure_meter`), saved (a) in the queue file `<brand><model>_queue.json` as an `include` key next to asset / serial / tech and (b) in that meter's OWN checkpoint (`include`, `include_log`: the audit entries of that meter only). A new window / resume takes the setting from the meter's saved progress when that has readings, else from the queue file, else ON; a setting changed after the progress was saved wins and is saved at once (only when that meter has readings). 'Load progress' brings the setting of the file loaded.
  - Shown: the Connections drawer lists one check box per queue meter ('<asset>: iFlex supplied' with its point count and, when OFF, 'not tested (no iFlex supplied)'); the queue chip says 'iFlex ON for all / OFF for all / ON for 1 of 2'; the set-up list's Status column says 'iFlex ON/OFF' per meter; the window box above the point table is the setting of the meter SHOWN ('Asset 4412: ...'), so selecting a meter in the progress window switches it. Editing follows the single-meter rules: not while a run is active (the box snaps back and the status line says why), turning OFF asks first and names the asset, a recorded reading is never discarded (the point stays in that meter's list and report).
  - Planning: the window lists the UNION of the points any meter takes part in (the iFlex points are the tail, steps 1-30..1-38, so point numbers stay stable). A meter with iFlex OFF is left out of those points by the planner (`QueueMeter.wants`), so: ON/ON runs exactly the plan it always did (the five iFlex simulation points are volts, shared; the four coil points are current, one meter at a time); ON/OFF runs the iFlex points for the meter that has them only, as one single unit with the swap prompt 'connect only meter A' (the same mechanism that serves a resumed meter that still needs points); the cable / function / meter-setup / high-voltage prompts of a shared unit name only the meters wired for THAT point (3 meters, 1 OFF: the iFlex prompts name 2). Prompt and reading-window counts on the bench, two meters point by point, model 376: ON/ON cable 16, function 14, swap 4, HV 1, reading windows 59; ON/OFF 14, 12, 2, 1, 55; both OFF 46 reading windows. Setup summary: ON/OFF 38+29 = 67 settings one meter at a time, 55 point by point (12 shared, 26 one at a time); progress totals and 'Done' are per meter (38 / 29).
  - Reports: each meter's PDF carries its own header line 'iFlex tests: Tested (iFlex supplied)' or 'Not tested (no iFlex supplied)' (with '; n reading(s) recorded before it was turned off are included' when that applies) and the same sentence in the note; rows are that meter's recorded points (38 vs 29). Demo marking unchanged (a demo queue is marked DEMO whatever the setting; a demo queue file is never loaded by a real run).
  - Procedure modifications: unchanged rule; a stored edit for an iFlex point is an orphan while the setting is OFF and the modification layer warns (owner accepted). Limits, instrument commands, pass/fail logic and safety prompts are not touched.
  - Shared files touched (additive, behaviour identical for every other queue): `run_queue.py` (`QueueMeter.skip` / `extra` / `wants()`, `to_dict` / `load_dict` carry `extra`, `plan` asks `m.wants(p)` and a shared unit lists only the meters that take part in it, `summary` and `progress` count per meter); `run_queue_engine.py` (hooks `queue_prepare_meters`, `queue_total`; `queue_lacking`, the progress fraction and the per-meter 'Done' test use `wants`). Neither file is in any procedure's fingerprint list (`Procedures/*.procedure.json`), so no verified procedure flips to 'Changed since verification' because of them; the behaviour of the other handheld queues is covered by `tests/test_beta_run_queue.py` (unchanged, passing) and `tests/test_fluke_376_queue.py::SharedFilesStayNeutral`. `iflex_option.py` (in the fingerprint of the four 37x procedures only) holds the rest.
  - Limits of this build: the setting is edited in the Connections drawer and with the window box, not inside the 'Run queue...' set-up dialog (that dialog is a shared file and only lists it); 'Start' in that dialog runs with the settings as listed (default ON). Tests: `tests/test_fluke_376_queue.py` (38 tests); real-Tk views in `tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_fluke_376`.
- Package: `tools/make_package.py Fluke_374_Verification` (and 375, 376, 376II) ships the launcher, sidecar, spec, builder, diagram README and test; `handheld_dmm.py` (the `unc_unit` edit), `hookups.py` (the `fluke376` entries) and `iflex_option.py` are root files that cannot ship, so importing a package into a suite that does not have this build gives a procedure whose launcher cannot import `iflex_option`, whose hook-up dialog for the leads is missing and whose iFlex simulation uncertainty is worded in amps: install the FULL BUILD first.

## Coil standard sheet: the lab's A2LA CMC lines for the Fluke 5500A/COIL (specs/standards/a2la_5500a_coil_standard.json; branch `coil-standard`)
Owner request: 'create a standards accuracy entry for the fluke 5500 coil. It should be pointed to these lines of the scope.' Model `A2LA-5500A-COIL`, description 'Fluke 5500A/COIL (A2LA scope)'. Builder: `specs/build_coil_standard.py` (reproducible; `tests/test_standard_coil.py` rebuilds it and compares with the shipped JSON). Same pattern as the light sheet.
- Source, VERIFIED against the RENDERED PAGE IMAGES (pdftoppm; each value read twice, full page then a 200 dpi crop, 2026-10-23; the extracted text only located the pages). Cert. 1888.03 rev. 07/07/2026, section V. Electrical - DC/Low Frequency, column header `CMC 2, 5, 9 (+/-)`:
  - printed p. 9 of 47, 'DC Current - Generate', last block, NO frequency column (these are DC ranges), comment 'Transconductance amplifier/coil': (2 to 20) A 90 uA/A + 0.1 mA; (20 to 120) A 80 uA/A + 8 mA; (120 to 3000) A 0.8 % + 0.08 A.
  - printed p. 30 of 47, 'AC Current - Generate (cont)', sub-heading 'Clamp-On Only', range (20.5 to 1025) A, Frequency column, comment 'Multifunction calibrator/coil': (45 to 65) Hz 0.8 %; (65 to 440) Hz 0.99 %.
- Stored as five rows (functions `Current, DC [Transconductance amplifier/coil]`, kind DCI, and `Current, AC [Clamp-On Only, Multifunction calibrator/coil]`, kind ACI with freq_lo / freq_hi), period '1 yr' only, direction 'output'. 'Clamp-On Only' is the row qualifier through the bracket of the function name (the repo's `variant` field, as '[Sinewave, LCOMP off]' on the 5522A sheet): visible in the Standards Specs view, found by the text filter, selectable with `lookup(..., variant='Clamp-On Only')`. The numeric `qualifiers` list was not used (it holds value ranges).
- Terms in the repo's usual form, computed in Decimal and stored as the nearest float: 90 uA/A = 90 ppm = 9e-05 of reading (80 uA/A = 8e-05; binary `80 * 1e-6` gives 8.999999999999999e-05), 0.8 % = 0.008, 0.99 % = 0.0099; fixed terms 0.1 mA = 1e-04 A, 8 mA = 0.008 A, 0.08 A. The two AC rows have no fixed term (a bare percentage on the scope). The builder cross-checks its Decimal figures against `parse_spec` to 1e-12 and stops on a disagreement.
- Confidence: footnote 2, expanded uncertainty 'approximately the 95 % level, usually k = 2': `confidence_pct` 95, divisor 2 (`tests/test_standard_confidence.py` lists it). Footnote 5 (first percentage = of reading) and footnote 9 (percent of reading plus a fixed floor) are in sheet note 4.
- Coil datasheet (Fluke 5500A/COIL | 50-Turn Current Coil technical data, 10/2026, read from the rendered pages) goes into sheet notes 7 and 8 only: 50 turns; max current 11 A rms continuous, 20 A rms for 2 min (above 11 A: 2 min ON / 8 min OFF); 3 V rms; frequency of operation DC, 45-440 Hz (5500A output: DC and 45-65 Hz 0-11 A, 65-440 Hz 0-2.19999 A; 5520A output: DC and 45-65 Hz 0-20 A, 65-440 Hz 0-2.99999 A, assuming a Fluke 80i-600 clamp); jaws >= 2.54 cm. The clamp / coil INTERACTION uncertainty (+/-(0.25 % of effective output + 0.5 A) for toroidal-wound clamps such as the 80I and 80I-1000; +/-(0.50 % + 0.5 A) for the 80i-kw / 80i-400 / 80i-410 / 80i-500 / 80i-1010 / Fluke 31 / 33) is NOT added to the scope rows: the repo has no second-term mechanism for a standard and no combination rule was invented (owner decision pending).
- Wired (generic, additive): `sm.seed()` loads it (Standards Specs: viewed on real Tk 9 under Xvfb in light and dark: rail card, 95 % / divisor 2, chips DCI 3 / ACI 2, the five rows); a driver-less candidate in the Datasheet Builder matcher (`tests/test_datasheet_builder_match.py` lists it; where the scope file covers a current point the calibrator CMC floor still ties the TURs and `LAB_DEFAULT` keeps 5560A / 5522A, but a datasheet current point above 30.2 A, or an AC clamp point at 45-440 Hz above 30.2 A, that used to be 'uncovered' is now offered this sheet with automation 'manual'); `uncertainty_budget.build(kind, ..., standard='A2LA-5500A-COIL')` and `tartur.standard_accuracy` work through the generic lookup; the TAR / TUR of coil-range current points come from the lab CMC table, which now carries the coil lines (see the next section); `tartur.py` has no coil special case (the one added with the first version was dropped once the CMC table covered those ranges).
- NOT wired: the sheet is not in any calibrator picker and no procedure uses it. The pickers (`handheld_dmm.CAL_CHOICES`, `calibrator_fit`, `procmods`) choose the instrument that is COMMANDED, and the run's one `calibrator_model()` then names the standard of every point of the run; the coil is not commanded, so offering it there would be a driver change, not an additive one. Per-point standards (a coil point inside a run whose other points use the calibrator) have no machinery today. The Fluke 365 procedure (20-turn coil, 5522A / 5560A) is unchanged: limits, commands, pass/fail and the owner-chosen 'regular calibrator current uncertainty, unscaled' behaviour. Selecting the coil sheet for the 365's coil points would also be wrong as it stands: it is the 5500A/COIL with 50 turns, the 365 coil has 20.
- Display note (existing rule, left alone): the Standards Specs value span is rounded to 3 significant digits, so 1025 A shows as '1.03 kA' and 3000 A as '3.00 kA' in the Value span column; the Range column and the tooltip carry the exact printed '(20.5 to 1025) A'.
- Not stored on this sheet: the AC (20.5 to 3000) A amplifier/coil lines (sheet `A2LA-CURRENT-AMP-COIL`, next section), DC Power - Generate with current coil (p. 10), and the clamp meter's own contribution.
- Observation stored as a sheet warning, for the owner: the datasheet allows only 2.19999 A (5500A) / 2.99999 A (5520A) at 65-440 Hz, i.e. about 110 / 150 A effective, while the 65-440 Hz row is listed to 1025 A (1025 A = 20.5 A x 50 turns is an inference, not stated by the scope). Owner confirmed (2026-10-23) that the DC 'Transconductance amplifier/coil' rows ARE the 5500A/COIL's: no doubt is left in the notes.
- UNVERIFIED: the datasheet's date (10/2026) and that it matches the coil in the lab; nothing was run on hardware.

## Amplifier/coil sheet and coil lines in the lab CMC table (branch `coil-standard-2`; owner answers to the coil questions)
- New sheet `A2LA-CURRENT-AMP-COIL` ('Transconductance amplifier/coil (A2LA scope, AC current)'), builder `specs/build_current_amp_coil_standard.py`, test `tests/test_standard_current_amp_coil.py`. Source: cert. 1888.03 rev. 07/07/2026, printed p. 30 of 47, 'AC Current - Generate (cont)', range (20.5 to 3000) A, comment 'Transconductance amplifier/coil': (16 to 850) Hz 0.7 % + 0.84 A; (0.85 to 6) kHz 0.8 % + 0.84 A. VERIFIED on the rendered page (two crops at 170 and 260 dpi, 2026-10-23); the whole block pp. 29-30 was checked for other 'coil' lines: there are none ('coil' occurs only on pp. 9, 10 and 30). Stored as two ACI rows, bands 16-850 Hz and 850-6000 Hz (printed 0.85 to 6 kHz kept in the label), rel 0.007 / 0.008, abs 0.84 A, period '1 yr', 95 % k = 2. No interaction uncertainty (that belongs to the 5500A/COIL datasheet and is still undecided; nothing is combined anywhere).
- Lab CMC table `specs/a2la_1888_03_cmc.json` (read by `uncertainty.py`): the 5500A/COIL DC rows (p. 9) were appended as three bands of the 'DC Current - Generate' entry, and the clamp-on rows (45-65 Hz 0.8 %, 65-440 Hz 0.99 %, (20.5 to 1025) A) and the amplifier/coil rows ((20.5 to 3000) A, 0.7 % + 0.84 A, 0.8 % + 0.84 A) as two blocks of the 'AC Current - Generate' entry. Scope CMC lookups above 30.2 A (DC to 3000 A, AC 20.5 to 3000 A inside the frequency bands) are therefore covered; TAR / TUR for such points are no longer blank. The file had no builder (prepared by hand); `specs/build_a2la_cmc_coil_rows.py` appends the rows idempotently and rewrites the file in its own format (json indent 1, no final newline) so the earlier text is untouched except the appended rows and a sentence added to the two entries' notes / standard text and to `source`.
- Existing rows byte-identical: `tests/test_a2la_cmc_coil_rows.py` compares sha256 hashes of every old entry (and of the first 15 DC bands / first 16 AC range blocks) with the values of the file before the change, and the serialised file against the builder.
- Procedure numbers unchanged (tested): the overlap rule of the table is 'larger CMC of overlapping bands'. DC: the coil DC rows are never larger than the calibrator CMC they overlap (2 to 30.2 A), so only DC above 30.2 A changes (None -> covered). AC: below 20.5 A nothing changes (dense grid, all frequencies); from 20.5 A up to 120 A the new 0.7 % + 0.84 A row (larger) now applies on top of the amplifier rows, so an AC current point of 20.5 to 120 A takes that larger CMC (it is the scope's own coil line for that range). No shipped spec point is in that range: every ACI / DCI point of every `specs/*.json` was compared before / after, and the Fluke 365 current points (calibrator output 0.5 / 5 / 9.5 A, 20 turns, meter 10 / 100 / 190 A) are looked up at the applied calibrator current as before, unscaled, with an identical CMC; the 365 procedure itself, its limits and its uncertainty behaviour were not touched.
- The model list tests were updated (`tests/test_datasheet_builder_match.py`, `tests/test_standard_confidence.py`). Real-Tk view of the new sheet under Xvfb, light and dark.
- Owner answers recorded: DC amplifier/coil rows ARE for the 5500A/COIL (kept as stored, doubt removed from the sheet notes and warnings); no per-point standard choice (nothing done); the interaction uncertainty stays out of everything until decided.
- Display note: the Standards Specs span rounds to 3 significant digits ('3.00 kA', '1.03 kA' for 1025 A); the range column keeps the printed text.

## Instruments tab removed from the Manager (branch `remove-instruments-tab`; owner request: assets are tracked in other software; UI removal only)
- Removed from `Calibration_Procedure_Manager.py`: the 'Instruments' tab (`instruments_frame`, `build_instruments_tab`, `refresh_instruments`, search, `New instrument...` / Edit / Delete dialogs, 'Launch assigned procedure' / Demo, `selected_instrument`, `launch_instrument_procedure`) and its `_tab_changed` branch / status text / empty state. The Manager still opens on Procedures (first tab). Tabs then: Procedures, Asset History, Standards Specs, Mudcats Datasheet Importer, Datasheet Builder, Procedure Development (the last one was removed later, see 'Procedures tab: Run at the top').
- NOT changed (standing rule, schema never changes): `init_db()` still creates / migrates the `instruments` table in calibration_procedures.sqlite3 and the duplicate-procedure migration still re-points `instruments.procedure_id`; no data deleted. `labregistry.py` still READS that table (serial number / interval) for a procedure window's Instrument ID lookup and 'next due'; with no way to add rows in the UI, that lookup only finds rows already in a person's database. `Manager._launch(..., asset=)` and the procedures' `--asset` argument stay (nothing passes it now). Procedure delete (Procedures tab) is unchanged (`ON DELETE SET NULL` on the unused column).
- Docs: README.txt (old note about Instruments 'Last report / Next due' columns), USER_GUIDE.txt (running a verification), comment in `labregistry.attach`.
- Tests: `tests/test_manager_tabs.py::test_manager_has_no_instruments_tab_but_keeps_the_instruments_table` (tab list from source, names gone, table creation kept); `checks_manager_tabs` in `tests/real_tk_checks.py` (real Tk: tab list, opens on Procedures, old attributes gone, a seeded `instruments` row and the table's `CREATE` sql identical before / after the Manager starts). Screenshot of the tab strip (light / dark): `manager_tabstrip_{light,dark}.png` in `$SHOT_DIR`, viewed on Tk 9 under Xvfb.
- Not run / known: on main (before this change) `real_tk_checks.py` already stops in `checks_mdd_manual` ('import again: cancel creates nothing') in this sandbox, so the checks after it were not run here.

### DSB run-time rules implemented (branch `dsb-runtime-rules`; simulated bench and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only, never run on hardware)
Run-time rules 6 - 13 for 2-way automated Datasheet Builder sheets (`dsb_runtime.DsbApp` / `DsbIO`, `Procedures/User_Datasheet.py --spec`). The rules text itself is in the rules section (other branch); this lists what the runtime does. New DSB-only modules: `dsb_rules.py` (pure logic: section modes, start-state probes, stable-reading settle), `dsb_override.py` (per-point override). No driver and no handheld engine file was edited.
- Rule 6 (already complied): writes go through the drivers' set / read back / compare, never retried; the error queue is read after the output command and OPERATE is read back. Test: `tests/test_dsb_runtime_rules.py::Rule6Writes`.
- Rule 7: before the first point of a Run `dsb_rules.run_probes` runs every read-only probe the drivers declare (`start_state_checks()` -> `[Probe]`; a driver that declares nothing is not checked). The 8808A is declared by an adapter in `dsb_rules.ADAPTERS` (it reuses the driver's own prompt-tolerant `identify` / `ask`; echo / prompts; identity skipped when the driver says `IDN_UNAVAILABLE`; the remote state cannot be read back and is listed as NOT CHECKED). A failed probe stops the Run before any output with plain instructions; the log lines are `start-state: ...`.
- Rule 8: a reading of the 5790B or the counter is polled (each poll one read-only query) until N consecutive readings agree within a band; the point's fixed settle time is the minimum, a timeout is the cap, the LAST reading is recorded (no averaging), the outcome is a `SETTLE` line in the command log (a timeout is a `!!` line and the reading is still judged by the limits). Tunables `dsb.stable.n` (3), `dsb.stable.band_counts` (3 counts of the limits' resolution, never more than a tenth of the tolerance), `dsb.stable.timeout` (30 s) and per-point `dsb.point.stable_*`, all labelled 'UNVERIFIED estimate'; they appear in the Edit-procedure window. The 0.2 s pause between polls is this program's choice.
- Rule 9: standby after every point and on stop / error / window close (already); one HIGH VOLTAGE question per hook-up group (was per point; `hv_once_per_group`, injected at run time, a copy of the spec); the generator sheet's 'Connect' step only when the hook-up changes; dial prompt on every function change (already).
- Rule 10: `RESET_ONCE_PER_SESSION = True`: the first Run resets (with the unit-under-test notice), later Runs only check the connection and standby.
- Rule 11 (already complied): progress saved after every point, datasheet match check on resume; the command log is now also saved before the 'Measuring standard' dialog.
- Rule 12: each section is Automated / Typed by hand / Not tested (combobox in the bar above the test points, acts on the section chosen on the left, sidebar shows `(hand)` / `(skip)`); saved in the progress file (`dsb.sections`); a Not-tested section is never run, asked, counted, pending or failed and the report lists it as 'Not tested' (the existing skipped convention; a point recorded before it was switched off still counts and prints); a hand section uses the manual calibrations' typed entry, padding and judging (`manual_grid.GridEntry` asks `app.grid_allows`, with `procmods_engine.py`'s additive `dsb` tunables, the only edits to shared files besides the tests; neither is in a verified procedure's fingerprint). A manual calibration starts all Typed by hand.
- Rule 13: right-click a point > 'Enter reading manually...' (`manual_override.ManualOverrideMixin`, as the 8808A). No note on the report; trace only in the command log (`MANUAL OVERRIDE point ...: auto reading X, entered Y`) and the progress file (`overridden`, `auto_reading`). The route that was used stays on the record, so a report with an override is identical to one without.
- Tests: `tests/test_dsb_runtime_rules.py`; the two 'only the 8808A opts in' tests now also allow the datasheet runtime; `tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_dsb_sections`. Looked at on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb in light and dark (section control, sidebar labels, a Not-tested section).
- UNVERIFIED: the poll interval and the three tunable defaults; that the 8808A adapter's echo / prompt probe behaves the same on the real RS-232 meter as on its simulator; no SCPI command was added or changed.

## Datasheet Builder: Low / High limits instead of a +/- tolerance (branch `dsb-low-high-limits`; Mike: 'display a low limit and a high limit'; DISPLAY change; simulated bench, stub tests and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb, light and dark; never seen on Windows)
- What the owner sees: wherever the builder showed a symmetric `±x`, it shows the two ABSOLUTE limits, e.g. `9.9500 V` and `10.0500 V`, worked out by the unchanged `Function.limit_for` (exact Decimal, nominal -/+ tolerance), shown with `GridModel.fmt_value` (same exact digits, SI prefix choice, range display unit and zero padding to the range's resolution as the nominal cell). A side with no limit says `open`. A pass / fail row says `Pass / fail` / `Beeper on|off` in the Low cell.
- What is stored does NOT change: a tolerance STATEMENT (`SpecEntry` / `Point.spec`, symmetric or with a different minus side) or a typed Low / High pair (`Point.overrides[period] = {low, high}`, absolute SI strings). No schema change, no new field. The conversion happens only at the edge (the cell / box the person types in).
- Changed (display only): (1) grid: `VIEW_COLUMNS` = STEP NUM, FUNCTION TESTED, NOMINAL VALUE, LOW LIMIT, HIGH LIMIT (`COL_KEYS` / `EDIT_COLS` / Tab / `GridGeometry` follow; no `tol` column any more); row dicts carry `low`, `high`, `formula` (the statement the limits came from, '' when typed) and `limits_from` ('formula' | 'typed' | ''); a tolerance problem marks both limit cells. (2) printed sheet / Preview / PDF: `GRID_COLUMNS` ends with LOW LIMIT, HIGH LIMIT instead of CALIBRATION TOLERANCE (8 columns; As Found / As Left / Out of Tol still blank). (3) Inspector, section 'Limits and tolerance' (was 'Tolerance'): Low limit / High limit boxes first; read-only while calculated; the checkbox 'Type the limits myself' [REMOVED, see below] made them the row's limits and HID the tolerance boxes; the chips / boxes / Asymmetrical tolerance / Calc are the formula editor and unchanged. (4) Worked example (`EditorModel.example`, Specification table page) says 'the low limit is 4.9965 V and the high limit is 5.0035 V' (the per-term amounts in `parts` are the breakdown of the statement being edited and are still amounts). (5) messages: Calc says `Accept if ... Low limit x, High limit y.`; `point_rows()['tol_text']` / `db.point_limits()['tol_text']` are now `Function.limits_text()` ('Low limit 4.9965 V, High limit 5.0035 V  (from 0.05 % of reading + ...)', '(typed limits)' for a typed pair). (6) Run window: `handheld_dmm.limit_line(p)` words the 'Limit:' line of a Datasheet Builder step (it has a 'dsb' block) as 'Low limit .., High limit ..  (from <accuracy statement>)' using the step's own display strings; shipped specs keep their own tol_text. The run grid already had Low limit / High limit columns and NO Error column for handhelds (`results_table(error=False)`), so nothing changed there; the measured 'Error' column of the engines that have one is a measurement and is untouched. (7) Report: the rows already carried the absolute limits under the keys `Tolerance -` / `Tolerance +` (unchanged: snapshots and history use them); a Datasheet Builder report (`DsbApp.report_details` sets `details['limit_labels']`) now prints the headings Low Limit / High Limit (`report_pdf.LIMIT_HEADINGS`); every other procedure's report is exactly as before.
- [SUPERSEDED by 'Datasheet Builder: limits only from the tolerance calculator' below: the limit cells are read-only and `_edit_limit`, 'Type the limits myself' and `is_statement` are gone] Editing paths (Edit Cell bar and typing in the sheet; `GridModel._edit_limit`): a plain quantity in Low / High types THAT limit (bare number = base unit like the nominal, `4950 mV` fine) and keeps the other limit's current value, writing the typed pair for the period on show (the statement, if any, stays but loses to the pair); `low to high` types both; a tolerance statement (`±5 mV`, `0.05 % of reading + 2 counts`, `max(..)`; recognised by `GridModel.is_statement`: leading ± / +/-, a % or ppm, reading / range / counts words) sets the row's statement exactly as the old CALIBRATION TOLERANCE cell did and drops the period's typed pair; Delete clears the tolerance (both limits); typing the number a limit already has is a no-op (Enter on a calculated cell never turns it into typed limits). A row that ends with one typed limit and one `open` gets the error `limit_open` (the run needs both). OLD BEHAVIOUR THAT CHANGED: a bare `20 µV` typed in the old tolerance cell meant ±20 µV; in a limit cell it is a limit (type `±20 µV`). Inspector: Calc with 'Type the limits myself' off = the old path (boxes -> statement, `_set_spec`); on = both limit boxes (bare numbers in the Units box) -> typed pair, both required; 'Several rows at once' needs the statement (refused with typed limits). 'Copy tolerance to rows below' copies the statement (not typed limits).
- Frozen on purpose: `Function.tol_text` and the spec step's `tol_text` stay byte for byte (`±(...) = ±3.5 mV`): `procmods._HASH_FIELDS` hashes `tol_text` into every point's identity, so changing it would make existing procedure modifications of user-built sheets 'stale'. It is not shown anywhere now (the run window words the limits itself from `low` / `high`). Not touched: `limit_for`, `Limit`, `validate`, `export_spec`, `judge_numeric`, the reports' numbers, `calibration_procedures.sqlite3`.
- Golden check: `tests/dsb_golden.py` snapshots, for 8 sheets (the four samples, an asymmetric one, typed Low / High pairs, row statements, outward rounding) and every period, each point's exact limit, every step of the runnable spec and the engine's PASS / FAIL for readings just below / on / just above each limit; `tests/dsb_golden_limits.json` (digests) was written on origin/main BEFORE this change and `tests/test_dsb_low_high_limits.py::Golden` compares today's code with it. The full (not digest) snapshots before and after are byte-identical.
- Tests: tests/test_dsb_low_high_limits.py (new: golden, every sample row's cells equal the core limits, 9.9500 / 10.0500 example, asymmetric / typed shown as they are, every editing path above, Inspector path, run-window wording, report headings only when asked), updated tests/test_dsb_grid_model.py, test_dsb_ui_model.py, test_dsb_ui_widgets.py (+ the Inspector boxes), test_dsb_mock_workflow.py (hand arithmetic: 4.970 / 5.030 V, 49.745 / 50.255 V, 9.8999 / 10.1001 mA), test_dsb_runtime_bench.py (PDF headings), tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_dsb_layout (five columns; boxes read-only / typed / hidden tolerance boxes).
- Unverified: Windows fonts; the Inspector's typed mode was built after the owner's mockup B was chosen (it is a new control, not in the mockup); a limit-cell bare number is in the BASE unit (same as the nominal cell) - a different choice (the unit shown in the cell) is a one-line change in `_edit_limit`.


## Datasheet Builder: limits only from the tolerance calculator (branch `dsb-limits-calculator-only`; owner decisions on the Low / High display; simulated bench, stub tests and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb, light and dark; never seen on Windows)
- Why: the owner never asked for typed limits. EVERYTHING goes through the tolerance calculator (Inspector, section Limits and tolerance: the chips / boxes, Asymmetrical tolerance, Calc, and the row's tolerance statement). The LOW LIMIT / HIGH LIMIT cells are READ-ONLY displays of the limits worked out by the unchanged `Function.limit_for` (still absolute values; `open`, `no limit`, `Pass / fail`, `Beeper on|off` wording as before).
- REMOVED: the 'Type the limits myself' check box (`Inspector.typed_cb`, `b['typed']`, `typed_changed`), the typed Calc branch (`form['typed']`, `form['low']` / `['high']` are display only now), the `typed` refusal in 'Several rows at once', and in `GridModel` `_edit_limit`, `_typed_pair`, `_STATEMENT`, `is_statement`. `VIEW_COLUMNS` low / high are `edit=False`, so Tab skips them (like As Found); `EDIT_COLS` = function, nominal.
- Typing in a limit cell (Edit Cell bar, a printable key, Enter / F2, Delete): `cell_editable` answers (False, `LIMITS_HINT`) = 'Limits come from the tolerance calculator: use "Limits and tolerance" in the row panel and press Calc.' It goes to the status line like the other read-only cells (As Found, STEP NUM) and the Edit Cell bar stays read-only. Chosen over jumping into the Inspector (least surprising: no focus jump, the panel may be hidden). Delete on a limit cell no longer clears the tolerance (clear it in the calculator / delete the row).
- Stored data is untouched and still read: `Point.overrides[period] = {low, high}` (old sheets) is judged, run and printed exactly as before (`tests/dsb_golden_limits.json` digests still match; `tol_text` frozen; no change to `limit_for`, `set_override`, `_HASH_FIELDS`, stored dicts). A row with a stored pair shows `limits_from = 'typed'`: the grid draws a small muted `typed` marker in the limit cells (the value text is shortened to make room), the Inspector note says 'This row has limits that were typed earlier ...' and shows a **Clear typed limits** button (`GridModel.clear_typed_limits`, one undo step, removes the pair of the period on show only; the pairs of other periods are not touched; with no tolerance statement the limits are then empty until Calc). **Calc** on such a row builds the statement from the boxes and replaces the pair (`_set_spec` pops it, as it always did) and says so ('The typed limits of this row were replaced by the calculated ones.'); Calc with no tolerance ticked errors and keeps the pair. A stored pair with only one side is still the error `limit_open` (the run needs both), worded for the calculator now.
- Bare number in a NOMINAL cell = the unit shown: `GridModel.typing_prefix(fn, p)` is the prefix the row's own nominal cell shows (range display unit, else the function's, else the automatic prefix of the row's value; `_prefix_for`), `bare_unit()` is that with the symbol ('mV') or '' when it is the base unit. `parse_nominal(fn, text, bare_unit)` appends it to a bare number in the value part only; `5 V`, `4950 mV`, `5 @ 1000` (frequency stays in Hz) are untouched. Typing `5` on a row shown in mV gives `5 mV` (padded to the range resolution as before, e.g. `+5.0000 mV`). A row that does not exist yet has no range: only the FUNCTION's display unit counts (else base unit, as before); the Inspector's Units box for a new row (`blank_form`) now starts from the same unit. Value stored as the exact SI number either way (display only).
- Consistency of the other typed-value paths: Edit Cell bar and typing in the sheet = `edit_cell` (changed as above); Inspector Nominal / Units boxes = unchanged, they already read a bare number in the unit shown in the Units box (for an existing row the Units box is the unit the nominal cell shows); Frequency / Signal level, Floor, Resolution, F.S. boxes unchanged (their own Units logic); Test point calculator dialogs and the Ranges dialog are separate editors and unchanged. A ZERO row is displayed without a prefix (`0.0000000 V`), so a bare number on it is in V (what the cell shows); say so if it should follow the range.
- Period: nothing in the Builder asks for a period any more. The 'Tolerances for' COMBOBOX on the sheet bar is replaced by a plain label (shown only when the sheet has more than one period, naming the period on show); `period_chosen` is deleted. The limits shown are the sheet's default period's (`ds.default_period`, '1y' for every new sheet; `GridModel.period` follows it as before). KEPT: `GridModel.set_period`, the periods in the data model and stored data (all periods' statements and pairs), 'Details and time periods...' page (add / remove / set default period / names), the Specification table page's per-period columns, `set_default_period`. Nothing was deleted from a sheet.
- Re-hash: no change to any `procmods` hash field. Verified-status check: no `Procedures/*.procedure.json` fingerprint lists `datasheet_builder*` or `dsb_*`; the definition hash of all 40 shipped procedures computed on origin/main and on this branch is identical (`Registry.file_components` + `definition_hash`); `verified.USER_CODE` (`Procedures/User_Datasheet.py`, `dsb_runtime.py`) not edited, so no user-built datasheet flips either.
- Tests: `tests/test_dsb_low_high_limits.py` (LimitCellsAreReadOnly, StoredTypedLimitsStillWork, InspectorPath, BareNumberIsInTheUnitShown + the golden / display / run-window / report tests as they were), `test_dsb_grid_model.py` (Tab skips the limits), `test_dsb_ui_widgets.py` (hint on typing, marker / Clear, no typed_cb, label not picker), `tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_dsb_layout` (no check box, limit boxes read-only with the tolerance boxes always shown, key typed in a limit cell -> hint and nothing edited, `5` on a mV row -> 5 mV via the Edit Cell bar, stored pair marked + Clear button, light and dark).
- Unverified: Windows fonts / DPI for the `typed` marker; the rest of `tests/real_tk_checks.py` stops at `import again: cancel creates nothing` on this Linux box with and without this change (pre-existing, unrelated), so the Datasheet Builder checks were run on their own (`checks_dsb_*`).

## Find instruments (branch `find-instruments`; owner-approved design; simulated bench / fake ResourceManager and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb, light and dark; never run against real GPIB / USB hardware or a real VISA library)
- What it is: a Manager tool, button 'Find instruments...' in the bottom bar of the Manager (every tab; `ui.dock_button`, the same place the procedure windows dock their buttons). It asks each GPIB / USB / LAN instrument who it is and shows `GPIB1::22::INSTR  Keysight 3458A  3458A  serial -` (resource, the instrument's own reply, the suite's model key, serial, status). The technician can Copy the resource string and press 'Accept and remember'. Nothing is connected for use by this window. Files: `instrument_scan.py` (Tk-free: scan, matching, memory), `instrument_scan_ui.py` (the window; `open_find_instruments(parent)` and the chooser `ask_resource(parent, model)`), tests `tests/test_instrument_scan.py` (63 tests, fake ResourceManager), `tests/real_tk_find_instruments.py` + `tests/test_instrument_scan_real_tk.py` (real Tk subprocess, light and dark; a separate file so `real_tk_checks.py` is untouched). The only shared-file edit is 5 lines in `Calibration_Procedure_Manager.py` (the dock button and `Manager.find_instruments`) and this section / one README line.
- What a scan sends (rules in the module docstring; `tests/test_instrument_scan.py::ReadOnly` proves them with a fake that records every call): ONLY read-only queries. `assert_read_only` refuses anything that is not one query ending in '?' (no ';', no newline, no `*RST`, `*CLS`, `*SAV`, `*RCL`, `*TRG`, `*PSC`, `*SRE`, `*ESE`, `*OPC` without the '?'), so no write can leave the module; the fake asserts `writes == []`, no `clear()`, and no GTL / `control_ren` unless the technician ticks 'Send GTL afterwards' (off by default: a procedure window may still hold the instrument, and GTL drops a calibrator to local). No `*CLS`, no `*RST`, no device clear. Probes per interface, in order: GPIB `*IDN?` then `ID?` (the 3458A speaks HP Multimeter Language and has no `*IDN?`); USB and TCPIP INSTR `*IDN?`. A timeout is retried once on the LAST probe of a resource only (`*IDN?` is not repeated before `ID?`); any other error is not retried. Timeout 2 s per query, 3 s to open. A remembered 3458A is asked with `ID?` only (`Scanner(preferred=...)`, `Scanner.verify` uses the family's own query only), so a known 3458A is never sent `*IDN?`.
- Side effect that cannot be avoided: any GPIB query puts the addressed instrument into REMOTE (front panel keys locked on a 34401A, 3458A etc.) until local is selected or GTL is sent; and an unknown GPIB instrument that does not answer `*IDN?` and is then sent `ID?` (or the 3458A that is sent `*IDN?` first on a first scan) gets a syntax-error entry in its own error queue. Nothing else changes. UNVERIFIED on hardware (never run on a real bus).
- Serial (ASRL) is never probed blind: listed (VISA ASRL names plus pyserial ports, `COM9` -> `ASRL9::INSTR`) and only checked when the technician picks the port and the instrument type in the 'Serial (RS-232) ports' box; `serial_identity` then opens the port with the DRIVER'S OWN serial settings (`Fluke8808A.SERIAL` 9600 8N1 LF / CR LF; 5560A / 5522A / 5790B / Keithley 2000 their `SERIAL_DEFAULT`; 34970A 57600; ...) and calls the driver's own `identify()` after `open(verify_model=False)` (the 8808A's is the prompt-tolerant one; nothing else is sent: test `SerialPorts`, 8808A simulator saw only `*IDN?`), then closes. A remembered serial resource is never verified automatically (`default_resource` answers '' for it). The check refuses a family that has no RS-232 identity code here (3458A, 33xxx, 3453x counters, scopes).
- Matching (`FAMILIES`, 28 model keys): 5560A, 5522A, 5520A, 5502A, 5500A, 5790B, 8808A, 8846A, 34401A, 34410A, 34411A, 34460A / 61A / 65A / 70A, 34970A, 34972A, 33210A, 33220A, 33250A, 53131A, 53132A, 96270A, Keithley 2000, 3458A, MDO4000C (MDO4xxxC), TDS2000C (TDS 2xxxC and TDS 1xxxC-EDU), WS4104HD (WS4xxxHD). The model field of the reply (upper case, spaces removed; `HEWLETT-PACKARD, 53132A` trimmed) is matched by a regular expression; `Family.source` says where each reply form was read. `tests/test_instrument_scan.py::Identity` checks that a sample reply of every family also contains the driver's own `IDN_MATCH` text (a model this table accepts is not then refused by its driver). The 8808A, 8846A etc. rows exist so the explicit serial check can name them; a GPIB 8846A is matched too.
- Duplicates: same model, same serial on two resources (e.g. GPIB and USB) = one instrument, the second row says 'Same instrument as ...' and cannot be remembered; same model with different serials = both rows say 'choose by serial'; two 3458As (no serial in `ID?`) = both flagged and the technician chooses by resource. Chooser mode (`ask_resource`) returns only an accepted, identified, non-alias row.
- Memory: NEW table `instrument_resources(workstation, model, serial, resource, idn, last_seen, PRIMARY KEY(workstation, model, serial))` in `lab_registry.sqlite3` (created by the first Remember; reading never creates a file; damaged file reads as empty). `workstation` = `<OS user>@<computer name>`. `calibration_procedures.sqlite3` is not opened (test compares the bytes). Re-checked each session: `default_resource(model)` returns a remembered resource ONLY after `Scanner.verify` (one read-only query, the family's own) confirms the same model (and the same serial when one was saved); a renumbered adapter (`GPIB1::22` now answers something else or nothing) gives '' ; the confirmation is cached per process (`clear_session_cache`). The window's Status column shows 'remembered' or 'address changed (was GPIB0::22::INSTR)' after each scan.
- GPIB names and back ends: no board number is assumed. NI-VISA / Keysight VISA are used through `pyvisa.ResourceManager()`, falling back to `'@py'` (pyvisa-py). When the back end lists a GPIB interface (`GPIBn::INTFC`) with no instruments on it, or the technician ticks 'Ask every GPIB address 0-30', the addresses 0-30 of that board are asked one by one (`GPIBn::a::INSTR`; an empty address fails at open / on the first query with 'no listeners' and is not shown, only counted). pyvisa-py without linux-gpib / gpib-ctypes lists no GPIB at all: the window says so. No pyvisa / no VISA library: a plain notice (`ScanError`), no traceback. The note about board numbering (NI usually GPIB0; Keysight IO Libraries uses the interface ID of Connection Expert, a USB-GPIB adapter is often GPIB1 or higher; linux-gpib follows gpib.conf) is shown when the all-address probe runs.
- The window never blocks: `ScanJob` runs the scan in a daemon thread; the window polls; Cancel returns at once (a call already inside VISA is abandoned and its result dropped, and the thread ends when VISA returns). 'Show what was sent' lists every query and reply of the scan.
- AUDIT: wiring the procedure windows' connection dropdowns. There is NO shared UI helper for the connection dropdown: `ui_theme.py` / `ui_shell.py` have none; every engine builds its own Combobox and its own `find_instruments()` (which only lists `list_resources()`), and every one of those files is in the sidecar fingerprints of the procedures that use it. So NO engine, launcher or driver was edited (`tests/test_instrument_scan.py::Wiring` also checks that no sidecar fingerprint lists `instrument_scan*`). Not touched either: `drivers/base.py` / `drivers/transport.py` (40 sidecars list both). To wire the dropdowns later, each engine needs a hook of 3 lines in its existing `find_instruments` (and one line to fill the default at start):
  ```
  import instrument_scan_ui                                     # inside find_instruments(self)
  picked = instrument_scan_ui.ask_resource(self.root, model=MODEL)    # '' = closed without choosing; remembers an accepted one
  if picked: self.dmm_addr.set(picked)                         # the engine's own variable (cal_addr for the calibrator)
  # default at start:  addr = instrument_scan.default_resource(MODEL)   # '' unless a quick read-only check confirms the remembered one
  ```
  Engine files with the dropdown code and how many sidecar procedures list each (the verified ones among them would show 'Changed since verification'; which of them are marked verified is in the owner's registry, not in the repo; the seed is Fluke 117, TDS2000C, WaveSurfer): `handheld_dmm.py` 19 (Fluke 110/113/114/115/116/117/175/177/179/287/289/365/374/375/376/376II, 77IV, 87V, Klein MM325; also the base of every user-built datasheet), `truevolt_verification.py` 4 (34460A/61A/65A/70A), `dacq_verification.py` 2 (34970A/72A), `fgen_verification.py` 3 (33210A/33220A/33250A), `agilent34410a_verification.py` 2 (34410A/34411A), `keithley2000_verification.py` 1, `mdo4000c_verification.py` 1, `tds2000c_verification.py` 1, `fluke8808a_verification.py` 1 (its button is 'Refresh ports'; RS-232, so the hook would be the serial check). That is 34 procedures in 9 files. Six more procedures build their address boxes inside their own launcher (plain Entry, no Find button): `Agilent_34401A.py`, `Fluke_8846A_Verification.py`, `Fluke_8846A_Adjustment.py`, `Keithley_2000_Adjustment.py`, `Fluke_8808A_Adjustment.py`, `WaveSurfer_WS4104HD.py` (6 more = 40 of 40). User-built datasheets run on `handheld_dmm.App` (`dsb_runtime.DsbApp`), so they inherit the old button; `verified.USER_CODE` = `Procedures/User_Datasheet.py` + `dsb_runtime.py` are in a user-built procedure's fingerprint too, so a hook in `dsb_runtime.py` would flip verified user-built datasheets (and that file is being edited on other branches): it was NOT wired. Net effect now: the Manager tool only; nothing flips.
- UNVERIFIED: (1) every identity reply form marked 'assumed' in `Family.source` (only the 5522A p. 6-19, 5790B p. 6-19, 53131A/53132A p. 4-130, 3458A p. 276 and the Keithley 2000 and 96270A forms quoted in the driver notes were read; the 33210A/33220A/33250A, 34401A, 34410A/11A, Truevolt, 34970A/72A, 5560A, 5520A/5502A/5500A, 8808A, 8846A, MDO4000C, TDS2000C and WaveSurfer reply texts were NOT read in a manual here - their drivers' `IDN_MATCH` substrings are what the table is tested against); (2) that `ID?` reaches the 3458A through VISA with EOI / LF termination without a read-termination setting (the 3458A driver sets LF at connect; the scan sets none: if a real 3458A answers but pyvisa waits for a terminator the scan shows 'no reply'); (3) that an empty GPIB address fails fast on NI-VISA, Keysight VISA and pyvisa-py/linux-gpib (the scan assumes a 'no listeners' error at open or on the first query and bounds it by the 2 s timeout otherwise: 31 addresses could then take up to a minute; Cancel works); (4) the REMOTE-state and error-queue side effects above on each model; (5) that 'Send GTL' behaves as the transport's `go_local` does (same call); (6) the wording of VISA errors matched for 'timeout' / 'listeners'; (7) the 3458A reports no serial number here (`ID?` has none), so a second 3458A is chosen by resource only; (8) the dialog on Windows (fonts, DPI) - seen on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only. No SCPI command was added to any driver, no instrument command, limit, pass / fail rule or safety prompt was changed.
- Tests: `python -m unittest tests.test_instrument_scan` (63; fake ResourceManager with IDN / ID? replies, timeouts, an erroring resource, duplicates, ASRL never opened, writes never sent, GPIB board probing, back ends, ScanJob cancel, memory, default_resource) and `tests.test_instrument_scan_real_tk` (skipped without tkinter and a display). Screenshots (light / dark) were taken with `FIND_SHOTS=<folder>` set for `tests/real_tk_find_instruments.py` (the window before a scan, results, remembered, 'what was sent', serial check, scanning, no VISA, in the Manager).

## Procedures tab: Run at the top, scrolling, row menu (branch `run-buttons-top`; presentation only; viewed on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb at 1267x860 and 900x600, light and dark; never seen on Windows)
- Run controls (revised, branch `run-in-card`; the big Run row under the view strip was removed on the owner's request, draft board Card.dc.html): `Run` (`AccentCompact.TButton`, new compact primary style in ui_theme) and `Try in demo mode` sit side by side in the Selected procedure card, with the `Fast demo (no windows)` checkbox beneath and one muted line (`RUN_HINT`: 'Run uses real instruments. Demo uses virtual instruments; nothing from a demo is calibration evidence.'). Same widgets names (`btn_run` / `btn_demo`), commands, enabled rules, Enter / double-click, row menu and status wording ('Select a procedure, then press Run procedure (or Try in demo mode).'). Tooltips: Run = 'Run the selected procedure on the real instruments.', demo = the full `DEMO_EXPLANATION`. Run does what it did. The Find / Import bar follows the view strip directly; its buttons use `Compact.TButton`. Manager minimum width 1000 -> 900.
- Pinned part of the card: the run block, the status pill + info button and the two verification buttons are packed to the bottom of the card BEFORE the scrolling part, so they are never inside the scroll area and a short window never squeezes them out; the title, sub line, 'Ready to run' pill, description and file info scroll above them. The verification buttons show short texts side by side (`SHORT_VERIFY`: 'Mark verified...', 'Verify again...', 'Unlock...', 'Lock again'); the full text from `verified.ui_state` is kept in `button.full_text` (their tooltip, and the row menu). verified.py untouched. At 900x600 the pinned block fits in all three views (checked), but with the Manual / Adjustments note the scrolling part above it is only about 15 px high under Xvfb's large fixed font (the selected row still shows the name; the scrollbar reaches the rest); the table shows about 3 rows there.
- Scrolling: `ui_theme.ScrollFrame` (canvas + scrollbar; `.body` holds the content). The body is at least as tall as the panel (expanding children still fill it), the scrollbar only shows when the content is taller, the wheel scrolls it while the pointer is over it (MouseWheel on Windows / Tk 9, buttons 4 / 5 on Tk 8 X11) except over a Treeview / Text / Listbox / combobox, which keep their own wheel. One application-wide binding, handled only for a pointer over a panel; the innermost panel that can scroll wins. It re-checks its content height 4 times a second (a re-wrapped label changes the wanted height without a size event). Used for the top part of the Selected procedure card (the run block and verification buttons are pinned below it) and for the Asset History, Standards Specs, Mudcats Datasheet Importer and Datasheet Builder pages (`Manager._scroll_page`, content in `.content`). The Procedures table keeps its own scrollbar and is not inside a scroll panel; at 900x600 in the Adjustments view (note + wrapped bar) it shows about 3 rows.
- Verification text: the card shows the one-line status pill (verified.Status.pill, unchanged wording and colours) and a small info button; the full text (verified.Status.detail_lines, unchanged) opens in a small window under the button (`Manager.show_verified_details`: selectable, Esc closes it, closes when focus leaves). verified.py untouched.
- Row menu: right click (also middle button / Ctrl-click) on a Procedures row selects it, updates the card, and posts `ui.themed_menu` (built afresh each time, released with `ui.post_menu`); Shift+F10 / the Menu key posts it for the selected row. Entries (`procedure_menu.items`, Tk-free): Run procedure, Try in demo mode, Mark verified... / Unlock...
